## RQ-205: the prototype's embedding magic and version are not in the corpus *(REQ-TOOL-109, REQ-TOOL-118)*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-81 — The embedded record uses the contract names *(closes RQ-32)*": "Rewrite the embedded
    record against the contract names and the 8-D latent. Drop `jitter_frac` (the footprint fixes the offsets, R-80).
    Bump the embedding version."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_image_embedding.md` § "6. What travels": "This record layout bumps the embedding
    `version`; the byte value is set with the rest of the header (R-71)."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_image_embedding.md` § "2. Layout": "header = magic(4) ‖ version(1) ‖ flags(1) ‖
    payload_len(4) ‖ n_records(2) ‖ crc32(header)(4)", with no value for `magic` or `version`, and none for the
    prototype's.
  - `plan/requirements.yaml`, REQ-TOOL-109 (`kind: calibration`), verify: "the proposal gives the four magic bytes
    (checked not to collide with PNG signatures or the prototype's) and a version byte above the prototype's";
    REQ-TOOL-118, verify: "the header version of a newly embedded record differs from the prototype layout's version".
- **What:** TASK-M7-27 must propose a version byte above the prototype's and check its proposed magic against the
  prototype's, and its test `embed_record_version` must assert that a new record's version differs from the prototype
  layout's. No normative file gives the prototype's magic or version: the prototype itself is in `workbench/`, which
  is never a source (R-159; CLAUDE.md § Authority), and no task References names a `docs/reference/` file holding it.
  TASK-M7-27's PR proposes the magic `8F 50 72 6E` with its PNG checks done (`principia_dd_image_embedding.md` §2,
  "Magic and version"); only the comparison with the prototype, the version byte and `embed_record_version` wait.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **The human transcribes the prototype's magic and version byte into `principia_dd_image_embedding.md` §2
     (recommended)**, as the values the new layout must not reuse. TASK-M7-27 then proposes the version as the
     prototype's plus one, checks the magic against the prototype's, and adds `embed_record_version`.
  2. The human adds the prototype's file to `docs/reference/` and to TASK-M7-27's References, and the task transcribes
     the two values into §2 itself.
  3. The human rules that the prototype's records need not be told apart, and gives the version byte directly; R-81's
     "bump" and REQ-TOOL-118 would then need restating.
- **Needed:** the prototype's magic and version byte, or a ruling on how to get them. A calibration input (R-71;
  R-369 item 3). Only TASK-M7-27's REQ-TOOL-109 and REQ-TOOL-118 wait; its other requirements are built in its PR.
- **Ruling:** R-380 (4 Oct 2026): option 1. The prototype's magic `PRPX` (`50 52 50 58`) and version byte 2 are
  transcribed into `principia_dd_image_embedding.md` § "2. Layout" as the values a new record must not reuse; the
  new layout's version byte is proposed as 3 (R-71, confirmed at the M7 gate), and the proposed magic `8F 50 72 6E` is
  checked against `PRPX`. The prototype's version, 2, is the orchestrator's reading of the human's answer, flagged in
  R-380 for the M7 gate. REQ-TOOL-109 and REQ-TOOL-118 lose their `rq: RQ-205` marks.

## RQ-206: the drift views' scale before `dbg_sentinel`: diverging (debug plan §D) or log with floor (render contract Part 6) *(TASK-M3-05, REQ-TOOL-149)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_debug_tooling_plan.md` § "D. Payload field views — `SimState` scalars (ledger §3.4)":
    "**The two drift fields are signed → diverging scale, not sequential-log** (generation-root finding)", and its
    rows "`energy_drift` | derived: `H(r,p) − E_0` | **diverging**" and "`Lz_drift` | derived: `L_z(r,p) − Lz_0` |
    **diverging**".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.4 `SimState` scalars — with presentation metadata" (the note at
    line 146): "**the two drift fields are signed** → *diverging* colour scale, not sequential-log".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 6 — The debug catalogue (first build target)": "So
    `energy_drift` → log with floor, `t_end` → lin [0, T], …".
  - The layout table (TASK-M0-09, R-263) carries `Scale::Diverging` with `floor: eps_E` for `energy_drift` and
    `floor: eps_L` for `Lz_drift`, so the metadata has both a diverging scale and a floor.
  - `plan/tasks/M3/TASK-M3-05.md` § "Deliverables" and REQ-TOOL-149: the energy-drift and L_z-drift views apply the
    suspect styling on `dbg_sentinel`'s output, and a non-suspect pixel equals `dbg_sentinel`'s output (R-379).
- **What:** `dbg_sentinel` places a value on the viridis ramp at `0.5 + 0.5·x/(1 + |x|)`, with no range (render contract
  Part 5 "Presentation layer"). Fed the raw drift, which is ≪ 1 on any sane run, a drift of 10⁻⁶ to 10⁻³ lands within
  0.0005 of the ramp's middle, and the view is one flat colour. So the drift views must apply the field's scale first and
  pass the scaled value to `dbg_sentinel` (TASK-M3-05's styling definition states it). The corpus gives that scale two
  ways: §D and generation-root say diverging and expressly not sequential-log; render contract Part 6 says log with
  floor. They disagree on whether the sign is shown, and on whether the magnitude is compressed by a log.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **Signed log with floor (diverging):** `x ↦ sign(x)·ln(1 + |x|/ε)`, ε the field's floor (`eps_E`, `eps_L`), fed to
     `dbg_sentinel`. It keeps §D's sign and Part 6's log compression and floor, and reads the layout table's
     `Diverging` plus `floor` as one scale. Part 6's "log with floor" is reworded to say so.
  2. **Diverging, linear:** `x` over a symmetric range `[−R, R]`, which needs a range value (a calibration, R-71).
     Part 6's "log with floor" is corrected to "diverging".
  3. **Log with floor, unsigned:** `ln(1 + |x|/ε)`, the sign dropped, as Part 6 says; §D and generation-root are
     corrected.
- **Needed:** a ruling on which scale the drift views apply before `dbg_sentinel`. TASK-M3-05's suspect styling
  definition (REQ-TOOL-149) waits for it; nothing in TASK-M1-03 does.
- **Ruling:** R-381 (4 Oct 2026): all three, as options, with option 1 the default. The drift views offer
  colour_composition's `symlog` (signed, through the midpoint, with the field's floor), `lin` (diverging, linear) and
  `log` (unsigned, with the floor) compactions; `symlog` is the default, the preset `f_edrift` =
  `energy_drift · diverging · symlog`, and `lin` and `log` are reached through the presets' compaction override. The
  value fed to `dbg_sentinel` is the compacted value. Render contract Part 6's "log with floor" is reworded to cite the
  preset; `lin`'s default range is a new R-71 calibration requirement, closed by TASK-M3-05.

## RQ-207: the L_z-suspect threshold has no pass criterion: which occupant, on which orbit, must light it and which must not *(TASK-M3-05, REQ-INT-085)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.5 Invariant monitoring (per `STEP`, post-projection)": "suspect
    predicates (READ-TIME, over the stored drift latches — no stored suspect bits, payload §5): energy-suspect on
    relative δE ; L_z-suspect on **absolute** ΔL_z". It names the predicate and its quantity, not a criterion.
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "5. Unit tests", test 2: "KDK/Yoshida energy error *oscillates* about E₀
    with no secular trend; RK4 and Euler drift secularly; **Euler's violent drift lighting up `SUSPECT_ENERGY` is the
    pass condition**". Test 7: "a close-encounter IC shows `max|ΔE| ≫ |ΔE_final|` (spike that recovered)". Both are
    about energy only; no test names L_z-suspect.
  - `docs/contracts/principia_integrator_contract.md` § "Part 2 — Occupants and the capability profile": "On
    KDK/Yoshida, energy oscillates around `E_0` and drift measures the *physics* (close-encounter stress). On Euler/RK4
    it measures the *integrator* — Euler drifts secularly and will light up `SUSPECT_ENERGY` everywhere." Energy only.
  - `docs/contracts/principia_integrator_contract.md` § "COM projection and invariant monitoring": "accumulate the worst
    deviation so far, … and likewise $\Delta L_{z,\max}$" and "The exact accumulators and suspect predicates are in
    `principia_dd_integrator.md` §3.4–3.5". No criterion.
  - `docs/design/principia_debug_tooling_plan.md` § "D. Payload field views — `SimState` scalars (ledger §3.4)", the
    `Lz_drift` row: "**diverging** | absolute-gated suspect". It names the gate, not what it must separate.
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-INT-085 (R-71) and `plan/tasks/M3/TASK-M3-05.md` § "Acceptance tests", the
    "Proposal:" line: the thresholds are proposed "with evidence", but only the energy threshold has a pass/fail rule
    ("Euler lights SUSPECT_ENERGY and a symplectic occupant does not").
- **What:** the corpus gives the energy-suspect threshold a criterion (dd test 2: Euler lights it, the symplectic
  occupants oscillate), and says a symplectic spike on a close encounter is physics, not integrator error (integrator
  contract Part 2; dd test 7). For the L_z-suspect threshold it gives none: no text says which occupant, on which
  orbit, must light the L_z-suspect predicate, or must not. So the proposal can measure absolute ΔL_z, final and max,
  but any value it picks passes (PR #137's qa review on 55ce919, finding 1). Background, not a ruling: for pairwise
  central forces each KDK kick and drift conserves Σ rᵢ × pᵢ exactly, so KDK and the Yoshida compositions drift L_z by
  round-off only, close encounters included; an Euler step changes it by dt²·Σ vᵢ × Fᵢ, and RK4 by its truncation
  error.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **Mirror the energy rule:** on dd test 2's long bounded orbit, Euler lights the L_z-suspect predicate and no
     symplectic occupant does; RK4 and test 7 are recorded only.
  2. **Round-off bound for the symplectic occupants:** no symplectic occupant lights it on either test 2's orbit or
     test 7's close-encounter IC (their L_z drift is round-off by construction); Euler and RK4 are recorded, with no
     requirement that they light it.
  3. **Both:** 1 and 2 together, so the threshold sits above the symplectic occupants' round-off on both fixtures and
     below Euler's drift on test 2's orbit.
- **Needed:** a ruling on the L_z-suspect threshold's pass criterion, the occupants and fixtures on which it must and
  must not light. REQ-INT-085's verify detail and TASK-M3-05's "Proposal:" line give the L_z part only as far as the
  corpus goes (measured and recorded on both fixtures) until then; nothing in TASK-M1-03 waits on it.
- **Ruling:** R-382 (4 Oct 2026): option 3, both ("do 3"). The L_z-suspect threshold (REQ-INT-085) must be set so
  that, on dd test 2's long bounded orbit, Euler lights the L_z-suspect predicate, and no symplectic occupant (KDK,
  Yoshida-4, Yoshida-6) lights it on either test 2's orbit or test 7's close-encounter IC, their L_z drift being
  round-off by construction; RK4 is measured and recorded, with no requirement. REQ-INT-085 loses its `rq: RQ-207`.

## RQ-208: the drift views' ramp: the preset's `diverging` through a neutral, or `dbg_sentinel`'s viridis *(TASK-M3-05, REQ-TOOL-149)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_colour_composition.md` § "6. Debug views as presets": "`f_edrift` =
    `energy_drift · diverging · symlog`", and § "1.2 Family B — field-ramp → `vec3` or `f32`": "**Ramp** (scalar →
    colour): … `diverging(c−,c0,c+)` (through a neutral)", and "**Default ramps by field role.** Signed fields
    (energy, L_z, drifts) default to diverging-through-neutral so the zero-crossing is a legible contour".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Presentation layer (hand-written, small, reused by every debug
    view)": "Any other value … shows as its literal value on the viridis ramp at `t = 0.5 + 0.5·x/(1 + |x|)`", and
    "the view that reads the drift suspect predicates (Part 4; the energy-drift and L_z-drift views) applies the
    styling on `dbg_sentinel`'s output".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-381": "**`symlog` is the default**: the field-view preset `f_edrift` = `energy_drift ·
    diverging · symlog`", and "**The value fed to `dbg_sentinel` is the compacted value**".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-TOOL-149 and `plan/tasks/M3/TASK-M3-05.md` § "Acceptance tests",
    `drift_suspect_styling`: a non-suspect pixel equals `dbg_sentinel`'s output on the compacted value.
- **What:** R-381 settles the compaction, not the ramp. The preset colours the compacted drift with a diverging ramp
  through a neutral; `dbg_sentinel`, whose output the drift views style (R-379), draws viridis, a sequential ramp with
  no neutral at its middle. `dbg_sentinel`'s `0.5 + 0.5·x/(1 + |x|)` does put 0 at the middle and the two signs on
  either side, so the sign stays legible, but the colours differ from the preset's, and the corpus gives the drift
  views both.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **`dbg_sentinel`'s viridis:** the drift views are `dbg_sentinel(compacted value)`, as R-379 and REQ-TOOL-149
     read; colour_composition §6's `diverging` names the scale's signed midpoint, not the colour ramp.
  2. **The preset's diverging ramp:** the drift views colour the compacted value with `diverging(c−,c0,c+)`, and the
     absence hatch and the suspect styling are applied as `dbg_sentinel` applies them; REQ-TOOL-149's "equals
     `dbg_sentinel`'s output" is restated for that ramp, and the ramp's three colours are a definition (R-72).
  3. **Both, as the palette swap:** viridis by default, the diverging ramp through the presets' palette swap
     (colour_composition §6), or the reverse.
- **Needed:** a ruling on which ramp the drift views' compacted value is drawn on. TASK-M3-05's styling definition
  (REQ-TOOL-149) waits for it; nothing in TASK-M1-03 does.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (4 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator in PR #137 (TASK-M1-03), it being none of physics, a
  calibration value, a gate pass or a dropped or deferred requirement: option 3, both, through the palette swap. The
  drift views' default ramp is the preset's `diverging(c−,c0,c+)` through a neutral, as colour_composition §6's
  `f_edrift` = `energy_drift · diverging · symlog` states; viridis, `dbg_sentinel`'s ramp, is reached through the palette
  swap every preset inherits (§6). R-381's principle, in the human's words, is that the scalar views' options are
  "all data driven / customisable", with one the default; colour_composition already names the preset's ramp
  `diverging` and gives presets the palette swap; and the drift is signed (debug_tooling_plan §D, generation-root
  §3.4), which a diverging ramp shows. The drift views draw the compacted value at `dbg_sentinel`'s place on the ramp,
  with its absence hatch and literal-value rule (render contract Part 5), on the preset's ramp; under the viridis swap
  that is `dbg_sentinel`'s output, as R-381 states. REQ-TOOL-149 and TASK-M3-05 restate "equals `dbg_sentinel`'s
  output" for the preset's ramp, and the diverging ramp's three colours are part of TASK-M3-05's definition (R-72).
  REQ-TOOL-149 loses its `rq: RQ-208`.

## RQ-209: the colour-vision reference implementation, its version and its source, are not named *(REQ-COL-042, REQ-COL-045; TASK-M7-20)*
- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_colouring.md` § "3.8 Palettes and CVD": "The matrices and golden values come from a
    published reference implementation, named with its version when the task lands (R-78)."
  - `docs/design/principia_colour_composition.md` § "4.3 Display stage (terminal, outside the pipeline)": "with matrices
    and golden values from a published reference implementation, named with its version when the task lands (R-78;
    dd_colouring §3.8)."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-78 — Real Viénot and Brettel colour-vision simulation *(closes RQ-29)*": "Implement real Viénot
    (protan, deutan) and Brettel (tritan) through LMS, with golden values from a published reference implementation.
    dd_colouring §3.8's matrices are replaced."
  - `plan/tasks/M7/TASK-M7-20.md` § "Notes": "Gap: R-78 leaves the reference implementation (and version) to be named
    when the task lands; no requirement carries that choice to the human, so it is flagged in the milestone report."
- **What:** TASK-M7-20 needs the Viénot (protan, deutan) and Brettel (tritan) matrices, the LMS transform from linear
  sRGB they use, Brettel's two half-plane projections and separating plane, and the reference golden values for
  `fixtures/cvd/`. The corpus names no implementation, no version and no source to take them from, and the task file
  pins no fetchable source. The published implementations differ in the LMS basis (Smith–Pokorny, Hunt–Pointer–Estévez
  and others), in the anchor colours of Brettel's planes, and in whether Viénot's single-plane form is also used for
  tritan, so the choice changes every simulated colour and every golden value. The implementer may not choose one and
  transcribe its coefficients from memory (never guess a published coefficient), and may fetch only a source the task
  file pins. Achromatopsia (§3.8's M_achrom) and off need no source; the task's two acceptance tests both need the
  golden values, so the task can't close without them.
- **Options seen:**
  1. Name an implementation that has both methods through LMS from linear sRGB, with a released version, and pin it in
     the task file (repository URL, release tag or commit) so the implementer can fetch it read-only into scratch,
     transcribe its matrices and generate the golden values from it. DaltonLens-Python (Nicolas Burrus) is one such
     candidate: it implements Viénot 1999 and Brettel 1997 as separate simulators. Its coefficients and the version
     would then be transcribed into dd_colouring §3.8, as the task's third deliverable says.
  2. Name a different reference (another library, or the papers themselves, Viénot, Brettel & Mollon 1999 and Brettel,
     Viénot & Mollon 1997, with the LMS basis stated), pinned the same way.
- **Needed:** the name, version and pinned source of the reference implementation. Whether this is the human's (it
  changes results, but is a choice of colour reference rather than physics) or the orchestrator's per R-369 is itself
  for the orchestrator to judge; either way the source must be pinned in `plan/tasks/M7/TASK-M7-20.md` before the task
  resumes. TASK-M7-20 waits.
- **Ruling:** R-383 (6 Oct 2026): option 1, DaltonLens-Python, pinned to commit
  `3cba5e6a7c8f0e8199c8f83f1afb58eb6dab7a3d` (`master`, version 0.1.6 in its `setup.cfg`; the newest tag, `v0.1.5`,
  predates its Judd–Vos fix to the Smith–Pokorny LMS conversion). Its `Simulator_Vienot1999` (protan, deutan) and
  `Simulator_Brettel1997` (tritan) on `LMSModel_sRGB_SmithPokorny75` are the reference; the method's sources are
  Viénot, Brettel & Mollon 1999 and Brettel, Viénot & Mollon 1997; TASK-M7-20's goldens in `fixtures/cvd/` are
  generated from it, in linear sRGB. TASK-M7-20 pins the source; REQ-COL-042 and REQ-COL-045 lose their `rq: RQ-209`.

## RQ-210: `cargo xtask golden m1-coords` can render neither the `ctx` coordinate view nor picking *(plan, TASK-M1-07, REQ-TOOL-027)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-07.md` § "Acceptance tests": "`cargo xtask golden m1-coords` — green increases upward in the
    coordinate view's golden image; picking returns the expected corners (top-left click → v ≈ 1, bottom-left → v ≈ 0)
    (REQ-TOOL-027)."
  - `xtask/src/golden.rs`:31: "The shader is a WGSL fragment module, drawn over the whole target by a full-screen
    triangle the runner supplies; `constants` set its `override` declarations." The runner reads one shader file per
    case (`config.text("shader")`, :795–796) and binds only its own quantise texture (`QUANTISE_FS`, "`@group(0)
    @binding(0) var golden_float: texture_2d<f32>;`"), so a case's fragment has no `RenderContext`, no `ctx` lanes and
    none of #149's bind groups.
  - `docs/design/principia_systems_architecture.md` § "7.1 Crate map": "| `xtask` | `ledger` | `cargo xtask codegen`
    runs the generator (R-241); a normal dependency. Nothing depends on `xtask` |" and "Every other workspace edge is
    forbidden". So xtask can call neither `engine::picking` nor `render`'s assembly as code.
  - `fixtures/golden/BASELINES.md`: "A new case's row names the ruling that created it."
- **What:** the picking half of the command cannot run inside the golden runner, and the view half can run only as a
  standalone fragment that computes `v = 1 − frag_coord.y/H` itself, a second copy of the flip, which REQ-SYS-009's
  checklist ("no other Y inversion in coordinate code") then fails. The pre-flight draft said the runner already
  prepends files a case lists; it does not (one `shader` file per case), so that is new runner work.
- **Options seen:**
  1. The picking assertion is `picking_corners` (engine) alone, which REQ-GUI-001 already demands; the golden case's
     fragment takes the flip from `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/lib/coords.wgsl`, which the runner prepends by path, so
     the flip has one source.
  2. A ruling adds an `xtask → render` (and `→ engine`) edge, as R-241 did for ledger.
  3. The coordinate golden is a `render` test through #149's headless helper, outside `cargo xtask golden`.
- **Needed:** which option, and the acceptance line reworded to match.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement (tooling): option 1. Picking is checked only by `cargo test -p engine
  picking_corners`, which now names REQ-TOOL-027 beside REQ-GUI-001; `cargo xtask golden m1-coords` checks the view
  only. TASK-M1-07 extends the runner (`xtask/src/golden.rs`) with a case field naming WGSL files it prepends to the
  case's shader by repo-relative path, and the `m1-coords` case's fragment prepends `lib/coords.wgsl` and calls its
  flip. The case's row in `fixtures/golden/BASELINES.md` cites R-369 (this decision). REQ-TOOL-027 gains a note;
  TASK-M1-07's Deliverables and acceptance lines say so.

## RQ-211: "exactly one flip": picking needs a Rust flip, and #149's raster already inverts Y four times *(plan, TASK-M1-07, REQ-SYS-009, REQ-GUI-001)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-SYS-009 verify: "grep finds one framebuffer→UV flip and one export flip, both named;
    no other Y inversion in coordinate code".
  - `docs/design/principia_coordinate_conventions_note.md` § "Code paths that must honour the single convention (each
    is a separate path)": "canvas events are Y-down top-left; flip them **the same way** before computing the picked
    quad / `z`".
  - `docs/design/principia_trajectory_viewing.md` § "1. The single mechanism": "`uv  = flip_y(pointer_to_uv(event))
    // Y-up convention: v = 1 − event.y/H`" (CPU-side).
  - PR #149 (`c5a0bc1`), `crates/render/src/raster.rs`: WGSL :146 "`let up = vec2<f32>(pos.x, f32(r.target_dims.y) -
    pos.y);`", :140 "`r.target_dims.y - 1u - r.pixel.y`"; the CPU mirror `Grid::cell` :91 "`(height - 1 - y)`" and
    `tile_pixels` :111 "`let y1 = height - row * t;`". On `main`, `crates/render/src/embed/search.rs`:58
    "`Self::FlipV => (u, bottom - v)`".
- **What:** picking runs on the CPU in Rust (`crates/engine/src/picking.rs`), so it cannot call a WGSL function; the
  same flip needs a Rust implementation, a second Y inversion by the checklist's words. And #149 lands four Y
  inversions in `raster.rs` (two WGSL, two Rust) before TASK-M1-07 starts; TASK-M1-07's Deliverables do not list
  `raster.rs`.
- **Options seen:**
  1. One named flip per language, the WGSL flip in `lib/coords.wgsl` and its Rust twin, with a test holding them
     equal; #149's raster calls them; the checklist counts the named definitions.
  2. Picking calls the WGSL flip through a GPU readback (one source, but a GPU round trip for every pointer event).
- **Needed:** what "one flip" counts, and TASK-M1-07's Deliverables gaining `crates/render/src/raster.rs`.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement: option 1. There is one named flip per language: the WGSL flip in
  `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/lib/coords.wgsl` and its Rust twin in `crates/render/src/coords.rs` (`render`, which
  `engine` reaches for picking), with `cargo test -p render flip_twin_matches_wgsl` holding them equal at every row of
  a headless target. REQ-SYS-009's "no other Y inversion" means none outside those two. #149's four Y inversions in
  `crates/render/src/raster.rs` (the WGSL `raster`, `Grid::cell`, `tile_pixels`) are rewritten to call the named flip
  of their language, and `raster.rs` joins TASK-M1-07's Deliverables. `embed/search.rs`'s `FlipV` is one of the
  eight image transforms the embedding recovery searches, not coordinate code, so it is outside the checklist.
  REQ-SYS-009's verify detail is reworded to say so.

## RQ-212: the export flip: what it reverses, when the readback is already Y-down *(docs, TASK-M1-07, REQ-SYS-009)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_coordinate_conventions_note.md` § "Code paths that must honour the single convention (each
    is a separate path)", path 5: "**Exported image** — if internal is Y-up and the output format is Y-down, the encode
    step flips back **once** (the mirror of the input flip), equally single-and-named."
  - `docs/design/principia_coordinate_conventions_note.md` § "The one-line rule" (:9), which REQ-SYS-009 transcribes:
    "exactly one flip, at the framebuffer↔UV boundary (`v = 1 − frag_coord.y / H`), mirrored once at image export."
  - PR #149 `crates/render/src/headless.rs`:17: "A rendered target, read back: rows from the top, each `width` texels
    of the format's block size, unpadded."
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-07.md` § "Deliverables": "`crates/render/src/export.rs`: the single export flip (named) for
    image output."
- **Silence:** a framebuffer readback is already top-row-first, as PNG stores it, so a row reversal of it mirrors the
  image. The note's condition ("if internal is Y-up") names no internal Y-up image; nothing defines what `export.rs`
  takes as input.
- **Options seen:** (1) `export.rs` takes UV-addressed (Y-up, row 0 = bottom) data and its named flip maps it to PNG
  row order; a framebuffer readback goes out unreversed. (2) The export path takes the framebuffer readback as
  returned and writes it unreversed, the note's conditional flip not firing for it.
- **Needed:** the definition, written into the coordinate note (R-72) under a physics reviewer; TASK-M1-07 names
  none (PR #149 added physics to TASK-M1-06 for the same reason).
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement: option 2, as a definition requirement, REQ-SYS-080 (R-72). Export takes the
  headless readback as returned (rows from the top, #149's `headless.rs`) and writes the PNG's rows in that order,
  with no reversal; path 5's conditional flip does not fire for this path, since its input is not Y-up. TASK-M1-07
  writes the definition into the coordinate note's path 5, and `cargo test -p render export_orientation` checks that
  the coordinate view's exported PNG has its top-left pixel at UV (0, 1) and its bottom-left at (0, 0). Physics
  joins TASK-M1-07's reviewers for its definition requirements, as it joined TASK-M1-06's.
- **The one-line rule, read (review 5434682486):** "mirrored once at image export" is path 5's flip, stated in short;
  path 5 makes it conditional on a Y-up internal image. The headless readback is already display-oriented (rows from
  the top), so the conditional flip does not fire on this path, and the one-line rule and path 5 do not conflict.
  REQ-SYS-009's statement stands; REQ-SYS-080 states the reading, and TASK-M1-07's Goal follows it.

## RQ-213: the coordinate view's quad-local δ mode has no colour mapping, and the runner the task cites has none *(docs, TASK-M1-07, REQ-TOOL-027)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_debug_tooling_plan.md` § "F. Structural views — `RenderQuad` / quadtree (read quad
    metadata, not payload)": "raw post-flip `(u,v)` as a gradient (`u→red, v→green`), + quad-local `δ` as a second
    mode".
  - `docs/design/principia_deep_zoom.md` § "2. Linearised decoder — IC precision": "`δ` is the quad-local UV offset";
    § "1. Quad-local coordinates — UV precision": "`u = c_u + h_u · (2t − 1)`" and "`h_u = 2^−(ℓ+1)`"; so
    δ = h·(2t − 1), signed, |δ| ≤ h.
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-07.md` § "Notes": "The δ-mode colour mapping and the exact golden tolerance come from the
    golden runner's defaults (M0)."
  - `xtask/src/golden.rs`: `TOLERANCES` holds one entry, REQ-VAL-138, `max_step: 0`; the runner has no colour
    mapping.
- **Silence:** a signed offset of magnitude ≤ 2^−(ℓ+1) needs a normalisation and a signed→[0, 1] map to show as RG;
  the corpus gives neither, and the cited "runner's defaults" do not exist (only the tolerance does).
- **Options seen:** δ/h = 2t − 1 ∈ [−1, 1] mapped by ½(1 + ·) to RG; or δ in slice units on a diverging map; or
  another.
- **Needed:** the definition (R-72, physics reviewer), and the task note corrected.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement: a definition requirement, REQ-TOOL-153 (R-72). Each component of δ is
  normalised by its bound 2^−(ℓ+1), to [−1, 1], and mapped to (x + 1)/2: u's in R, v's in G, with B = 0. TASK-M1-07
  writes it into debug_tooling_plan §F's UV-passthrough row, under the physics reviewer. Flagged: in exact arithmetic
  this is t, which `uv_quad` (`ctx.quad.uv → RG`) shows, so the two agree pixel for pixel unless the δ path (c, h or
  the orientation deep_zoom §1 requires of δ) is wrong; that agreement is what the mode checks. The note's broken
  reference is fixed: the δ mapping is REQ-TOOL-153's, and the golden tolerance is REQ-VAL-138's (`max_step: 0`,
  confirmed by R-376), the runner's one tolerance.

## RQ-214: the UV preset's "c ± h·(2t−1)" at M1: no per-quad c and h, and no banding threshold *(plan, calibration, TASK-M1-07, REQ-TOOL-019)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-TOOL-019: "The UV preset (fragment-side, from the quad-local uv in ctx) must
    reconstruct quad-local (u, v) equal to c ± h·(2t−1) for sampled quads with no banding (adjacent-sample deltas
    smooth, not step-quantised)."
  - `docs/design/principia_deep_zoom.md` § "1. Quad-local coordinates — UV precision" (not in the task's References):
    "`u = c_u + h_u · (2t − 1)        t = (i + 0.5)/N   (quad-local sample coord)`"; `c` and `h` are per-quad
    uniforms the CPU computes in f64.
  - `plan/tasks/M5/TASK-M5-04.md` § "Goal": "`QuadRequest` carries the quad's centre and half-width (f64 on the CPU,
    passed as f32)", at M5.
  - PR #149 `dd_generation_root.md` § "3.7a `RenderQuad` — the CPU-written quad record": the ten members carry no
    centre or half-width; `ctx.quad` has `centre` and `tl` computed from the flat grid (`bind.rs`), and no `h`.
- **What:** (1) "quad-local (u, v)" is t ∈ [0, 1]; `c + h·(2t − 1)` is the sample's UV coordinate in the view, so
  the requirement equates the two, and writes "±" where deep_zoom writes "+". (2) At M1, c and h exist only as the
  flat grid's derived values; the per-quad uniforms arrive with TASK-M5-04. (3) "not step-quantised" has no
  threshold, and on M1's shallow flat grid f32 cannot band, so the test cannot be shown to fire.
- **Needed:** (a) the reading, and where c and h come from at M1; (b) a calibration requirement (R-71) for the banding
  criterion, with a negative fixture so it can fire.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement: the UV preset stays in TASK-M1-07; nothing is deferred. The synthetic harness
  supplies each quad's c and h, which it knows from its own grid, as part of TASK-M1-07. REQ-TOOL-019's statement
  transcribes the formula as deep_zoom § 1 writes it, `u = c_u + h_u · (2t − 1)` ("+", not "±"), and says what is
  quad-local: t is the quad-local sample coordinate, and u, v the sample's UV coordinate it reconstructs. "Not
  step-quantised" is made testable by a new R-71 calibration requirement, REQ-TOOL-152, for the banding criterion,
  proposed with its evidence by TASK-M1-07, with a negative fixture (a quad deep enough that deep_zoom §1's global
  form `u_min + (u_max − u_min)·t` bands in f32) that must fail it, and confirmed by the human at the M1 gate.
  TASK-M1-07's References gain deep_zoom §1 and §2 (RQ-224).

## RQ-216: picking's "z" at M1: no placement exists *(plan, TASK-M1-07, REQ-GUI-001)*

- **File, section:** `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-07.md` § "Deliverables": "canvas event → post-flip UV → picked quad / z";
  REQ-GUI-001 verify: "picking cross-check: click each known corner, assert the reported UV/IC coordinate is that
  corner". PR #149 `bind.rs`: `ctx.chart.z` is `ctx_uniforms.z[…]`, one value for the whole target;
  `crates/engine/src/contract/sim_config.rs`:43: `pub struct Plane {}`, empty until M2.
- **What:** a per-pixel z = z₀ + (2s − 1)q₁ + (2t − 1)q₂ needs z₀, q₁, q₂, which `SimConfig.plane` holds from M2. At M1
  picking can report UV, quad and the one z the screen is drawn at.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement: picking at M1 reports the UV, the quad and the screen's single
  `ctx.chart.z`. Per-pixel z arrives with `SimConfig.plane` in M2, through the same picking function. REQ-GUI-001
  gains a note; TASK-M1-07's Deliverables and `picking_corners` line say so.

    generated `RenderQuad` follows", typed only: "Every member is one 4-byte scalar", no scale or range. On `main`,
    `ledger::layout()` returns `payload::ledger()` only, and `QUAD_REDUCTION` (`payload.rs`:470) is a separate list;
    #149 adds `RenderQuad` as `ledger::quad::render_quad()`, separate too.

    overlay) are real files in `debug/`"; TASK-M1-13 writes `s_depth`, `s_state`, `s_impurity` by hand.

## RQ-219: a debug occupant's `slot`, and where `frag/debug/generated/` is *(plan, TASK-M1-08, REQ-GEN-009)*

- **File, section:** `docs/contracts/principia_gui_state_contract.md` § "3. The registry is the scanned filesystem —
  for the *fragment* side; compute occupants are Rust build variants": "registry entries `{id, slot, source, category,
  uniformSchema, inputDomains}` keyed by directory (slot)"; "Debug occupants satisfy the same signatures as their
  slot"; "emitted into `frag/debug/generated/`". Its tree puts generated unpack accessors in `shaders/wgsl/lib/`; the
  code has them in `crates/render/frag/generated/` (`crates/ledger/src/gen/wgsl.rs`:35, `read.rs`:52), outside
  `shaders/wgsl/`. The hand-written occupants are in `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/frag/<slot>/`
  (`crates/render/src/assemble.rs`:52), and TASK-M1-13 puts hand-written debug occupants in
  `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/frag/debug/`. TASK-M1-08 § "Deliverables": "the directory scan of `shaders/wgsl/frag/**`
  (and `frag/debug/generated/`)".
- **Silence:** a file under `debug/` has no slot directory, so `slot` must come from elsewhere; and the generated
  views' path is either inside `shaders/wgsl/frag/` or beside the current `crates/render/frag/generated/`.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (6 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator, it being none of physics, a calibration value, a gate pass
  or a dropped or deferred requirement: the generated field views go in `crates/render/frag/debug/generated/`, beside
  the generated unpack layer and read side in `crates/render/frag/generated/`, so every generated WGSL file is under
  `crates/render/frag/` and every hand-written one under `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/`. The scan covers
  `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/frag/**` and `crates/render/frag/debug/generated/`, and tags everything under either
  `debug/` `category: debug`. A debug occupant's `slot` is the slot function its file defines, exactly one of
  `colour`, `brightness`, `combine` and `post` (gui_state §3's signatures); a generated field view defines `colour`,
  so its slot is `colour`. TASK-M1-08's Goal, Deliverables and Notes say so.

## RQ-223: θ̃'s initial value and its behaviour near the poles are not defined *(physics, TASK-M1-11, REQ-INT-001)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.7 The shape readout and winding (live, per macro-step — lockstep,
    ratified)": "**Unwrapped phase** `θ̃`: the equatorial longitude `atan2(n_v-axis, n_u-axis)` accumulated
    continuously — per step, add the principal-value delta (∈ (−π, π]) so no 2π jumps enter; `orbit_count =
    ⌊|θ̃|/2π⌋` and `retrograde = sign(θ̃) < 0` are **derived at read from the running accumulator**".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` § "1. `SimState` — the hot struct": "`theta  : f32,                 //
    unwrapped phase     → orbit_count, retrograde`"; § "5. Derived — computed at read, NOT stored": "| `orbit_count`
    | `⌊|theta| / 2π⌋` | winding count |" and "| `retrograde` | `theta < 0` | winding sense |". Neither says how
    `theta` starts.
- **Silence:** (1) whether θ̃(0) = 0 (winding since the start) or atan2 of n(0) (an absolute longitude). It changes
  `orbit_count` and `retrograde`: starting at longitude 3 rad, one full retrograde turn gives θ̃ = −2π (count 1) from 0,
  or 3 − 2π ≈ −3.28 (count 0) from atan2. (2) At the poles (`n_u = n_v = 0`, w = ±1) atan2 is undefined, so a step
  passing near a pole adds an arbitrary delta; nothing says whether to hold θ̃ there.
- **Needed:** the definition, which changes results (physics, R-369), written into dd_integrator §3.7 and built by
  TASK-M1-11 under its physics reviewer.
- **Ruling:** R-389 (6 Oct 2026): (1) θ̃(0) = 0, the net turning from the IC; (2) θ̃ holds below a radius with a frozen
  reference: on entering `√(n_u² + n_v²) < r_pole` the last longitude is stored, no delta is added inside, and on exit
  `wrap(exit − stored)` into (−π, π] is added; a difference of exactly ±π adds +π. `r_pole` is a new R-71 calibration,
  REQ-INT-086, confirmed at the M1 gate and closed by TASK-M1-11. dd_integrator §3.7 gains the rule; REQ-INT-001 and
  TASK-M1-11 gain the test obligation for a pole passage and the exact-π case. An IC starting inside the radius is
  not reached by the ruling and is filed as RQ-225.

## RQ-225: an IC that starts inside θ̃'s pole radius has no stored longitude for its first exit *(physics, R-389, TASK-M1-11, REQ-INT-001)*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-389", the human's words: "Hold below a radius, with a frozen reference: on entering
    √(n_u²+n_v²) < r_pole, store the last longitude; add no delta while inside; on exit, add wrap(exit longitude −
    stored longitude) into (−π, π]."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-389", Q1's answer: "θ̃ counts net turning from the IC: orbit_count is completed revolutions,
    retrograde is net clockwise motion, independent of starting longitude."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.7 The shape readout and winding (live, per macro-step — lockstep,
    ratified)", the paragraph R-389 adds: "An IC that starts inside the radius has no stored longitude; that case is
    open (RQ-225)."
- **Silence:** the reference is stored "on entering" the radius. An IC with `√(n_u² + n_v²) < r_pole` at `t = 0`
  never enters it, so on its first exit there is no stored longitude to subtract. Whether that exit adds nothing, or a
  difference from `n(0)`'s own longitude, changes θ̃ by up to π, and with it `retrograde` and, near a whole turn,
  `orbit_count`.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **The first exit adds nothing** (recommended): θ̃ counts turning from the first longitude the hold trusts, the
     exit longitude. It keeps the hold's premise that a longitude inside the radius is not trusted.
  2. **The stored longitude is `n(0)`'s**, `atan2(n_v, n_u)` at the IC (taken as 0 when `n_u = n_v = 0` exactly):
     the IC counts as the entry point, so the first exit adds `wrap(exit − longitude(n(0)))`, reading Q1's "net
     turning from the IC" literally.
- **Needed:** the human's choice: it changes `retrograde` and `orbit_count` for such ICs (physics, R-369). REQ-INT-001
  carries `rq: RQ-225`. Only this case waits: TASK-M1-11 builds and tests every other part of R-389, and its
  `theta_unwrap` adds the start-inside case once this is ruled.
- **Ruling:** R-392 (6 Oct 2026): option 1, "Nothing": the first exit of an IC that starts inside the radius adds no
  delta, so θ̃ is still 0 there and counts from the exit longitude; a longitude inside `r_pole` is never trusted, the
  IC's included. dd_integrator §3.7 gains the case; REQ-INT-001 and TASK-M1-11 gain the test obligation (θ̃ unchanged
  at the first exit) and REQ-INT-001 drops `rq: RQ-225`.

## RQ-226: θ̃'s frozen reference and its has-reference flag have no storage across a resumed march *(physics, ledger, R-389, R-392, TASK-M3-11, REQ-INT-082)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.7 The shape readout and winding (live, per macro-step — lockstep,
    ratified)", R-389's paragraph: "on entering `√(n_u² + n_v²) < r_pole`, the last longitude (the last one outside
    the radius) is stored; no delta is added while inside; on exit, `wrap(exit longitude − stored longitude)` is
    added", and R-392's: "An IC that starts inside the radius has no stored longitude, and **its first exit adds no
    delta**".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` § 2, on resumption: "on a **cached-state resume** (a quad
    continuing its march from cached `SimState` at `t_cached`, which lockstep does)".
  - The same file, § 1 and § 2, on the free space: `_reserved : u16, // free under alignment (4 B and 8 B cost the
    same); do not spend`, and descriptor bits 10–15: "Reserved, decode as zero, never opportunistically reused".
    `SimStateFTLE` is "144 B actual / 144 B effective".
- **The silence:** the corpus says the longitude is "stored" but gives it no slot. The hold needs two values that
  persist between macro-steps, the frozen longitude and a has-reference flag (set outside the radius, clear for an IC
  that starts inside). The previous shape point can be recomputed from the stored positions; these two cannot. Kept
  only in kernel locals, they are lost at a cached-state resume, so θ̃ would depend on how the march was split into
  dispatches. Every place to keep them is protected by the payload document, so any choice is a ledger change.
- **Options** (from the physics review, which prefers (a)):
  - **(a)** A new f32 `theta_ref`, plus the flag as a versioned assignment of descriptor bit 10. `SimStateFTLE` goes
    from 144 B to 152 B (+8 B per sample with padding). Implements R-389 exactly.
  - **(b)** The reference as a u16 longitude in `_reserved` (steps of about 9.6e-5 rad), plus the bit-10 flag. No
    size change, but the exit delta and the exact-π case become quantised.
  - **(c)** Rebuild the reference from θ̃ and the longitude θ̃ counts from. It still needs storage for R-392's
    IC-inside case, and it changes R-389's arithmetic.
  - **(d)** Drop the hold at dispatch boundaries. Physics rejects it: θ̃ would depend on the dispatch split.
- **Needed:** a ruling on where the two values live, before TASK-M3-11 (REQ-INT-082) runs θ̃ in the kernel's march.
  TASK-M1-11 computes θ̃ in a single call and is not blocked.
- **Ruling:** R-397 (7 Oct 2026): option (b) without its bit-10 flag: the frozen reference goes in `_reserved` as a
  u16 in steps of `2π/65535`, with 0xFFFF for "no reference" (R-392's IC that starts inside), and no descriptor bit is
  used; "inside the hold" is recomputed from the current state on resume, not stored. `SimState`'s size is unchanged
  and the schema version changes with the ledger (R-36). R-389 and R-392 stand; the quantisation, at most ~5e-5 rad
  per pole passage, is documented in payload §2 and dd_integrator §3.7. The precondition held: the corpus names no
  future use for `_reserved`, only the alignment argument, which the port rechecks. TASK-M3-11 builds it under its
  physics reviewer, with REQ-INT-087 to REQ-INT-089.

physics, a calibration value, a gate pass or a dropped or deferred requirement; the corpus settles each.*

## RQ-227: the stain's `Ctx` has no `ic`, so the `ICDescriptor` views have nothing to read *(plan, TASK-M1-08, REQ-TOOL-020)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 6 — The debug catalogue (first build target)", the
    catalogue row: "`ICDescriptor` — all 12: masses as ternary, `q_mass`, `rho_mag`, `lambda_mag`, `rho_ratio`,
    `rho_angle` (cyclic), `K_0`, `V_0`, `virial_ratio`, `r_min_pair_0` | `ctx.ic` | decoder + canonicaliser per
    sample, field by field".
  - The same contract's opening definitions: "**`RenderContext`**: `{sample: SimState, ic: ICDescriptor, quad:
    RenderQuad, uv, screen_uv, time}`", and "The decode stage emits `ICDescriptor` per sample; … The fragment shader
    binds all of it."
  - The code: the stain's `Ctx` is `{sample, frag_xy, inputs}`, and the runtime pipeline binds only the masses, as a
    uniform.
- **The gap:** the contract gives the fragment `ctx.ic`; the code doesn't have it yet. This is not a silence in the
  corpus.
- **Options raised:** (a) add `ic` to `Ctx` and bind `ICDescriptor[]`, loading only the members a view reads (R-378);
  (b) make the IC views harness-only; (c) leave them to a later task.
- **Decided:** (a), as the contract says. TASK-M1-08 adds `ic` to the stain's `Ctx` and binds `ICDescriptor[]` per
  sample, loading per member (R-378), and generates the twelve views on `ctx.ic`. (b) contradicts Part 6's "`ctx.ic`";
  (c) would be a deferral, which only the human makes.

## RQ-228: the shadow `r_sh`/`p_sh` in the catalogue, against Part 3a's "never resurrected state" *(plan, TASK-M1-08, REQ-TOOL-020, RQ-218)*

- **File, section:**
  - RQ-218 (above): every entry of `ledger::layout().entries` is in the catalogue, the shadow included.
  - `docs/contracts/principia_lowering_contract.md` § "Part 3a — The uniform read-side interface (tier features
    degrade by NaN, not by struct shape)": "The expensive *state* is still baked out exactly as Part 3 says — the
    Benettin **shadow trajectory** (48 B, marched every step) and the **word buffer binding** are absent in the
    feature-off variant, preserving the occupancy/memory win; … What Part 3a keeps uniform is only the cheap
    *read-side result* — a computed expression or a naming convention, never resurrected state." Its item 1: "The
    read-side `SimState` type is fixed across all variants … What varies per variant is not the *type* but what
    *fills* each field", with a feature-off column that returns NaN.
- **The question:** whether a view of the shadow reads state that Part 3a forbids resurrecting.
- **Options raised:** (a) the read side gains `r_sh`/`p_sh`, read from the stored shadow at the FTLE tier and NaN at
  the base tier; (b) exclude the shadow, amending RQ-218's reading; (c) the shadow's view shows a derived read.
- **Decided:** (a). At the FTLE tier the shadow is stored, so reading it resurrects nothing; at the base tier it is
  absent, and the field reads NaN, as Part 3a's heading says ("tier features degrade by NaN, not by struct shape")
  and as its table does for `sample.ftle`. The read-side type stays fixed across variants, and RQ-218's reading
  stands. The read goes through generated accessors loading per member (R-343, R-378).

## RQ-229: `cargo xtask golden` can bind no payload, so `m1-numeric`, `m1-outcome`, `debug-views` and `m1-structural` cannot render their views; #160's `prepend` does not change that *(plan, tooling, TASK-M1-09, TASK-M1-10, TASK-M1-12, TASK-M1-13, TASK-M1-14)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-09.md` § "Acceptance tests": "`cargo xtask golden m1-numeric` — NaN-absent ftle renders
    hatched …"; `TASK-M1-10.md`: "`cargo xtask golden m1-outcome` — one sample per class renders exactly the §1.4 sRGB
    values"; `TASK-M1-12.md`: "`cargo xtask golden debug-views` — each view in the debug picker renders on a synthetic
    payload"; `TASK-M1-13.md`: "`cargo xtask golden m1-structural` — each structural view renders from a synthetic
    RenderQuad set". TASK-M1-14's `m1-subfields` and `m1-synthetic` are the same shape.
  - `xtask/src/golden.rs`:31 (main): "The shader is a WGSL fragment module, drawn over the whole target by a
    full-screen triangle the runner supplies; `constants` set its `override` declarations." The runner's only bind
    group is its own quantise texture (`golden.rs`:926–971).
  - PR #160, `xtask/src/golden.rs`:33–35: "`prepend`, optional, names WGSL files by their path relative to the
    workspace root … the runner places their text, in order, before the shader's". It concatenates static text and
    binds nothing.
  - PR #160, `crates/render/src/bind.rs`:77: "`pub const FRAME_BINDING: u32 = 1;`", and `preset_module` and `upload`,
    which bind the four payload buffers, the context uniforms and the per-quad frames. That is `render`'s harness,
    which `engine::presets` renders through, not the golden runner.
  - `docs/design/principia_systems_architecture.md` § "7.1 Crate map": "| `xtask` | `ledger` | `cargo xtask codegen`
    runs the generator (R-241) …" and "Every other workspace edge is forbidden". The same section: "`cargo xtask
    screenshot` reaches `gui`'s windows by spawning `gui`'s headless capture mode as a separate process (R-274), as
    `gate` spawns validation's binary; that is not a crate edge." `validation` "may depend on any crate but `gui` and
    `prin`".
- **What:** each of these views reads `ctx.sample`, `ctx.ic` or `ctx.quad` from storage buffers that the stain's
  harness binds. The golden runner binds none of them, and it cannot call `render` or `engine`. The known issue for
  TASK-M1-13 is confirmed, not resolved. #160's `prepend` adds static library text, which is enough for `m1-coords`
  because that fragment computes everything from `frag_coord`. The per-quad frame binding is in the render harness.
  Even there, #160's `CtxQuad` carries only `uv`, `centre` and `half_width`, not `RenderQuad`'s metadata (RQ-238). The
  same gap blocks every payload-bound golden suite in M1, not only TASK-M1-13's.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **A harness case kind, rendered by a spawned process.** `case.json` names a scene. `cargo xtask golden` spawns a
     `validation` binary, which may depend on `render` and `engine`. That binary renders the named synthetic scene
     through `render::bind::preset_module` and `upload` and writes the `Rgba32Float` image. The runner then quantises
     it in its own pass and compares it as today: REQ-VAL-138, `BASELINES.md` and `repro` are all unchanged. There is
     no crate edge, by the precedent of `gate` and `screenshot`.
  2. **Golden tests in `render` and `engine`, through the headless helper.** This needs a second implementation of
     quantise, compare and BASELINES outside xtask.
  3. **A ruling adding `xtask → render, engine` edges.** This changes R-170/R-185's confirmed crate map, so it needs
     the human.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator (tooling): option 1. `cargo xtask golden` gains a harness
  case kind: the case's `case.json` names a synthetic scene, the runner spawns a `validation` binary that renders that
  scene through `render::bind::preset_module` and `upload` and writes the `Rgba32Float` image, and the runner
  quantises and compares it in its own pass as today, so REQ-VAL-138, `BASELINES.md` and `repro` are unchanged and no
  crate edge is added. The first of TASK-M1-09 and TASK-M1-13 to start builds the case kind and the binary, and its
  Deliverables name them; the other, and TASK-M1-10, TASK-M1-12 and TASK-M1-14, reuse it. Each new case's
  `BASELINES.md` row cites R-369 and this entry (and R-153 for `debug-views`) and is confirmed at the M1 gate, as R-376
  confirmed M0's rows. The acceptance lines keep their `cargo xtask golden <suite>` commands.
  *Amended per code review 5438179638 (PR #163; R-369, 7 Oct 2026, by the orchestrator):* neither TASK-M1-09 nor
  TASK-M1-13 depended on the other, so "the first to start builds it" could build the case kind twice or leave
  TASK-M1-13 waiting on an unmerged task it does not depend on. TASK-M1-09 builds the case kind and its `validation`
  binary, with no condition; TASK-M1-13 gains TASK-M1-09 in its Depends on and uses the case kind TASK-M1-09 builds.
  R-388's early start applies to TASK-M1-13 once TASK-M1-09's head is approved, if it is then its one unmerged
  dependency.

## RQ-230: TASK-M1-09 still builds the diffusion −1.0 sentinel that R-245 removed, and REQ-TOOL-023 and REQ-GEN-012 still put `d_min`'s sentinel on the ramp against R-280 *(plan, TASK-M1-09, REQ-GEN-012, REQ-TOOL-023, REQ-TOOL-012, REQ-TOOL-137)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-09.md` § "Goal": "the diffusion −1.0 survives pack/unpack bit-exact and renders as −1.0 on
    the ramp, distinct from NaN"; § "Acceptance tests": "`cargo test -p render diffusion_sentinel` — −1.0 round-trips
    bit-exact; the catalogue render of −1.0 shows the literal value −1.0 on the ramp (R-136) (REQ-GEN-012)" and
    "`cargo xtask golden m1-numeric` — NaN-absent ftle renders hatched and diffusion = −1 as its literal ramp value";
    § "Notes": "PIT-8: NaN absence and the −1 sentinel must stay distinct … −1 its literal value on the ramp".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-245 — An invalid diffusion fit reads NaN, by a validity predicate; no −1.0 sentinel *(amends
    R-17, R-136; closes RQ-152)*": "R-136's rule stands for any stored sentinel; `diffusion` is no longer one, and an
    invalid fit gets the hatch as NaN."
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-GEN-012 verify: "… catalogue render of a stored sentinel shows its literal value
    (R-136); a diffusion with n < 2 renders the hatch (R-245)". REQ-TOOL-012 verify: "synthetic payload with
    NaN-absent ftle and a diffusion with n < 2 renders both hatched (R-245)".
  - REQ-TOOL-023 statement: "sentinels (including diffusion's −1) must be bit-exact and shown as their literal values
    on the ramp, never scaled (R-136)". R-245's list of reworded requirements does not name REQ-TOOL-023.
  - `decisions.md` § "R-280 — An unset `d_min` renders in the neutral "not yet" grey *(closes RQ-170)*": "it doesn't
    place +inf on the ramp. The other sentinels still show as their literal values on the ramp (R-136)."
  - `crates/ledger/src/payload.rs`:145: `latch("d_min", …).sentinel(f64::INFINITY)`. The ledger's sentinels are
    `dmin_pair` = 3 (:141), `d_min` = +∞ (:145) and the word `length` = 127 (:194). None is `diffusion`'s.
  - REQ-TOOL-137 verify: "a forced-failure sample and an unstepped sample draw d_min in the running-sample grey; a NaN
    in the same view is hatched; a valid d_min sits on the ramp". TASK-M1-09's line names only the forced-failure
    sample.
  - `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/lib/present.wgsl`:81: `fn dbg_sentinel(x: f32, frag_xy: vec2<f32>)` already exists
    (TASK-M1-03). TASK-M1-09 § "Deliverables" lists it as new work.
- **What:** the task file predates R-245, whose rewording reached the requirements but not the task. REQ-TOOL-023 kept
  the −1. REQ-TOOL-023 ("sentinels … on the ramp") and REQ-GEN-012 ("a stored sentinel shows its literal value") both
  cover `d_min`'s +∞, which R-280 sends to the grey.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator (mechanical consequences of R-245 and R-280):
  TASK-M1-09's Goal, PIT-8 note and acceptance lines are reworded. `diffusion_sentinel` becomes
  `diffusion_invalid_fit`: a diffusion with n < 2 reads NaN and is hatched, distinct from a valid slope, and
  `m1-numeric` hatches a diffusion with n < 2. The task's sentinel tests use the ledger's stored sentinels, `dmin_pair`
  3 and `length` 127, and the failed-state 0.0 of `dE_max` and `dLz_max`. Through reqio, REQ-TOOL-023's statement drops
  "(including diffusion's −1)" and excepts `d_min`'s unset value (R-280), and REQ-GEN-012's verify detail gains
  "except `d_min`'s unset +∞, drawn in the grey (R-280)". The task's REQ-TOOL-137 line gains the unstepped sample and
  the valid-`d_min` leg. The `dbg_sentinel` deliverable becomes "uses TASK-M1-03's `dbg_sentinel`".

## RQ-231: the two-line template meets the ledger's scale metadata: log, cyclic and diverging fields, unbounded fixed ranges, which `ramp`, the drift views, and `RANGE_AUTO` as a uniform *(plan, TASK-M1-09, REQ-RENDER-022)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "10.1 The shared prelude library": "Each numeric debug field therefore
    generates a two-line `colour()` — the NaN guard … then `ramp( range_norm(raw, lo, hi, RANGE_AUTO, u_range) )`",
    with "**`auto = false`** uses the **fixed `[lo,hi]`** — a per-field domain **declared in the manifest** … (… a `u16`
    step index `[0, horizon_steps]`; an `f16` latch's range; `d_min ∈ [0, ~2]`)".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 6 — The debug catalogue (first build target)": "per-field
    presentation metadata: `{scale: lin|log|cyclic, range, sentinel?, categorical_n?, tier_gate?}`. So the drift
    fields `energy_drift` and `Lz_drift` default to `diverging · symlog` with the field's floor … `rho_angle` →
    cyclic".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-381 — The drift views offer `symlog`, `lin` and `log`, with `symlog` the default; the value fed
    to `dbg_sentinel` is the compacted value *(closes RQ-206)*": the drift views' three compactions, "built by
    TASK-M3-05". The human's words there: "Yeah I'm pretty sure this sort of thing is in the spec for all of the debug
    shaders / shaders that display a scalar value / range".
  - `crates/ledger/src/payload.rs`:7: "Where §3 gives a field no scale or range, the entry is `lin` over (−∞, ∞)". PR
    #161's views give `C_ty`, `E_0` and `theta` the range "(−∞, ∞)", and `d_min`, `dE_max` and `dLz_max` the scale
    "log".
  - `crates/render/src/assemble.rs`:186–191: a `@uniform` is `f32`, `i32`, `u32` or `vec2/3/4<f32>`; there is no bool.
    `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 3 — Cache tiers and the recompute rule": "a param edit
    recolours without a recompile".
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-03.md` § "Notes": "`meas` for `auto = true` comes from `QuadReduction` in production (M5);
    at M1 the test supplies it as a uniform from a CPU min/max over the synthetic buffer."
- **Silences:**
  1. The template ramps `raw` linearly. Nothing says how a `log`, `cyclic` or `diverging` field passes through it.
  2. The fixed `[lo, hi]` is undefined for a field the ledger leaves unbounded.
  3. Which `ramp` the template uses is not stated.
  4. Whether the drift views take the template or R-381's `symlog` default.
  5. "Toggling `RANGE_AUTO` on the node param updates the generated code" conflicts with a uniform param, which
     changes no code.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator (not physics, not a value):
  1. `raw` is the field's compacted value per its ledger scale: `lin` is the identity; `log` is the field's floor
     form, as `dbg_log` places it; `cyclic` is `fract(x/period)` on the fixed range [0, 1] with `ramp_twilight`;
     `diverging` uses a symmetric range.
  2. A field with an unbounded ledger range defaults to `RANGE_AUTO = 1`; its fixed mode is that range's finite bound
     where one exists. `t_end_step` and `t_dmin_step` use `[0, horizon_steps]`, per §10.1.
  3. `ramp` is `ramp_viridis`, `dbg_sentinel`'s ramp.
  4. `energy_drift` and `Lz_drift` keep R-381's default, so TASK-M1-09 leaves them TASK-M1-08's view until TASK-M3-05.
  5. `RANGE_AUTO` is a `@uniform RANGE_AUTO: u32 = <0|1> [0, 1]` in the view's header. "Updates the code" means the
     header default the generator writes from the node's param, and "parsing back" means `Declaration::parse` of that
     header. `u_range` is a `vec2<f32>` uniform filled from the CPU min/max (TASK-M1-03's note).
  TASK-M1-09's Goal, Deliverables and Notes say so, and REQ-RENDER-022 gains a note.

---

## RQ-232: REQ-COL-001 at M1: `FieldRamp`, `Compaction` and `ScalarField` are M7's, and the M1 golden asks one view to hatch a sentinel and show it literally *(plan, TASK-M1-09, REQ-COL-001, TASK-M7-05, TASK-M7-09)*

- **File, section:**
  - REQ-COL-001 (M1): "Every ScalarField must return (value, valid) and every Ramp and Compaction must carry an
    explicit invalid-pixel colour/value, defaulting to the invalid pattern REQ-COL-055 sets … to which every colouring
    must map NaN or a sentinel — except debug fields, which must show literal stored values". Verify: "a field with
    invalid pixels (NaN and sentinel) renders them in the invalid pattern; overriding the node's invalid colour changes
    only those pixels; a debug field view shows a failed-state 0.0 as the literal 0.0".
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-09.md` § "Deliverables": "`crates/render`: `FieldRamp` / `Compaction` invalid-colour lane
    (default the REQ-COL-055 pattern, per-node override)". § "Acceptance tests": "`cargo xtask golden m1-numeric` —
    invalid pixels (NaN and sentinel) render the invalid pattern … a debug field view shows a failed-state 0.0 as
    literal 0.0".
  - `plan/tasks/M7/TASK-M7-05.md` § "Deliverables": "`crates/render/src/colour/field_ramp.rs` — the
    `FieldRamp{field, ramp | compaction}` node … its invalid colour/value (REQ-COL-001)". `TASK-M7-09.md` § "Goal":
    "FieldRamp's ScalarField sources, each returning (value, valid) through `ctx`".
  - No `FieldRamp`, `Compaction` or `ScalarField` exists in `crates/render/src` at `40ff11f`.
- **What:** REQ-COL-001 is in M1's gate, but the types it governs are built in M7. Every M1 colouring of a numeric
  field is a debug view. Debug views show sentinels literally (R-79, R-136), so the "sentinel → invalid pattern" leg
  has no M1 subject. As written, the acceptance line asks the same views to do both.
- **Options seen:**
  1. **TASK-M1-09 builds the minimal types.** `FieldRamp` over one `ScalarField` (a payload field with its validity
     predicate, `(value, valid)`), a `lin` `Compaction`, viridis, and the invalid lane with its per-node override. One
     non-debug field ramp in `m1-numeric`, `d_min`'s, maps NaN and the sentinel to the pattern; the debug view of the
     same field shows the stored value literally. TASK-M7-05 and TASK-M7-09 extend the same files; their Deliverables
     say "extend".
  2. **Split REQ-COL-001.** The M1 part covers the debug views' invalid lane, and the FieldRamp, Compaction and
     ScalarField part moves to M7. This defers part of an M1 requirement, which only the human does (R-369).
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator: option 1, so nothing is deferred. TASK-M1-09 builds the
  minimal `FieldRamp` over one `ScalarField` (a payload field with its validity predicate, returning `(value, valid)`),
  a `lin` `Compaction`, viridis, and the invalid lane with its per-node override, in
  `crates/render/src/colour/field_ramp.rs`, which its Deliverables name. Its REQ-COL-001 line separates the two legs:
  one non-debug field ramp in `m1-numeric`, `d_min`'s, maps NaN and the sentinel to the invalid pattern, and the debug
  view of the same field shows the stored value literally. TASK-M7-05's and TASK-M7-09's Deliverables gain "extends
  TASK-M1-09's".
  *Amended per code review 5438179638 (PR #163; R-369, 7 Oct 2026, by the orchestrator):* R-280, in the human's
  words, draws an unset `d_min` in the neutral "not yet" grey, "Not the invalid hatch", in every colouring (RQ-170's
  option 1, the invalid pattern, was not chosen), so `d_min`'s unset value cannot carry the sentinel leg. The
  "sentinel → invalid pattern" leg moves to a minimal non-debug `FieldRamp` over the word `length`, whose stored
  sentinel 127 maps to the invalid pattern while the `length` debug view shows 127 literally (R-136). `d_min`'s field
  ramp keeps only the NaN → invalid pattern leg and draws its unset value in R-280's grey, as REQ-TOOL-023 now says.
  The invalid-colour override is checked on both ramps.

## RQ-233: the neutral "not yet" grey is needed by TASK-M1-09 (R-280) but proposed by TASK-M1-10, which depends on TASK-M1-09 *(plan, calibration ordering, TASK-M1-09, TASK-M1-10, REQ-COL-053, REQ-TOOL-137)*

    bits and drawn in the neutral "not yet" grey of running samples … (REQ-TOOL-012, REQ-TOOL-137)".

- **What:** TASK-M1-09 records a golden image in the grey before any task has proposed the grey's value. It would

## RQ-234: triple collision and triple ejection (`detail = 3`) have no class in §1.4's nine-class palette *(docs, calibration, TASK-M1-10, REQ-COL-002)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` § "`sample_descriptor` (low 16 bits of `packed_a`) — 10 used, rest
    reserved", `detail` row: "escape → body id (0–2), `3` = **triple ejection**; collision → pair id (0–2), `3` =
    **triple collision** — one rule, *3 means all three* (pending change 7)". REQ-EVT-006: "Triple ejection
    (ionisation) must be reported as `escape` with `detail = 3`". `decisions.md` § "R-30 — Event precedence is by time
    *(IE-2)*": "Triple collision and triple ejection are placed explicitly."
  - `docs/design/principia_colour_composition.md` § "1.4 Categorical colour-assignment — the outcome-state default
    palette": the table has collision pairs 2/1/0, escape bodies 0/1/2, bounded, collision @ t=0 and degenerate. "The
    two states the table has no row for: `running` shows neutral grey, and `sim_failed` shows the invalid pattern
    (§3)." Neither triple outcome is named.
- **Silence:** a collision or an escape with `detail = 3` is a valid outcome (not `sim_failed`), and the canonical
  palette gives it no colour. TASK-M1-10's golden ("one sample per class") cannot cover it, and the occupant must still
  draw something.
- **Options seen:**
  1. Two new swatches, an R-71 calibration requirement. TASK-M1-10 proposes them with evidence (OKLab separation from
     the nine classes, the grey and the hatch). The human confirms them at the M1 gate. The R/G/B and Y/M/C mnemonic
     suggests a neutral-family choice per event family, but the proposal decides.
  2. Reuse an existing class colour, for example triple collision as collision @ t=0's orange. This conflates two
     outcomes.
  3. The invalid pattern. Rejected: these are valid outcomes, and the hatch is NaN's (PIT-8).
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator: option 1. No colour value is chosen here; the values are
  the human's at the gate. A new R-71 calibration requirement (REQ-COL-062, through reqio) gives triple collision and
  triple ejection a swatch each, proposed with its evidence by TASK-M1-10 (OKLab separation from the nine classes, the
  running grey and the hatch), checked by a reviewer and confirmed by the human at the M1 gate. colour_composition §1.4
  gains the two classes, without values, as a port that adds, marked proposed. REQ-COL-002's verify and TASK-M1-10's
  `m1-outcome` gain a triple-collision and a triple-ejection sample, and REQ-COL-055's no-collision measurement
  includes the two swatches.

## RQ-235: `n` is not in the ledger's code, so REQ-VAL-010's "generated" vector occupants have nothing to generate from; the task's note about it is stale; and "every vector field" includes `r` and `p` *(plan, TASK-M1-12, REQ-VAL-010, REQ-TOOL-010)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-12.md` § "Notes": "the ledger metadata schema (generation root §3.8) has no vector type, so
    "the ledger knows n is a vector" (gui_state_contract §4) has nothing to key on." § "Deliverables": "WGSL `shape_n`
    / Hamiltonian helpers in the prelude library with Rust twins from the kernel."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.8 Metadata schema (what every entry must carry)" (REQ-GEN-024,
    TASK-M0-07): "**`vector(type, k)`** is the type of a vector-valued field … `n` is `vector(f32, 3)`; the stored `r`
    and `p` are each `vector(f32, 6)`" and its worked row "| `n` | `derived(from: [r, m0, m1, m2])` | the Montgomery
    map, never stored … | vector(f32, 3) | lin, per component | [−1, 1] per component | — |".
  - `crates/ledger/src/payload.rs`:150–170: derived entries `ftle`, `energy_drift`, `Lz_drift`, `diffusion`, and no
    `n`. PR #161's `PENDING` list (`crates/ledger/src/gen/catalogue.rs`:46) does not name `n` either.
  - `crates/render/frag/generated/read_side.wgsl`:119 `fn hamiltonian(…)` and :134 `fn energy_drift(…)`, already
    generated (TASK-M1-01). `crates/kernel/src/shape.rs`:43 `pub fn shape(…)` (TASK-M1-11).
  - REQ-VAL-010: "For every vector field the generated debug occupants must offer '‖·‖ as scalar' beside 'as
    direction-cosines'". PR #161's `r.wgsl` and `p.wgsl` show `‖·‖` only.
- **What:**
  - The doc has `n`'s entry and the vector type; the code has the type but not the entry.
  - The Hamiltonian helper already exists in the read side.
  - A hand-written prelude `shape_n` would sit outside the generated read side that holds every other derived field.
  - "Direction cosines" as RGB fits k = 3 only, but `r` and `p` are k = 6.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator: TASK-M1-12 adds `n` to `ledger::payload` as §3.8's worked
  entry (`derived`, `vector(f32, 3)`). The read side generates its WGSL from the same source as `kernel::shape`, with
  a twin test, as it does `hamiltonian`. The catalogue (TASK-M1-08's generator) offers both reductions, '‖·‖ as
  scalar' and 'as direction-cosines', for `vector(f32, 3)`, and `‖·‖` alone for k ≠ 3. The `‖n‖ = 1` test
  (REQ-VAL-122) is on `n` only, the one unit vector. The Hamiltonian deliverable is dropped, since it exists. The stale
  note is replaced by a citation of §3.8, and `catalogue_equals_ledger` then covers `n`. REQ-VAL-010 gains a note on
  k ≠ 3.
  *Amended per code review 5438179638 (PR #163; R-369, 7 Oct 2026, by the orchestrator):* "`‖·‖` alone for k ≠ 3" is
  withdrawn: it would narrow REQ-VAL-010 and generation-root §3.8 ("the catalogue offers its reductions (`‖·‖` as a
  scalar beside direction cosines …)" for every `vector(type, k)`), which only the human does (R-369). Every vector
  field offers both. Direction cosines of a k-vector are well defined (vᵢ/‖v‖); what the corpus lacks is their colour
  rendering for k ≠ 3 (`r` and `p`, `vector(f32, 6)`), which becomes an R-72 definition requirement (REQ-TOOL-157,
  through reqio) that TASK-M1-12 writes under its physics reviewer. REQ-VAL-010's note says so.

## RQ-236: three renderings TASK-M1-12 needs and the corpus does not give: "masses as ternary colour", the whole-word hash of a `uint4`, and "invalid-styled" for a `u32` *(docs, definition, TASK-M1-12, REQ-TOOL-010, REQ-TOOL-025)*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Live-state & array inspection": "**ICDescriptor views**: masses as
    ternary colour"; § "Field views (one per field, every struct)": "Word views: `fgw_reduced_length` (invalid-styled
    when truncated) … **whole-word hash** (`dbg_hash` of the uint4)".
  - The same contract's § "Presentation layer (hand-written, small, reused by every debug view)": "`dbg_hash_u32(v)`:
    the PCG hash …", which takes one `u32`; `present.wgsl`:57 already has `fn dbg_pcg(v: u32) -> u32`.
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-12.md` § "Notes": "`dbg_hash_u32`'s hash is not specified (TASK-M1-03's gap)". This is
    stale: TASK-M1-03 specified PCG (above).
  - `decisions.md` § "R-136 …": "only NaN gets the hatch"; § "R-245 …": an invalid derived value "gets the hatch as
    NaN".
  - "Ternary" appears nowhere else as a colour definition (`grep`: render contract :243, :261 and debug plan, all
    without a mapping). PR #161 (`8a29799`) generates no masses view yet: `m0`, `m1` and `m2` are in its `PENDING`.
- **Silence:**
  1. How `(m0, m1, m2)` becomes a colour.
  2. How the word's four limbs fold into the one `u32` that `dbg_hash_u32` takes.
  3. Whether "invalid-styled" means the hatch, which R-136 reserves for NaN, or another style.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator: each is an R-72 definition requirement, added through
  reqio (REQ-TOOL-154 to REQ-TOOL-156), which TASK-M1-12 writes under its physics reviewer:
  1. Ternary: `(m0, m1, m2)`, which sum to 1, as linear RGB scaled by `1/max(mᵢ)`, so that the equal-mass point is
     white and each vertex a primary. It is written into render contract Part 5. If RQ-227's application in #161 has
     TASK-M1-08 generate a combined masses view, TASK-M1-12 uses that view's rendering instead.
  2. Word hash: `dbg_pcg` chained over the limbs, `h = pcg(w ^ pcg(z ^ pcg(y ^ pcg(x))))`, then `dbg_hash_u32`'s
     byte-to-RGB step. It is written beside `dbg_hash_u32`.
  3. "Invalid-styled": the reduced length draws `debug_invalid(frag_xy)` when `fgw_reduced_length_valid` is false,
     read as a failed validity predicate, R-245's precedent, not a stored sentinel. The raw `length` view still shows
     127 literally (R-136).
  The stale note is dropped.

## RQ-237: TASK-M1-12's acceptance shape: the suite's name, references "recorded at the M1 gate", the picker's scope, and "equal" for fragment-computed derived views *(plan, TASK-M1-12, REQ-TOOL-010, REQ-TOOL-024)*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-12.md` § "Deliverables": "Golden/screenshot fixtures `fixtures/golden/m1-derived/`"; §
    "Acceptance tests": "`cargo xtask golden debug-views` — each view in the debug picker renders on a synthetic payload
    and matches its own golden image, recorded at the M1 gate (R-153)"; "`cargo test -p render
    derived_views_match_cpu` — … each computed in the fragment, equal a CPU reference".
  - `xtask/src/golden.rs`:45 (main): "Each reference is refused unless `fixtures/golden/BASELINES.md` holds its hash
    with the decision that set it" (R-110: no re-baselining without a gate decision).
  - `crates/kernel/tests/derived.rs`:159–183: TASK-M1-01's bounds for the same derived fields, from WGSL's stated
    accuracy ("`log` within an absolute `2⁻²¹` … 3 ULP").
- **What:**
  - The suite name (`debug-views`) and the fixture directory (`m1-derived`) differ.
  - Before the gate the runner refuses a case with no BASELINES row, so the line cannot pass in the PR as worded.
  - There is no debug picker at M1, and its view set grows with TASK-M1-10 and TASK-M1-13, which TASK-M1-12 does not
    depend on.
  - "Equal" between f32 GPU `log` or `/` and a CPU reference needs a bound.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator: the suite is `fixtures/golden/debug-views/`. TASK-M1-12
  records each reference in its PR, with a BASELINES row citing R-153 as "proposed, confirmed at the M1 gate", as M0's
  rows were confirmed by R-376. "The debug picker" is the registry's `category: debug` entries at the task's merge;
  TASK-M1-10 and TASK-M1-13 add their own cases to the same suite. `derived_views_match_cpu` compares integers and
  booleans exactly, with synthetic θ̃ kept away from multiples of 2π so `floor` is stable; `ftle` and the drift are
  compared within the derived WGSL-accuracy bound of `crates/kernel/tests/derived.rs`, which is not a calibration. The
  rendering runs through RQ-229's harness.

## RQ-238: the stain's `Ctx` after #160 has `quad { uv, centre, half_width }` only: no `RenderQuad` metadata and no tile lane for TASK-M1-13's views and tile boundaries *(plan, TASK-M1-13, REQ-TOOL-026, REQ-RENDER-024)*

- **File, section:**
  - PR #160, `crates/render/src/assemble.rs`:938–953, the stain's context: "`struct CtxQuad { uv: vec2<f32>, centre:
    vec2<f32>, half_width: vec2<f32>, }`" (at `c34ddb7`, `c` and `h`) and "`struct Ctx { sample: SimState, frag_xy:
    vec2<f32>, screen: CtxScreen, quad: CtxQuad, inputs: … }`". PR #161 moves that text to
    `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/stain/context.wgsl`, and RQ-227 adds `ic`. The two PRs conflict there.
  - `crates/render/src/bind.rs` (main), the harness's own lanes: `QUAD_LANE` (:154, "depth" ← `quad_depth`, "state" ←
    `quad_state`, …) and the tile lane with `uv` (:310, `r.tile_uv`). These are in `RenderContext`, which the stain
    does not receive.
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-13.md` § "Deliverables": "`s_depth`, `s_state`, `s_impurity`, … as `ctx.quad` presets"; §
    "Notes": "`ctx.tile.uv` (render_gui_spec §12.1) is not in colour_composition §3's lane list (milestone Gaps; carried

    `ctx.quad.uv` and `ctx.tile.uv` (R-72; REQ-COL-056).**", written by TASK-M1-06.

  `.priority` and `.cache_age`, and the tile-boundary overlay reads `ctx.tile.uv`. The stain's `Ctx` has none of

  extension. `CtxQuad` gains colour_composition §3's quad members, filled in `preset_module` from `quad_read(r.quad)`
  by `QUAD_LANE`'s mapping, and a `CtxTile { uv }` lane is filled from `r.tile_uv`; `shade_sample` fills both with
  the absence NaN, as #160 does. The note is replaced by a citation of colour_composition §3's REQ-COL-056 paragraph.
  The orchestrator resolves #160/#161's `CONTEXT` conflict when the second of them merges, before TASK-M1-13 starts.
  TASK-M1-13 cannot start early (R-388 item 3 as applied): two of its dependencies are unmerged.

## RQ-239: TASK-M1-13 builds the Tier-3 tile debug path, which is TASK-M5-29's (REQ-RENDER-055) with its record TASK-M8-08's (REQ-PERF-079); and "present iff contains-ensemble" has no flag in `RenderQuad` *(plan, TASK-M1-13, REQ-TOOL-026)*

## RQ-240: TASK-M1-13's reviewers and acceptance shape: a definition with no physics reviewer, a depth test the M1 raster cannot exercise, a save/load check in a golden run, and a second hatch *(plan, TASK-M1-13, REQ-TOOL-124, REQ-RENDER-024)*

    tint written into debug_tooling_plan §F and approved by the physics reviewer (REQ-TOOL-124)". REQ-TOOL-124:

    the golden images; the overlay persists across save / load of the graph (REQ-RENDER-024)".

    covers the target exactly", so every quad is drawn at `N × tile_px` pixels and a quad's depth is metadata only.
    PR #160's per-quad frames carry `h = 2^−(ℓ+1)` (deep_zoom §1).
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "10.1 The shared prelude library": `debug_invalid` "draws the hatch from
    the pixel position (R-136), a pattern that collides with no palette entry", and only NaN gets it (PIT-8, R-136).
- **What:**
  1. A definition requirement needs the physics reviewer, and the task does not name one. RQ-212 added physics to
     TASK-M1-07 for the same reason.
  2. On M1's uniform raster, depth 3 and depth 20 quads are drawn the same size, so equal widths prove nothing about
     `fwidth`.
  3. Graph persistence is not a pixel comparison.
  4. A "pending hatch" beside the invalid hatch could be read as NaN.
- **Decided:** per R-369 (7 Oct 2026), by the orchestrator:
  1. physics joins TASK-M1-13's reviewers.
  2. The width test draws the depth-3 and depth-20 sets, with their deep_zoom frames, at two raster quad sizes (for
     example 16 and 64 px). The line's measured width in px is equal across all four, and a thresholded-uv control
     (`u < 0.01`) must fail it.
  3. Persistence becomes `cargo test -p engine boundary_overlay_roundtrip`: serialise the graph, load it, and get an
     equal graph and an equal render key.
  4. REQ-TOOL-124's definition must differ from `debug_invalid` in pattern and in colours, and REQ-COL-055's
     measurement is rerun with the tint and the hatch.
