## RQ-266: what `class_histogram` does when a setting's footprint count per quad, N² × (E+1), exceeds its bin width, is not given *(silence, physics, R-398, R-137, R-132, TASK-M5-01, REQ-PAY-075, REQ-PAY-077, REQ-PAY-089)*

*Found in physics review 5480220637 of PR #185 (the R-388 pre-flight of TASK-M5-01). Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "Outcome (all at joint `class ⊕ detail` grain)": "`class_histogram[N]`
    | u8 × N | derives dominant *and* impurity from **one** source, so they cannot disagree", and "`outcome_impurity` |
    f16 | `1 − max(class fraction)`".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "Ensemble spread — the two bounded contributors": "**Uniformity
    invariant — every footprint carries `E+1` copies at all times; no copy is ever removed from the sample.**"
  - `docs/design/principia_memory_tiers.md` § "4. The six quality tiers": "**Ultra and Extreme cap `N` at 16** (`N² ≤
    256`, the one-workgroup-per-quad invocation ceiling)", Extreme's `E` = 15 "*(provisional, R-137)*", and Medium's
    `N` = 16 with `E` = 1.
  - `docs/design/principia_memory_tiers.md` § "5. Controller levers, ranked by impact": "**Custom mode** exposes
    `render_scale` (0.25–2.0; …), `N`, `MAX_REL_DEPTH`, E, and FTLE directly (arbiter off)", and "**Custom's `N` is a
    power of two (R-398)**, each within the one-workgroup-per-quad thread ceiling (`N² ≤`
    `maxComputeInvocationsPerWorkgroup`, REQ-PERF-011)", so `N` = 32 on a 1024-invocation adapter, and `E` has no bound.
  - `docs/design/principia_memory_tiers.md` § "7. The "are you sure?" safety system (three severities, none blocking)":
    "**Red does not prevent** — … the user has final say".
  - REQ-PAY-075 (TASK-M5-01's definition): "the bin width cannot overflow at the largest footprint count per quad".
- **Silence:** a bin `w` bits wide holds at most `2^w − 1`. §3.7's u8 overflows already at Medium (16² × 2 = 512); at
  Extreme the count is 16² × 16 = 4096; in Custom, with `E` unbounded and `N` up to 32, no fixed width covers every
  setting. The corpus does not say what happens past the width. A bin that wraps silently corrupts `dominant_outcome`
  and `outcome_impurity`, which feed the split decision, so the choice changes results.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) A fixed bin width chosen so that no setting that can allocate reaches it (e.g. u32 bins: at `N` = 32,
    `E + 1` would have to exceed 2^32/1024 ≈ 4.2 M copies), with the bound stated and checked.
  - (b) The bin width is chosen from the setting (`⌈log₂(N²(E+1) + 1)⌉`, rounded to a packable width), so the
    `QuadReduction` layout follows the sim key's `N` and `E`.
  - (c) A setting with `N²(E+1) > 2^w − 1` is refused or clamped (Custom's `E` capped, and the internal rungs kept
    under it). Against it, §7's "none blocking" for Custom; for it, the corpus's precedent of refusing a configuration
    that exceeds a stored format: `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.1 `sample_descriptor` (u32)",
    `t_end_step`'s row, "**Dispatch refuses a configuration with `horizon_steps=⌈T/dt⌉ > 65535`** (R-86; single format,
    no Q0.16 fallback; long integrations use coarser dt/epochs)".
  - (d) Bins saturate at `2^w − 1` and the quad is flagged (its `outcome_impurity` marked untrustworthy), so a
    saturated quad is never read as a clean one.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing.
- **Waits:** all of TASK-M5-01, not single lines (`plan/WORKFLOW.md` § "Escalation": "The task stays open, blocked,
  until the human rules"), and through it its dependent TASK-M5-17, and every task downstream of it in
  `plan/tasks.yaml`. Three of its requirements carry `rq: RQ-266`: REQ-PAY-075 (the definition's overflow clause, and
  `quad_reduction_histogram_capacity` at Extreme's 4096 and at the boundary the ruling sets), and, because under (b)
  the layout follows the setting, REQ-PAY-077 and REQ-PAY-089 (member order, packing, aligned size). TASK-M5-01 is
  also held by the M1 gate (and the gates before M5).
- **Ruling:** R-410 (10 Oct 2026): option (a), u32 bins. §3.7 states the bound, `N² × (E+1) ≤ 2³² − 1` (overflow
  needs more than about 4.2 M copies per footprint at `N` = 32, which can't be allocated), and dispatch asserts it
  (REQ-SCHED-098, TASK-M5-17). No settings are capped. Perf confirms the size cost to `QuadReduction` on TASK-M5-01.

## RQ-267: what "over- and under-samples relative to uniform" measures, the density of samples or the link's Jacobian weight, is read two ways *(conflict, docs wording; context: TASK-M2-01, PR #187)*

*Found in physics review 5480715305 of PR #187 (TASK-M2-01), finding F6. Wording only: no decode, encode or hash reads a
sampling note (dd_generation_root §3.9, "The hash"). Nothing is chosen, and the σ row is left as it is.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` § "Integrity: the link is part of the experiment": "a registry
    entry carries — beyond forward, inverse, and log-det — a note on **which region of the block it over- and
    under-samples relative to uniform**". Read as the density of samples drawn with uniform controls, a link
    over-samples where its Jacobian is small: σ crowds every control past a few units into a sliver at each bound.
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.9 The link registry (consolidated from chart contract Part 2.5)",
    the table's bounded row: "`log((b−a)·σ')` | centre-heavy vs uniform". σ's slope, and so its Jacobian weight, is
    largest at the centre; its sample density there is the smallest, so "centre-heavy" reads as the Jacobian's weight,
    not as the density of samples.
- **Conflict:** the two texts read "relative to uniform" one way each. The notes TASK-M2-01 adds (`stick_breaking`,
  `softsign_*`) say they use the density-of-samples reading, citing chart_decoder_contract.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Notes describe the density of samples drawn with uniform controls; §3.9's σ note is reworded to match (for
    example "edge-heavy: crowds the bounds").
  - (b) Notes describe the Jacobian's weight; the new notes are reworded to match the σ row.
  - (c) Each note names its reading.
- **Ruling:** R-413 (10 Oct 2026): option (a), density. Sampling notes describe the density of samples drawn with
  uniform controls; §3.9's σ note is reworded to "edge-heavy: crowds the bounds", in PR #187 (TASK-M2-01), which
  carries this entry and edits §3.9. Archived here from #187's head `8b449829` by R-413's rulings PR
  (`plan/OPERATIONS.md` § "Merging"); #187 deletes its open copy.

## RQ-268: three more f16 `QuadReduction` members have no declared range, and no calibration requirement names them *(silence, missing value; context: TASK-M5-01)*

*Found by TASK-M5-01's implementer. The task's pre-flight found five f16 members with no range and made them
REQ-PAY-093 (R-71); three more have none either. TASK-M5-01 proposes their ranges as it does the five's, marked
proposed, so the ledger generates; nothing waits on this but their confirmation.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.8 Metadata schema (what every entry must carry)": "**A field
    without a complete entry fails generation loudly.**" and "`f16` (R-312) … follows R-248's rules as `f16-pair`
    does: exactly 16 bits, a declared range within ±65504, and an unbounded end allowed only when the entry states
    `overflow`."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "Validity and diagnostics": "| `escape_time_min` / `_max` | f16 × 2
    | diagnostic range |" and "| `mean_orbit_count_spread` | f16 | tiebreaker |", with no range, unit or bound.
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PAY-093 names only "`error_ratio`, `roundtrip_error`, `alpha_area`, `alpha_energy`
    and `worst_energy_drift`".
- **Missing value:** the declared `range` of `escape_time_min`, `escape_time_max` and `mean_orbit_count_spread`, and
  their `overflow` where an end is unbounded (R-248). An escape time is a time within the horizon `[0, T]` (§3.4's
  `t_end`), but `T` is a sim-key parameter with no corpus bound; an orbit-count spread is ≥ 0 with no upper bound.
- **Missing value, the no-escape case (added at physics's review of TASK-M5-01's PR, F2):** `escape_time_min` and
  `escape_time_max` have no value when no copy of the quad escaped. §3.7 gives them neither a value nor a sentinel for
  that case, and 0 or any finite time would read as an escape that happened.
- **Proposed (TASK-M5-01's PR, as R-71 proposes a value):** each `[0, ∞)` with `overflow: saturate`. The lower ends
  follow from the definitions (a time, a spread). Saturate rather than inf: a diagnostic range or a tiebreaker past
  65504 reads "at least 65504", a finite value, where +∞ would read as a time never reached.
- **The no-escape case, options (corrected at physics's review of TASK-M5-01's PR, F3):** the minimum of the empty
  set of escape times is +∞, but its maximum is −∞, so no one value is both reductions' identity. Either option is
  tested by its bits, as `d_min`'s unset value is (R-271) and `first_divergence_t`'s is (§3.7), and saturate keeps every
  escape time finite, so the sentinel is distinct from every time recorded. Either sentinel lies outside a finite
  escape time; the human chooses it with the ranges, and until then §3.7 holds it open and the ledger entries carry
  none.
  - (d) **+∞ for both** (binary16 `0x7c00`, f32 bits `0x7f80_0000` once widened). For `_min` it is the min
    reduction's identity. For `_max` it is a chosen sentinel, not the empty max, so the writer (TASK-M5-17 to M5-19)
    needs a no-escape branch that stores +∞ when no copy escaped. One test, `== +∞`, reads "no escape" on either
    member.
  - (e) **Each its reduction's identity**: +∞ for `_min` and **−∞** for `_max` (binary16 `0xfc00`, f32 bits
    `0xff80_0000`). Both reductions start from their identity and need no branch, and an empty range reads
    `_min > _max`. `_max`'s −∞ lies below its proposed range `[0, ∞)`, as a sentinel may.
- **Options seen for the ranges' confirmation:**
  - (a) REQ-PAY-093 names the three as well (reqio), and the human confirms the proposal with the five at the M5 gate.
  - (b) A calibration requirement of their own, confirmed at the M5 gate.
  - (c) A ruling gives their ranges now.
- **Ruling:** R-418 (11 Oct 2026): options (a) and (e). The three ranges join REQ-PAY-093, confirmed at the M5
  gate with the five (proposed `[0, ∞)` with `overflow: saturate`). With no escape, `escape_time_min` = +∞ (`0x7c00`)
  and `escape_time_max` = −∞ (`0xfc00`), each its reduction's identity, with no branch; "no escape" reads
  `_min > _max`. Archived here from #191's head `fe682029` by R-418's rulings PR (`plan/OPERATIONS.md` § "Merging");
  #191 deletes its open copy.

---

## RQ-269: Custom's `N` has an upper bound but no lower bound, and the memory models (memory_tiers §3, telemetry §6.2a) have no per-quad `QuadReduction` term, which at Custom `N` = 1 or 2 is the size of `SimState` per render pixel *(silence, R-398, R-410, REQ-PERF-023, REQ-PERF-025, REQ-PERF-035; context: TASK-M5-01, PR #191)*

*Found in perf review 5481822520 of PR #191 (TASK-M5-01), as an observation "outside this task's deliverables; not a
finding against this diff". u8 bins (80 B) would leave the same gap, so R-410 does not create it. Widened in PR #193's
perf review 5481953808 (F1, F2) and physics review 5481954814 (finding 4). Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_memory_tiers.md` § "3. Memory model — payload + render targets": "`payload_mem    = (E+1) ×
    bytes × render_px` (the SimState samples — one per render pixel at the floor)" and "`total          ≈
    payload_mem + render_targets + fixed            (fixed = bake texture, staging)`", with `bytes` = "144/96".
  - `docs/design/principia_memory_tiers.md` § "4. The six quality tiers": "`N~`/`depth~` are indicative — the tier
    sets the sample grid and refinement budget", the table's `N~` of 8 (Potato, Low) and 16 (Medium to Extreme), and
    "**Ultra and Extreme cap `N` at 16** (`N² ≤ 256`, the one-workgroup-per-quad invocation ceiling)".
  - `docs/design/principia_memory_tiers.md` § "5. Controller levers, ranked by impact": "**Custom mode** exposes
    `render_scale` (0.25–2.0; <1 performance, >1 supersampling), `N`, `MAX_REL_DEPTH`, E, and FTLE directly (arbiter
    off)", and "**Custom's `N` is a power of two (R-398)**, each within the one-workgroup-per-quad thread ceiling (`N² ≤`
    `maxComputeInvocationsPerWorkgroup`, REQ-PERF-011); it offers no other value."
  - `docs/contracts/principia_scheduler_contract.md` § "Part 6 — The settled policy": "`SAMPLES_PER_QUAD_AXIS` (`N` —
    the sample grid inside a quad; each sample is a full simulation, so this is *the* quality/memory/compute driver —
    too low misclassifies a quad as coherent by undersampling itself) … **Both are tier-gated with a Custom
    override.** Per-tier values in the memory-tiers table (`N` ranges 8–32 across the six tiers; rel-depth budget
    likewise per tier)."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_telemetry_and_tiers.md` § "6.2a Budgeting before allocating": "The payload
    size is known in advance: `quads x N^2 x (E+1) x sizeof(SimState)`. So:" and "**Budget before allocating.** Query
    the adapter's reported limits, compute the requirement, and if it does not fit, **reduce the tier and report it**
    rather than attempting the allocation."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.7 `QuadReduction` — completed ledger": "There is no memory
    pressure: `QuadReduction` is per *quad*, against `SimState` at per *sample* (millions). The quads to cost it at are
    memory_tiers §3–§4's: render pixels / N² at each tier's `N`, at the largest display, plus their ancestors." It
    gives no size on main: "TASK-M5-01 defines it from the member list, aligns it and measures it, and writes the
    measured figure here".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PERF-086: "Ultra and Extreme must cap N at 16 (replacing memory_tiers §4's 24 and
    32, which exceed the one-workgroup-per-quad ceiling of 256 invocations)".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PERF-035 (Custom's bounds): "N must be a power of two at every tier and in the Custom
    override, which offers no other value (R-398)", verified as "Custom offers powers of two only, each within
    REQ-PERF-011's thread ceiling (R-398)"; REQ-PERF-011's check "asserts N² ≤ maxComputeInvocationsPerWorkgroup for
    every tier and Custom value".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PERF-025 (the estimator): "The memory estimate must be payload_mem = (E+1) × bytes ×
    render_px plus render_targets = k_d × display_px × 4 + k_r × render_px × 4 (k_d ≈ 3, k_r ≈ 3) plus fixed".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PERF-023 (the pre-allocation fit; TASK-M5-11): "Before allocating, the requirement
    quads × N² × (E+1) × sizeof(SimState) must be checked against the adapter's reported limits, and if it does not
    fit the tier must be reduced and reported instead of attempting the allocation".
- **Silence:** Custom's `N` is bounded above (`N² ≤ maxComputeInvocationsPerWorkgroup`) but not below: the "8–32" of
  the scheduler contract covers only the named tiers, so Custom offers `N` = 1 and 2 as powers of two. Neither memory
  model has a per-quad term: memory_tiers §3's estimate and telemetry §6.2a's pre-allocation fit (the check that
  decides whether a Custom setting allocates) both count only `SimState`. §3.7's "no memory pressure" is costed at
  "each tier's `N`" only. `QuadReduction` is 176 B as TASK-M5-01 sums and measures it (PR #191, unmerged; perf review
  5481822520; u32 bins per R-410), 80 B with u8 bins; main does not state a size yet. Per render pixel, leaves plus
  ancestors (× 4/3): about 3.7 B at `N` = 8, but about 59 B at `N` = 2 and 235 B at `N` = 1, the order of
  `SimState`'s 96–144 B, and neither model would count them. The corpus says neither whether `N` = 1 or 2 is a valid
  Custom setting nor whether the memory models count the reductions.
- **A further consequence (correctness, not memory):** the scheduler contract's Part 6 says too low an `N`
  "misclassifies a quad as coherent by undersampling itself". At `N` = 1 a quad has one sample, so a coherence test
  that compares samples has nothing to compare: every quad reads as coherent and refinement never triggers. At `N` = 2
  the test rests on 4 samples. Option (b) alone costs `N` = 1 or 2 correctly but still permits such a setting.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) A lower bound on Custom's `N` (memory_tiers §5, REQ-PERF-035), at which the reductions stay negligible
    against `SimState`, and at which a quad has enough samples for the coherence test (the further consequence above).
  - (b) A per-quad reduction term (`≈ size_of(QuadReduction) × quads`, ancestors included) in both memory models:
    memory_tiers §3 and REQ-PERF-025's estimator, and telemetry §6.2a and REQ-PERF-023's pre-allocation fit, so every
    `N` is costed and the two models agree.
  - (c) Both.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing.
- **Waits:** REQ-PERF-035 (Custom's `N` values; TASK-M6-15), REQ-PERF-025 (the memory estimate; TASK-M5-10) and
  REQ-PERF-023 (the pre-allocation fit; TASK-M5-11, also held through its dependency on TASK-M5-10) carry
  `rq: RQ-269`, so those three tasks wait, and through TASK-M6-15 the Custom `N` field (REQ-GUI-014, TASK-M6-22). All
  three tasks are unmerged and also held by the M1 gate. TASK-M5-01 (PR #191) does not wait: perf judged this not a
  finding against it, and none of its requirements carries the mark.
- **Ruling:** R-422 (11 Oct 2026): option (c). A lower bound on Custom's `N`, a correctness floor (`N` = 1 never
  refines), proposed with evidence at the M5 gate (REQ-PERF-095, TASK-M5-02); both memory models count the per-quad
  `QuadReduction` for every `N` (memory_tiers §3, telemetry §6.2a; REQ-PERF-025, REQ-PERF-023).

## RQ-270: a saturated configuration control decodes onto or past the poles: at f32 to `α = fl₃₂(π/2)` and `β = fl₃₂(π)`, just past their bounds, and at f32 and f64 to `α = 0` or `β = 0`, for the σ, tanh and softsign links alike, where dd_decoder §3.2 says finite `z` never reaches the poles *(conflict, R-21, R-82, REQ-DEC-018, REQ-DEC-027, REQ-DEC-028, REQ-CHART-034, REQ-VAL-020; context: TASK-M2-02, PR #192)*

*Found by TASK-M2-02's implementer, PR #192, § "Observations for the physics reviewer (not changed here)". Widened in
PR #193's physics review 5481954814 (findings 1–3): the canonical no-op, f64 and the tanh links; and in physics review
5481968627 (F1, the saturated-`z_α` geometry) and code review 5481971521 (F1, the softsign links). Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "3.2 Configuration — hyperspherical mass-weighted Jacobi": "`α = (π/2) ·
    σ(z_α)          β = π · σ(z_β)`", and "Finite `z` never reaches the exact poles (`α = (π/2)σ(z_α) ∈ (0, π/2)`
    strictly); a config ingested *at* a pole is caught by those flags, and exact-pole `atan2(0,0)=0` yields a
    deterministic canonical value rather than a NaN."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "3.3 Canonicalise": "rotate all rᵢ, pᵢ by R(−φ),  φ = atan2(ρ_y, ρ_x)",
    "mirror (full state: every rᵢ AND every pᵢ, y → −y)  iff λ̃_y < −δ_λ   (λ̃ = √μ_λ·λ, δ_λ = 10⁻¹²)", "|λ̃_y| ≤ δ_λ
    → no mirror   (the one mirror test, in encode's frame — R-82)", and "With the canonical-frame decode both are
    no-ops away from the seam — and **that no-op property is a test** (5.5)."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "5. Unit tests", test 3: "**Range respect:** `α ∈ (0, π/2)`, `β ∈ [0, π]`,
    `|qₖ| ≤ q_max`, for all finite z including ±∞-ish saturation."; test 5: "**Canonical no-op:** for canonical-frame
    decodes, applying §3.3 changes nothing (rotation angle ≈ 0, mirror not taken) away from the `λ̃_y = 0` seam; at
    the seam, the deadband makes the choice deterministic (same result twice)."; test 12 runs the decode as "the
    CPU-f64 and GPU-f32 instantiations".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-DEC-027 (TASK-M2-03): "For all finite z, including saturation, the decode must
    respect its ranges α ∈ (0, π/2), β ∈ [0, π], |qₖ| ≤ q_max", and TASK-M2-03's acceptance line "`cargo test -p
    kernel decode_ranges` — decoder test 3 over fuzzed z including ±large values: α ∈ (0, π/2), β ∈ [0, π], |qₖ| ≤
    q_max; masses stay in the open simplex (REQ-DEC-027)."
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-DEC-018 (TASK-M2-07, `canonical_noop`): "for canonical-frame decodes it must be a
    no-op (rotation angle ≈ 0, mirror not taken) away from the λ̃_y = 0 seam", verified by "decoder test 5: apply C
    to canonical-frame decodes of fuzzed z, assert unchanged". REQ-DEC-002 (TASK-M2-07), named alongside it:
    "Canonicalise must be the single function that quotients a physical state onto the section (CoM at origin, ρ̃ on
    +x, λ̃_y ≥ −δ_λ under the R-82 mirror tie, I = 1), used by both the decode path and encode."
  - `docs/notes/principia_validation_ground_truth_note.md` § "Degenerate configs: open & unguarded, approach as a
    limit": "the parametrisation stays **open `[0,1]`, endpoints excluded** (no endpoint-clamping — no landing
    machinery), and there are **no artificial guards** against approaching degenerate configs. … (sigmoid/softmax
    approach the boundary as `z → ±∞`, so `[0,1]` gets arbitrarily close but never lands)". REQ-DEC-028 (TASK-M2-02):
    "The chart parametrisation must stay open on `[0,1]` with endpoints excluded, with no endpoint clamping and no
    artificial guards against approaching degenerate configurations."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.9 The link registry (consolidated from chart contract Part
    2.5)": "| Bounded (a,b) | scaled/shifted `σ` | …"; the entries table's rows "| `sigmoid_alpha`, `sigmoid_beta`,
    `sigmoid_q` | α, β, q | `a + (b−a)·σ(x)` | …" and "| `tanh_alpha`, `tanh_beta`, `tanh_q` | α, β, q | `a + c·(1 +
    tanh x)` | …"; and § "5. Tests (properties any generator must satisfy)", test 8: "(a) constraint preservation ∀
    inputs incl. saturation (simplex outputs sum to 1 and stay positive; bounded outputs in range)". REQ-CHART-034
    (TASK-M2-01, merged): "constraint preservation for all inputs incl. saturation". TASK-M2-01's
    `link_properties_a_constraint` (`crates/kernel/tests/links.rs`) checks bounded outputs in the closed `[lo, hi]` at
    the f32-rounded bounds, "which a saturated control reaches exactly, as `σ(x)` rounds to 1".
  - `crates/kernel/src/generated/links.rs` (main): `const LIT_6: f32 = core::f32::consts::FRAC_PI_2;` and `const
    LIT_7: f32 = core::f32::consts::PI;`, both rounded up from π/2 and π (by 4.4×10⁻⁸ and 8.7×10⁻⁸; the f64 constants
    round down); `fn sigmoid` as `R::one() / (R::one() + Float::exp(-x))`; and `TanhAlpha::forward` and
    `TanhBeta::forward`, which read the same `LIT_6` (`(π/2)·½(1 + tanh x)` and `(π/2)(1 + tanh x)`); and
    `SoftsignAlpha::forward` and `SoftsignBeta::forward`, which read the same `LIT_6` and cap `|x|` at `LIT_4`, which is
    `f32::INFINITY` / `f64::INFINITY`, so the cap does nothing for finite `x` and `x/(2 + |x|)` rounds to ±1.
  - The same generated file's `sigmoid_q`, `tanh_q` and `softsign_q`, onto dd_generation_root §3.9's "each free
    momentum `q ∈ (−q_max, q_max)` (dd_decoder §3.4)", reach `±q_max` exactly at saturation, by the same rounding;
    `q_max` = 2 is exact, so they never overshoot. REQ-DEC-027's "|qₖ| ≤ q_max" is closed and admits that; §3.9's open
    codomain and test 8 (a)'s "in range" read as open do not. The mass links (`softmax_tanh`, `stick_breaking`) keep
    every mass positive (`μ_max·tanh` and the `(1−ε_μ)` factor), and the half-line links (`softplus`, `exp`), which
    reach 0 on underflow, serve none of the blocks ("the two lines, which no block uses").
  - *Added from TASK-M2-03's read-only pre-flight:* `docs/design/principia_dd_validation_orbits.md` § "1.3 Lagrange
    and Euler central configurations — the only analytic ones": "**Euler (collinear) is at `beta → 0` and `beta →
    pi`, the top and bottom edges.**" `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-VAL-020 (TASK-M2-03,
    `chart_landmarks_lagrange_euler`): "A chart-landmark test must assert the decoder places the Lagrange equilateral
    configuration at `z = 0` and the Euler collinear configurations at `beta → 0` and `beta → pi`", verified by
    "decode z = 0 -> equilateral; decode near beta edges -> collinear (approached as a limit)".
- **Conflict:**
  - *Upper poles, f32 only.* At f32, `σ` rounds to 1 for `z ≳ 17` and `tanh` for `z ≳ 9`. For the softsign links,
    `1 + x/(2 + |x|)` rounds up to 2: over most of the band `x/(2 + |x|)` rounds to `1 − 2⁻²⁴`, not to 1, and
    `1 + (1 − 2⁻²⁴)` is a tie that rounds to even, 2. Scanning every f32 (physics review 5481985015, re-checked),
    this first happens at the single point `x = 2²⁴ − 1 = 16777215`, then at every `x` from about 2.237×10⁷ up to 2²⁵,
    at only some `x` up to about 4.474×10⁷, and at every `x` from there on. So a saturated control gives
    `α = fl₃₂(π/2) > π/2` and `β = fl₃₂(π) > π` (for `tanh_beta` and `softsign_beta`, `2·fl₃₂(π/2) = fl₃₂(π)`
    exactly). Then `ρ̃_x = cos α ≈ −4.4×10⁻⁸` and `λ̃_y ≈ −8.7×10⁻⁸·sin α`, past `δ_λ = 10⁻¹²`: the pole and the
    mirror seam are reached by rounding, not by the formula.
  - *Lower poles, f32 and f64.* `α = 0` or `β = 0` exactly where the link's output underflows: for σ at `z ≲ −88.7`
    (f32) and `z ≲ −709.8` (f64), where `exp(−z)` overflows; for the tanh links at `z ≲ −9` (f32) and `z ≲ −19.1`
    (f64), where `tanh` rounds to −1; for the softsign links where `x/(2 + |x|)` rounds to −1, at some `x` from
    `x ≈ −3.4×10⁷` and at every `x` from about `−6.7×10⁷` (f32; 2²⁵ and 2²⁶), and at some `x` from `x ≈ −1.8×10¹⁶`
    and at every `x` from about `−3.6×10¹⁶` (f64; 2⁵⁴ and 2⁵⁵). Code review 5481971521 measured `α = fl₃₂(π/2)` at
    `x = 6.8×10⁷` and `α = 0` at `x = −6.8×10⁷` (f32) and a lower pole at `x ≈ 10¹⁷` (f64); `softsign_beta` has the
    same thresholds. The f64 upper images land on `fl₆₄(π/2)` and `fl₆₄(π)`, which are below π/2 and π.
  - *Canonical no-op.* §3.3 rotates first and then runs the mirror test in the rotated frame. At f32 with `z_α`
    saturated, `ρ̃ = (−4.4×10⁻⁸, 0)`, so `φ = atan2(0, −4.4×10⁻⁸) = π` and §3.3 turns the whole state by a half turn,
    followed by the mirror wherever `sin β` is not ≈ 0: after `R(−π)`, `λ̃_y = −sin α·sin β < −δ_λ` (with
    `λ̃ = sin α·(cos β, sin β)`, §3.2). With `z_β` saturated, `λ̃_y < −δ_λ`, so §3.3 takes the mirror. Decoder test 5
    and REQ-DEC-018 do not hold there. REQ-DEC-002's own text (canonicalise's output lies on the section) still
    holds, since the half turn and the mirror put the state back on it; it is named, not marked.
  - The outputs stay finite and decode's identities hold there (PR #192). §3.2's "never reaches the exact poles …
    strictly", decoder tests 3 and 5, REQ-DEC-027, REQ-DEC-018, and the note's and REQ-DEC-028's "never lands" /
    "endpoints excluded" do not hold as written. TASK-M2-01's merged test reads test 8 (a)'s "in range" as the closed
    interval at the f32-rounded bounds, which the §3.9 row's "(a,b)" does not state.
  - *Chart landmarks (REQ-VAL-020; added from TASK-M2-03's pre-flight).* The β links reach the lower edge exactly,
    `β = 0`, at the lower-pole thresholds above (f32 and f64), and at f32 pass the upper edge, `β = fl₃₂(π) > π` (by
    8.7×10⁻⁸). So a decode "near beta edges" at a large enough `|z_β|` lands on the edge, or at f32 past it, where
    REQ-VAL-020's verify has the Euler configurations "approached as a limit" and validation_orbits §1.3 writes
    `beta → 0` and `beta → pi`. The options below are unchanged by this addition; none names REQ-VAL-020's text, and
    under each `β = 0` stays reachable at saturation ((b) keeps the lower poles).
- **Context for weighing the options (physics review 5481954814, finding 3; not a recommendation):** the states past
  the upper poles are valid three-body configurations. With `z_α` saturated the state is the in-range configuration
  (`α' = π − fl₃₂(π/2) < π/2`, `β' = π − β`), mirrored and turned by π about the CoM; `β = fl₃₂(π) > π` is the
  in-range `β' = 2π − fl₃₂(π)`, mirrored. Both sit at a near-collision of the inner pair (`‖ρ̃‖ ≈ 4×10⁻⁸`), which
  the collision and conditioning flags already fence (§3.2), and the overshoot is under one f32 ulp of α near π/2
  (1.19×10⁻⁷). So what (a) gives up
  is the literal range statements and the canonical no-op, not physical correctness.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Amend dd_decoder §3.2, tests 3 and 5, REQ-DEC-027 and REQ-DEC-018 (and the note / REQ-DEC-028) to state the
    behaviour at saturation: the range closed and the poles reachable, at f32 for the upper poles and at f32 and f64
    for the lower poles, for the σ, tanh and softsign links (and, for `q`'s open codomain in §3.9, `sigmoid_q`,
    `tanh_q` and `softsign_q` reaching `±q_max`); the canonical no-op excepting the saturated poles; the outputs
    finite and the identities holding.
  - (b) Generate the f32 constants rounded toward zero (`fl↓(π/2)`, `fl↓(π)`) so the upper image stays inside the
    bounds (a change to the generated links, TASK-M2-01's output). It removes the upper overshoot and so the half turn
    and the mirror; it does not remove the lower poles: `α = 0` or `β = 0` would remain for σ at `z ≲ −88.7` (f32)
    and `z ≲ −709.8` (f64), for the tanh links at `z ≲ −9` (f32) and `z ≲ −19.1` (f64), and for the softsign links
    from `x ≈ −3.4×10⁷` (every `x` from about `−6.7×10⁷`, f32) and from `x ≈ −1.8×10¹⁶` (every `x` from about
    `−3.6×10¹⁶`, f64). The `q` links' `±q_max` would remain as well.
  - (c) Another form the corpus permits. Clamping the output is not one: REQ-DEC-028 and the note exclude "endpoint
    clamping" and "artificial guards".
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing.
- **Waits:**
  - REQ-DEC-027 carries `rq: RQ-270`, so TASK-M2-03 (`decode_ranges`) waits.
  - REQ-DEC-028 carries `rq: RQ-270` (applied per R-369: at f32 the parametrisation reaches its endpoints by rounding,
    which its "endpoints excluded" directly contradicts), so TASK-M2-02 (PR #192) waits.
  - REQ-DEC-018 carries `rq: RQ-270` (the canonical no-op fails at f32 saturation), so TASK-M2-07 (`canonical_noop`)
    waits. REQ-DEC-002, in the same task, is named but not marked: its text is not contradicted.
  - The REQ-CHART-034 tension: §3.9's "Bounded (a,b)" and test 8 (a)'s "bounded outputs in range" against TASK-M2-01's
    merged `link_properties_a_constraint`, which reads the range as closed at the f32-rounded bounds. TASK-M2-01 has
    merged, so the ruling may need a follow-up to that test or to the generated links. REQ-CHART-034 is not marked.
  - REQ-VAL-020 carries `rq: RQ-270` (added from TASK-M2-03's pre-flight: at saturation `β` reaches 0 at f32 and f64
    and passes π at f32, against its "approached as a limit"), so TASK-M2-03 (`chart_landmarks_lagrange_euler`)
    waits, as it already does through REQ-DEC-027. TASK-M3-28, which needs REQ-VAL-020, waits with it.
- **Ruling:** R-417 (11 Oct 2026): options (b) and (a). The f32 constants π/2 and π are generated rounded toward
  zero, removing the upper overshoot, the π turn and the mirror (TASK-M2-30, REQ-GEN-034). dd_decoder §3.2, its tests
  and REQ-DEC-018/027/028 are amended for what remains: the lower poles are reached at underflow (f32 and f64), `q`
  reaches `±q_max`, and the range is closed. Any exception to the canonical no-op covers only measured cases.
  TASK-M2-01's merged `link_properties_a_constraint` is followed up in TASK-M2-30.

## RQ-271: REQ-TOOL-160's log floors for `rho_ratio` and `r_min_pair_0` are an M1 calibration, in "that field's units", but the two fields' formulas are an M2 deliverable (REQ-PAY-088, TASK-M2-03): for `rho_ratio` its units with them, for `r_min_pair_0` only the formula's details and the evidence *(silence, R-71, R-72, R-401, REQ-TOOL-160, REQ-PAY-088; context: TASK-M1-18, TASK-M2-03)*

*Found in TASK-M2-03's read-only pre-flight. Narrowed in PR #194's physics review 5482528309 (F1, the split; F3,
`r_min_pair_0`'s units). Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-18.md` § "Deliverables" (lines 33–34; moved from `TASK-M1-17.md` lines 81–82 by this
    entry's PR): "`crates/ledger`: one log floor per log field without a ledger floor (`d_min`, `dE_max`, `dLz_max`,
    `closure_min`, `rho_ratio`, `r_min_pair_0`), each a named presentation constant in that field's units, read by
    the template"; and (lines 35–36) "The REQ-TOOL-160 proposal: each floor, its units, and its evidence (where the
    field's stored values fall under it on M1's fixtures, and why it sits there), attached to the PR."
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-TOOL-160 (`kind: calibration`, `milestone: M1`, TASK-M1-18): "The log floor ε_f of
    each log-scaled numeric field view whose field has no ledger `floor` (d_min, dE_max, dLz_max, closure_min,
    rho_ratio, r_min_pair_0) — one floor per field, in that field's own units (a distance for the distances, … a
    ratio for rho_ratio; R-401) … must be calibrated: proposed with its evidence per field by the task that needs it,
    checked by a reviewer, confirmed by the human at the M1 gate and recorded in decisions.md."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-401 — Each log-scaled field view has its own floor, in that field's units, proposed with
    evidence for the M1 gate; the single ε = 2⁻²⁴ is replaced": "its own `ε_f`, the magnitude where its compression
    sets in, in that field's own units: a distance for the distances, … and a ratio for `rho_ratio`", and "**The
    floors are calibrations (R-71):** proposed with their evidence for the M1 gate".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PAY-088 (`kind: definition`, `milestone: M2`, TASK-M2-03): "The formulas of the
    ICDescriptor fields `q_mass`, `rho_mag`, `lambda_mag`, `rho_ratio`, `rho_angle` and `r_min_pair_0` (including
    whether `rho_mag` and `lambda_mag` are ‖ρ‖, ‖λ‖ or the mass-weighted ‖ρ̃‖, ‖λ̃‖) must be defined in dd_decoder §3.6
    by the task that needs it". `plan/tasks/M2/TASK-M2-03.md` § "Notes", Gap G7: "the ICDescriptor formulas for …
    `rho_ratio`, `rho_angle` and `r_min_pair_0` are not in the corpus".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "3.6 ICDescriptor derived quantities (decode-time, pre-integration)":
    "`E₀ = K₀ + V₀ ;   virial_ratio = 2K₀ / |V₀| ;   ρ-magnitudes, ρ_ratio, ρ_angle, r_min_pair₀`", names with no
    formula. `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.6 `ICDescriptor` (12 × f32)": "`rho_ratio` (log), …
    `r_min_pair_0` (log). Provenance: decode stage, pre-integration."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "2. Consolidated contract (every clause that binds this component)":
    "Output is `(m, r, p)` in the dimensionless system `G = M = I = 1`"; § "4. Seams (its side of each — the
    integration-test list)", seam 3: "emit exactly `(m, r, p)`, canonical, `G=M=I=1`".
  - `fixtures/golden/BASELINES.md` (lines 84, 88): M1's `generated-r_min_pair_0` and `generated-rho_ratio` cases render
    each "generated view over the showcase set"; the decode stage that writes the two fields is TASK-M2-03's
    (REQ-PAY-034).
- **Silence:** the corpus says the floor is "in that field's own units" and calibrated at the M1 gate, and that the
  field's formula is defined by TASK-M2-03 at M2; it does not say which comes first. The two fields differ in what is
  open:
  - *`rho_ratio`: units and evidence.* R-401 gives the kind of unit, "a ratio", but not which: ‖ρ‖/‖λ‖ and the
    mass-weighted ‖ρ̃‖/‖λ̃‖ differ by a mass-dependent factor, √(μ_ρ/μ_λ) (REQ-PAY-088's own parenthesis), which moves
    the magnitude where compression should set in.
  - *`r_min_pair_0`: evidence and the formula's details, not its units.* R-401 calls it "a distance", and the decoder's
    output gauge, `G = M = I = 1` (§2, seam 3), fixes the scale a distance is measured in. What stays open is the
    formula's details (REQ-PAY-088) and the evidence.
  - For both, M1's fixtures hold showcase values, not decoded ones, so the evidence REQ-TOOL-160 asks for ("where the
    field's stored values fall under it") has no decoded values to fall under before M2.
  - The other four fields are defined in `docs/design/principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` (`d_min` and `closure_min` as
    running minima, `dE_max` and `dLz_max` as absolute maxima, REQ-TOOL-160's note), so the order question is for
    `rho_ratio` and `r_min_pair_0` only.
- **Options seen** (the field distinction above changes only (c) and (d), as marked):
  - (a) Calibrate those two floors at the M2 gate instead: REQ-TOOL-160 is split (or a new calibration requirement
    raised) so TASK-M1-18 proposes four floors for the M1 gate and the two others are proposed with TASK-M2-03's
    definitions, on decoded values. TASK-M1-18 still needs some constant for the two views meanwhile, which the corpus
    does not give, and the M1 gate confirms four floors, not six.
  - (b) Write TASK-M2-03's two definitions (part of REQ-PAY-088) first, ahead of the M1 calibration: a docs change to
    dd_decoder §3.6 for `rho_ratio` and `r_min_pair_0`, reviewed by physics, before TASK-M1-18 proposes. It splits a
    definition out of an M2 task into the M1 path; the evidence is then the formula, not decoded values, since the
    decode stage still lands at M2.
  - (c) Give the two floors provisionally, confirmed at the M1 gate as provisional and re-confirmed at M2 once
    TASK-M2-03 defines the formulas. For `rho_ratio` the proposal states assumed units (which ratio), and the
    assumption may not match the definition, so the floor may change at M2. For `r_min_pair_0` the units are not
    assumed (a distance in `G = M = I = 1`); only the evidence is provisional, so the re-confirmation at M2 is of the
    evidence. Two confirmations of each value.
  - (d) Read R-401's unit kinds ("a ratio", "a distance") as enough to propose the floors at M1 without the formulas,
    with TASK-M2-03's definitions then bound to be consistent with them. For `rho_ratio` it settles which ratio before
    its definition is written and reviewed; for `r_min_pair_0` it settles no unit the gauge leaves open, only the
    evidence, which is then the showcase values and the gauge's scale.
  - (c) and (d) may also be taken per field: one for `rho_ratio`, another for `r_min_pair_0`.
- **Applied meanwhile:** the plan split, applied per R-369 (physics review 5482528309 of PR #194, F1): REQ-TOOL-160's
  closing moves from TASK-M1-17 to a new task, TASK-M1-18 ("The per-field log floors (R-401)"), which depends on
  TASK-M1-17. TASK-M1-17 keeps its four other closures and is not held by this entry. The split settles nothing here.
- **Waits:** REQ-TOOL-160 carries `rq: RQ-271`, so:
  - TASK-M1-18 waits.
  - The M1 gate's confirmation of REQ-TOOL-160 waits, and so the M1 exit, and with it every task the M1 gate holds
    (all later-milestone tasks, save an early merge under R-415).
  - TASK-M7-05 depends on TASK-M1-18 (its `log` compaction reads each field's R-401 floor), and through it TASK-M7-08,
    TASK-M7-09 and TASK-M7-23 and their dependents, a chain that is behind the M1 exit anyway.
  - Checked and not dependent on TASK-M1-18, as none reads a floor value: TASK-M1-14 (its goldens are re-rendered by
    TASK-M1-18 if they draw a log view), TASK-M1-15, TASK-M2-25, TASK-M3-18 and TASK-M6-27.
  - REQ-PAY-088 is named, not marked: TASK-M2-03 can write its definitions under any option, and under (b) only
    sooner.
- **Ruling:** R-419 (11 Oct 2026): option (c) for both fields. `rho_ratio`'s provisional floor assumes the
  mass-weighted ‖ρ̃‖/‖λ̃‖ and says so; TASK-M2-03 may choose otherwise, and the floor is re-confirmed at M2
  (REQ-TOOL-163). For `r_min_pair_0` only the evidence is provisional.

## RQ-272: REQ-PAY-034 asserts every ICDescriptor field in f32 (and f64) against hand-computed values, but the corpus gives no tolerance for that comparison *(silence, R-71, R-85, REQ-PAY-034, REQ-DEC-043, REQ-DEC-044, REQ-VAL-061; context: TASK-M2-03)*

*Found in TASK-M2-03's read-only pre-flight. Widened in PR #194's physics review 5482528309 (F2, the parity
contract's starting tolerance). Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PAY-034 (TASK-M2-03, `ic_descriptor`), verify: "golden IC (z = 0): assert every
    descriptor field in f64 and f32 against hand-computed values; field names match the ledger; the derived E₀ equals
    K_0 + V_0 and the struct has no E₀ field". TASK-M2-03's acceptance line repeats it: "`cargo test -p kernel
    ic_descriptor` — golden IC (z = 0): every descriptor field in f64 and f32 against hand-computed values".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "5. Unit tests", the golden anchor: "**`z = 0` decodes to the canonical
    golden IC** — equal masses `(⅓,⅓,⅓)`, `α = π/4` (‖ρ̃‖ = ‖λ̃‖), `β = π/2`, rest (`q = 0`), `I = 1`. Assert its every
    derived value in f64 and f32." No tolerance is given there.
  - The same section, test 12: "the CPU-f64 and GPU-f32 instantiations (and browser-GPU-via-WGSL) agree on the golden
    IC and fuzzed interiors to f32 eps-scaled tolerance on continuous values". `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-DEC-043
    (calibration, TASK-M2-06): "dd_decoder test 12's f32-eps scale factor for cross-backend agreement on continuous
    decode values must be calibrated", its evidence "f64-vs-f32 decode differences on the golden IC and fuzzed
    interiors".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-DEC-044 (calibration, TASK-M2-02): the tolerances of "dd_decoder unit tests 1 (the
    identities …), 6 (the reconstruction inverse) and 8 (the invariant construction's L_z and K)". On PR #192's branch
    only (not on main), REQ-DEC-045 (test 1's Σpᵢ = 0) and REQ-DEC-046 (test 8's L_z and K) are split from it. None of
    the three names a descriptor field.
  - `docs/contracts/principia_parity_contract.md` § "Tier N — tight numerical (checkable because nothing amplifies)":
    "**decode → `(m, r, p)`, `ICDescriptor`, `E₀`**: the whole Matter rung (decoder dd §5)"; and § "4. Tolerance — and
    the cross-backend reality": "Tier-N tolerance absorbs **two** sources of difference, not one: 1. **f32 vs f64** —
    the intended comparison. 2. **cross-backend f32 variance**" (lines 141–144), and (line 148) "Relative tolerance,
    per quantity class: IC/decode ~1e-5; one-step state ~1e-5 scaled by force magnitude; monitored `E₀`/`L_z` at t=0
    ~1e-6. These are starting points to be replaced by measured values."
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-VAL-061 (`milestone: M4`, TASK-M4-04): "Parity Tier N must compare elementwise within
    tight tolerance: decode → `(m, r, p)`, `ICDescriptor` and `E₀`; …", verified with "starting tolerances: IC/decode
    ~1e-5 relative; … — to be replaced by measured values". REQ-TOOL-029's note (R-133): "before REQ-VAL-064 sets Tier
    N (M4), 'Tier-N tolerance' here is read as REQ-DEC-043's calibrated f32 decode factor."
- **Silence:** no requirement or doc gives the tolerance at which an f32 (or f64) descriptor field matches its
  hand-computed value. The nearest value is the parity contract's "IC/decode ~1e-5" relative, which covers the
  `ICDescriptor`, but it does not settle this comparison as written: it is Tier N's, a cross-backend parity check
  closed at M4 (REQ-VAL-061, TASK-M4-04) and set there by measurement (R-85, REQ-VAL-064); it is explicitly a
  "starting point" to be replaced; and it compares f32 with f64, not with hand-computed values (though "f32 vs f64 —
  the intended comparison" is one reading of REQ-PAY-034's f32 leg). It gives the f64 leg nothing, and as a relative
  tolerance it says nothing for a field whose golden value is 0 (`K_0` and `virial_ratio` at rest). Test 12's
  tolerance is a cross-backend agreement (CPU-f64 against GPU-f32), not a comparison with hand-computed values, and
  its factor (REQ-DEC-043) is proposed by TASK-M2-06, which runs after TASK-M2-03
  (TASK-M2-06 depends on TASK-M2-05, which depends on TASK-M2-03). REQ-DEC-044's tests do not include the
  descriptor. "Hand-computed" at f32 is itself open: the f32 rounding of the exact value, or the value the formula
  gives evaluated in f32.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) A new R-71 calibration requirement for the descriptor fields' per-precision tolerances against the
    hand-computed golden values, proposed with evidence by TASK-M2-03 and confirmed by the human at the M2 gate, as
    REQ-DEC-044 is for tests 1, 6 and 8. One more M2-gate calibration; TASK-M2-03 can run, with its value provisional
    until the gate.
  - (b) Reuse test 12's "f32 eps-scaled tolerance" (REQ-DEC-043's factor) for the f32 comparison. No new value, but
    the factor is calibrated by TASK-M2-06, after TASK-M2-03, for a different comparison (f64 against GPU-f32); either
    TASK-M2-03 waits for it, or REQ-DEC-043's proposal moves earlier. It leaves the f64 comparison without a
    tolerance.
  - (c) Bit-exact f32 (and f64) where the formula allows it, a tolerance only where it does not. At rest
    `K_0 = 0` and `virial_ratio = 0` are exact; fields through a quotient, square root or trigonometric function (`V_0`,
    and whatever REQ-PAY-088 defines for the magnitudes and `rho_angle`) are exact only if "hand-computed" means the
    same operations in the same order at that precision, so the fields left over still need (a) or (b).
  - (d) Take the parity contract's "IC/decode ~1e-5" relative (§4, REQ-VAL-061) as the f32 leg's provisional
    tolerance, against the f64 value or the hand-computed one, until Tier N is measured at M4. A corpus value, no new
    one; but it is a placeholder set for a different, cross-backend check, it leaves the golden's zero-valued fields
    and the f64 leg to (a) or (c), and the M4 measurement may then change what TASK-M2-03's test asserts.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing.
- **Waits:** REQ-PAY-034 carries `rq: RQ-272`, so TASK-M2-03 (`ic_descriptor`) waits. TASK-M3-19 and TASK-M3-22, which
  need REQ-PAY-034, wait with it. TASK-M2-03 also waits on RQ-270 (REQ-DEC-027, REQ-VAL-020) and on TASK-M2-02.
- **Ruling:** R-421 (11 Oct 2026): options (c) and (a). Bit-exact for the fields that are 0 at rest; per-precision
  tolerances for the others proposed by TASK-M2-03 and confirmed at the M2 gate (REQ-PAY-095). "Hand-computed at f32"
  means the exact value rounded to f32.

## RQ-273: REQ-TOOL-011's catalogue checklist covers two render_contract Part 6 rows the corpus gives no way to build in M1: the uniform echo (`SimUniforms`, `quality_tier`, unnamed thresholds) and the Optional Fourier block (an undefined truncated-Fourier path) *(silence and placement conflict, R-111, R-113, REQ-TOOL-011, REQ-PERF-014; context: TASK-M1-19, TASK-M1-14, TASK-M3-06, TASK-M5-02)*

*Found in TASK-M1-14's read-only pre-flight. Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Field views (one per field, every struct)" (line 318), the uniform
    echo row: "**Uniform echo** — flat swatches of `quality_tier`, `n_renorm` (R-111), thresholds *as currently bound*
    | `SimUniforms` | the CPU→GPU binding path — catches "slider moved but nothing rebound"".
  - The same table (line 315), the Optional Fourier block row: "Optional Fourier block: \|a_k\| per k, ω | quad payload
    | the truncated-Fourier path when enabled".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-TOOL-011 (`milestone: M1`, now TASK-M1-19): "The field-view catalogue must cover every
    group in render contract Part 6's field-view table that a synthetic payload can show: … the optional Fourier block,
    … and the uniform echo (quality_tier, n_renorm, thresholds)"; its verify: "tick each table row against the
    generated catalogue on a synthetic payload; the DECODE row is REQ-RENDER-027's (M2), the ensemble rows and the live
    effort heatmap are the M5 and M3 requirements R-113 split off; the uniform echo reads n_renorm, not M (R-111)".
    `plan/tasks/M1/TASK-M1-19.md` § "Acceptance tests" (line 31; moved from `TASK-M1-14.md` line 43 by this entry's
    PR): "… ticked against the generated catalogue on a synthetic payload, the uniform echo reading `quality_tier`,
    `n_renorm` and thresholds".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-113 — The placement fixes are accepted as written *(closes RQ-93 to RQ-100)*", as REQ-TOOL-011's
    note records it: "R-113 (RQ-94): DECODE row → REQ-RENDER-027 (M2); tier-gated ensemble views → a separate M5
    requirement; the live current-substep effort heatmap → the M3 requirement split from REQ-TOOL-010." It split off
    those three rows only; the echo and Fourier rows stayed with the M1 checklist.
  - `quality_tier`: `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PERF-014 (M5, TASK-M5-02): "There must be six quality tiers — Potato,
    Low, Medium, High, Ultra, Extreme …"; `plan/tasks/M5/TASK-M5-02.md` § "Deliverables": "`QualityTier` (six variants
    plus Custom)", and § "Acceptance tests" (line 46): "`cargo test -p engine quality_tier_enum` — the tier enum has six
    variants".
  - `SimUniforms`: `plan/tasks/M3/TASK-M3-06.md` § "Deliverables": "`crates/engine/src/contract/sim_uniforms.rs` —
    `SimUniforms { t_horizon, dt_macro, n_max, r_sub, gamma_sub, r_coll, tau, escape_window, r_close, eps_e, eps_l, g,
    m_total, epsilon, n_renorm, delta0 }`" (M3; REQ-INT-026), with no `quality_tier` field.
  - The code at origin/main 95c74bc9: no `SimUniforms` or `quality_tier` in `crates/`. The M1 harness's uniform block,
    `render::bind::Context` (`crates/render/src/bind.rs`, line 83), carries `n_renorm` but no tier and no thresholds.
  - The Fourier path: no doc in `docs/` outside the archive, no requirement other than REQ-TOOL-011, and no task in
    `plan/` builds or defines "the truncated-Fourier path", its `a_k` or its ω, or says when it is "enabled".
    `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 1 — The payload (render input)" gives the quad payload,
    `RenderQuad`, as nine fields, none of them Fourier. (Only `docs/archive/spec_sources/principia_spec_revised.tex`,
    record only per R-159, describes a truncated Fourier preview.)
- **Silence (and a placement conflict):**
  - *The uniform echo.* The row reads two names the M1 plan does not build: `SimUniforms` is TASK-M3-06's struct (M3)
    and `quality_tier` TASK-M5-02's enum (M5), and TASK-M3-06's `SimUniforms` holds no tier. "Thresholds *as currently
    bound*" names no threshold: `SimUniforms` carries several candidates (`r_sub`, `r_coll`, `tau`, `escape_window`,
    `r_close`, `eps_e`, `eps_l`), and the corpus does not say which the echo shows. An M1 checklist that ticks the row
    "on a synthetic payload" has no tier to echo and no list of thresholds; R-113 moved the three other rows that need
    a later milestone's machinery, but not this one.
  - *The Optional Fourier block.* The row reads a quad-payload block that the quad payload does not have, from "the
    truncated-Fourier path when enabled", which no doc, requirement or task defines or builds. Nothing says whether
    the row is ticked as absent (the path is "optional" and never enabled) or must show a view.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) The echo row moves, as R-113 moved the DECODE, ensemble and live-effort rows: to a task that depends on
    TASK-M5-02 (and TASK-M3-06), whose requirement names the thresholds the echo shows (for example the `SimUniforms`
    event thresholds, or a stated subset). REQ-TOOL-011's checklist then marks the row with that requirement. Placing
    the row needs a new or widened requirement; naming the thresholds is an R-72 definition.
  - (b) The echo row stays at M1, reduced to what M1 binds: `n_renorm` (in `render::bind::Context`) and a named list of
    thresholds the harness binds, with `quality_tier` ticked "absent until TASK-M5-02". The thresholds are still to be
    named (R-72), and the row's `SimUniforms` reads the M1 harness's block, not TASK-M3-06's struct.
  - (c) The Fourier row is ticked "absent until <task>", once a task is named to define and build the truncated-Fourier
    path (a new definition requirement, R-72); until then the checklist marks it with that requirement, as the DECODE
    row is marked with REQ-RENDER-027.
  - (d) The Fourier row is held until a task defines the path: REQ-TOOL-011 keeps it, and TASK-M1-19 waits, as now.
  - (e) The human drops or defers either row (a ruling; render_contract Part 6 then conformed). Dropping or deferring
    is the human's call only.
  - Note on (a) and any option that has TASK-M1-19 wait for TASK-M5-02: TASK-M2-25 now depends on TASK-M1-19 (below),
    and TASK-M5-02 is downstream of TASK-M2-25, so TASK-M1-19 cannot itself depend on TASK-M5-02 without a cycle; the
    row has to move to another task, as (a) says.
- **Applied meanwhile:** the plan split, applied per R-369 on PR #194's precedent: REQ-TOOL-011's closing moves from
  TASK-M1-14 to a new task, TASK-M1-19 ("The render_contract Part 6 catalogue checklist (REQ-TOOL-011)"), which
  depends on TASK-M1-14, with reviewers code, qa and physics, as TASK-M1-14's. The checklist deliverable and its qa
  acceptance line move with it. TASK-M1-14 keeps its four other closures and is not held by this entry. TASK-M2-25,
  which needs REQ-TOOL-011, depends on TASK-M1-19. The split settles nothing here.
- **Waits:** REQ-TOOL-011 carries `rq: RQ-273`, so:
  - TASK-M1-19 waits.
  - The M1 exit waits with it, as TASK-M1-19 is an M1 task, and with it every task the M1 gate holds (all
    later-milestone tasks, save an early merge under R-415).
  - TASK-M2-25 depends on TASK-M1-19, and through it TASK-M2-26, TASK-M2-29, TASK-M3-19 and TASK-M4-06 and their
    dependents, TASK-M3-22, TASK-M5-02 and TASK-M7-17 among them (152 tasks in all), a chain that is behind the M1 exit
    anyway.
  - Checked and not held: TASK-M1-14, and TASK-M1-15 and TASK-M3-18, which depend on TASK-M1-14 but need nothing of
    REQ-TOOL-011.
- **Ruling:** R-420 (11 Oct 2026): the echo row, option (a), moved after TASK-M5-02 and TASK-M3-06 to TASK-M5-31,
  which names its thresholds (REQ-TOOL-164, REQ-TOOL-165). The Fourier row, option (e): removed from render_contract
  Part 6 and parked as a possible future feature (philosophy §7.6a). M1 doesn't wait on it.

## RQ-275: render_contract Part 6's quad-fields row lists five payload/status flags read from `ctx.quad` that no contract defines: `RenderQuad` and the stain's `CtxQuad` carry none; the corpus offers per-variant and per-frame guards, a per-build constant and each cached payload's compatibility signature as candidate sources for some of them, and no quad-level sim_failed flag or count *(silence and placement, R-72, R-89, R-113, R-145, REQ-TOOL-011, REQ-TOOL-026; context: TASK-M1-19, TASK-M1-13)*

*Found in TASK-M1-19's read-only pre-flight. Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Field views (one per field, every struct)" (line 315), the quad
    fields row: "Quad fields — all 9 (depth, state enum, coherence, impurity, spread, suspect fraction, priority, cache
    age, ancestor gap) + payload/status flags (sim-failed, cache-valid, contains-ensemble, contains-FTLE, schema
    version) | `ctx.quad` | the **CPU scheduler** and the payload compatibility signature — CPU-written, so a wrong view
    here exonerates the GPU".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 1 — The payload (render input)" (line 15): "**`RenderQuad`**
    (CPU-written): `quad_depth, quad_state, coherence_score, outcome_impurity, ensemble_spread, suspect_fraction,
    priority_score, ancestor_gap, cache_age`", with no flag.
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.7a `RenderQuad` — the CPU-written quad record" (lines 462–485):
    "it is the ledger table the generated `RenderQuad` follows, member for member … the fragment reads it as
    `ctx.quad` (render contract Part 1; Part 6's "Quad fields" view)", with ten members, `quad_depth`, `quad_state`,
    `coherence_score`, `outcome_impurity`, `ensemble_spread`, `suspect_fraction`, `priority_score`, `ancestor_gap`,
    `cache_age` and `dominant_outcome`, and no flag. It adds: "It is scheduler state, not payload, and the payload
    compatibility signature excludes scheduler state … so it is not hashed into the schema version." That sentence
    says `RenderQuad`'s own bytes are not hashed into the version; it does not say whether `RenderQuad` may report the
    version of the payload it describes.
  - The code at origin/main 55513b7b: `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/stain/context.wgsl`, `struct CtxQuad` (lines 19–36),
    holds `index`, `tl`, `centre`, `half_width`, `uv`, `sample_count` and the `RenderQuad` members, and no flag.
  - contains-FTLE and contains-ensemble: `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 2 — Fixed pipeline,
    swappable slots" (line 46): "`has_ftle`/`has_word` are baked consts; `has_ensemble` is a uniform, read the same
    way, so toggling E never re-bakes (R-145 …)". `crates/render/shaders/wgsl/lib/prelude.wgsl` (lines 6–25): "This
    variant's tier (lowering Part 3a): baked … `has_ftle` is whether the stored variant is `SimStateFTLE`",
    `const has_ftle: bool = true;`, and the `PreludeUniforms` member `has_ensemble`, read by `fn has_ensemble()`.
    `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.5 The live-state block (replaces the checkpoint array —
    lockstep, ratified)" (line 167): "ensemble-on/off is a quad/tier flag (`contains-ensemble`)", and (line 156) "Tier gates
    existence; `contains-ensemble` / `contains-FTLE` advertise it". Each is per variant or per frame, not a
    `RenderQuad` member. RQ-239 (archived, `docs/archive/review_queue/M0.md`) decided for REQ-TOOL-026's spread view
    only that "Present iff contains-ensemble" reads `has_ensemble()`.
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Part 3 — Cache tiers and the recompute rule" (line 74),
    "Field-availability gating": "ensemble spread (footprint-derived at resolve) exists only when `contains-ensemble` —
    but it is not a per-sample field, so "availability" means the resolve stage runs the spread reduction, gated on
    the tier flag."
  - The schema version: `crates/render/frag/generated/payload_unpack.wgsl` (line 7), `const PAYLOAD_SCHEMA_VERSION:
    vec2<u32> = …`, one constant per build.
  - sim-failed: the per-sample `state` code 4, `sim_failed` (`docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.1
    `sample_descriptor` (u32)", line 37; render contract Part 5's `sd_is_failed`). `QuadReduction` (§ "3.7
    `QuadReduction` — completed ledger") carries `terminated_fraction`, `class_histogram[N]` and `dominant_outcome`,
    and, under "Validity and diagnostics" (line 408), "`valid_sample_count` | u16 | decoded of N²", a quad-level count
    of the samples that decoded, so of decode failures only. No quad-level sim_failed flag or count is defined.
    `RenderQuad` carries none of these but `dominant_outcome`.
  - The payload compatibility signature, which the row says it certifies: `docs/contracts/principia_caching_contract.md`
    § "Part 1 — Two-level keying: identity vs validity" (line 10): "every cached payload carries a compact
    compatibility signature that is stricter than the quad key, because it governs whether a payload can be reused
    without recomputation", and (line 14): "**Validity — *computed under which physics config*.** The payload
    compatibility signature: … enabled metrics (tier flags), event thresholds, `copy_index` for an ensemble copy (the
    nominal's signature excludes `E`, R-89), payload schema version (a content hash of the ledger, R-36)". Each drawn
    quad's payload so carries its own tier flags and schema version, which can differ from the current variant's
    `has_ftle` and `PAYLOAD_SCHEMA_VERSION` (a quad drawn from an ancestor's payload, or one computed at another
    tier); and "cache-valid" can read as that payload's signature matching the current config. `decisions.md` §
    "R-89 — Depth and E are not on the sim key *(closes RQ-40)*": "E changes live: copies are cached per
    `copy_index`, and the nominal's key excludes E", which bears on whether contains-ensemble is in it. No per-quad
    flag is defined from the signature, and no M1 cache exists.
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-TOOL-011 (`milestone: M1`, TASK-M1-19): "The field-view catalogue must cover every
    group in render contract Part 6's field-view table that a synthetic payload can show: … all 9 quad fields plus
    payload/status flags, and structural overlays". REQ-TOOL-026 (M1, TASK-M1-13, merged), the quad views: "quad-depth,
    quad-state …, coherence/impurity …, ensemble spread (present iff contains-ensemble), suspect fraction, priority
    score, cache age / ancestor gap, leaf outlines, fallback tint and pending hatch", with no status flag.
  - `decisions.md` § "R-113 — The placement fixes are accepted as written *(closes RQ-93 to RQ-100)*", as REQ-TOOL-011's
    note records it, split off the DECODE, ensemble and live-effort rows; § "R-420 — The uniform echo row moves …
    *(closes RQ-273)*" moved the uniform echo row and removed the Fourier row. Neither split off the flags.
- **Silence (and a placement question):** the row reads five flags from `ctx.quad`, but no contract makes any of them a
  `RenderQuad` member or says what each holds per quad. The corpus offers more than one candidate source and does not
  choose: contains-FTLE and contains-ensemble have a per-variant or per-frame reading in the prelude (`has_ftle`,
  `has_ensemble()`) and render_contract :74's "tier flag"; the schema version a per-build constant
  (`PAYLOAD_SCHEMA_VERSION`); and contains-FTLE, the schema version and cache-valid (and, R-89 aside, contains-ensemble)
  a per-quad reading in the drawn payload's compatibility signature (caching Part 1). sim-failed has no quad-level
  source: `valid_sample_count` counts decode failures only. An M1 checklist that ticks the row "on a synthetic payload"
  has nothing defined to tick for the flags; R-113 and R-420 placed other rows that need later machinery, but not these.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Define them now (R-72, a definition requirement): each flag's meaning per quad and its source (a `RenderQuad`
    member, the prelude guard, the schema constant, or the drawn payload's compatibility signature, caching Part 1),
    written into dd_generation_root §3.7a or render_contract Part 6, as a TASK-M1-19 deliverable or a new task's.
  - (b) Split them off to the task that builds their source, as R-113 split the DECODE, ensemble and live-effort rows:
    cache-valid with the M5 cache, per-quad sim-failed with the reduction (`QuadReduction`), and so on, each under a
    named requirement that REQ-TOOL-011's checklist then cites for the cell.
  - (c) Drop the flags from Part 6's row (a ruling; render_contract Part 6 then conformed). Dropping is the human's
    call only.
  - The options may be taken per flag: for example contains-ensemble and contains-FTLE ticked against the prelude's
    `has_ensemble()` and `has_ftle` (as RQ-239 read contains-ensemble for REQ-TOOL-026), and the rest split or dropped.
- **Applied meanwhile:** TASK-M1-19 is built with every other row of Part 6's table; its PR's checklist marks the five
  flag cells of the quad fields row as waiting on RQ-275. The row's nine quad fields are ticked as usual. This settles
  nothing here.
- **Waits:** REQ-TOOL-011 carries `rq: RQ-275`, for the quad fields row's five flags only, so TASK-M1-19's PR, which
  closes REQ-TOOL-011, does not merge until this entry is ruled (`plan/OPERATIONS.md` § "Autonomy (R-369)": a PR
  merges once "it waits on no open `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` entry"). With it wait the M1 exit and the chain behind
  TASK-M1-19 that RQ-273 listed (TASK-M2-25 and its dependents), which is behind the M1 exit anyway. TASK-M1-13
  (REQ-TOOL-026), merged, is not reopened.
- **Ruling:** R-424 (13 Oct 2026): as recommended, per flag. contains-ensemble and contains-FTLE are checked against
  `has_ensemble()` and `has_ftle` (as RQ-239 did); the schema version against `PAYLOAD_SCHEMA_VERSION` for M1;
  sim-failed moves to the `QuadReduction` task (TASK-M5-17, REQ-PAY-096); cache-valid moves to the M5 cache task
  (TASK-M5-08, REQ-SCHED-099), which also decides whether a per-quad schema version belongs with cache-valid.

## RQ-276: render_contract Part 6's structural-overlays row lists a "visible-set" and a "locked/stale" overlay that nothing defines or builds; render_gui_spec §12.1 calls them Tier 3, and TASK-M5-29, which builds Tier 3, names neither *(silence and placement, R-72, REQ-TOOL-011, REQ-TOOL-026, REQ-RENDER-055, REQ-PERF-079; context: TASK-M1-19, TASK-M1-13, TASK-M5-29, TASK-M8-08)*

*Found in TASK-M1-19's read-only pre-flight. Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_render_contract.md` § "Field views (one per field, every struct)" (line 316), the
    structural overlays row: "Structural overlays (quadtree boundaries, active leaf outlines, fallback tint, pending
    hatch, visible-set, locked/stale) | post node + quad + `ctx.uv` | the quad/instance render path and cache
    behaviour".
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "12.1 Structural overlays and tile debug shaders" (lines 893–895): "The
    rest of that render-contract list (**fallback tint**, **pending hatch**, **visible-set**, **locked/stale**) is
    Tier 3 by this test: those are *values attached to a quad*, not functions of position within it." Its tier table's
    Tier 3 examples are "refine-decision, dirty, resident, generation, budget, ancestor gap", neither overlay among
    them.
  - `plan/tasks/M5/TASK-M5-29.md` ("Tier 3 tile debug shaders and the quad-state transition table"; REQ-RENDER-055,
    REQ-RENDER-056, REQ-TOOL-116) builds the Tier 3 path and names neither visible-set nor locked/stale (its RQ-239
    note, line 40, names the fallback tint and the pending hatch, as M1 post occupants); its note: "Its per-visible-quad record is
    REQ-PERF-079's (TASK-M8-08)", whose statement is "The Tier-3 tile-debug buffer's per-visible-quad record must be
    defined in render_gui_spec §12.1 by the task that needs it".
  - `docs/design/principia_debug_tooling_plan.md` § "F. Structural views — `RenderQuad` / quadtree (read quad metadata,
    not payload)" lists the overlays "quadtree boundaries / leaf outlines" ("active leaf set correct; pinned chain never
    evicted") and "fallback ancestor tint / pending hatch", and no visible-set or locked overlay. REQ-TOOL-026 (M1,
    TASK-M1-13, merged) names "leaf outlines, fallback tint and pending hatch", which TASK-M1-13 built as post
    occupants reading `ctx.quad` (RQ-239); REQ-TOOL-011 (M1, TASK-M1-19) covers "structural overlays".
  - "stale", two readings in the corpus:
    - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.7a `RenderQuad` — the CPU-written quad record": "`quad_state` |
      u32 | 0 loaded · 1 pending · 2 refinable · 3 terminal · 4 stale", which the quad-state view `s_state`
      (`crates/render/shaders/wgsl/frag/debug/s_state.wgsl`, REQ-TOOL-026) already draws as a discrete colour.
    - `docs/contracts/principia_caching_contract.md` § "Part 5 — The stale backdrop: blur means loading": "The stale
      quads render through the normal pipeline from their cached `SimState`s — but under lockstep those states are
      **frozen at whatever `t` each had reached** when it left the live set (stale quads do not march; only the live
      set does — scheduler Part 7)", composited, blurred, under the fresh-identity quads as "the live stale layer".
  - "locked", no definition as a quad property; readings in the corpus:
    - The chart (tilt) lock: `docs/contracts/principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` § "The lock (projective microscope)"
      ("locked mode"); and caching Part 5 (line 98), which pairs the two words: "under a *locked* tilt, the anchor
      pixel's stale value is exactly correct (the lock is the fixed point of a basis edit) and staleness grows with
      distance from centre — so the honest stale rendering is a radially graded blur".
    - The pinned class: `docs/contracts/principia_caching_contract.md` § "Part 4 — Baseline-first: an absolute tier,
      not a priority weight" (line 62): "the baseline cover and its ancestor chain for the visible region, **plus the
      backdrop identity's leaf cover (Part 5)**, are pinned"; debug_tooling_plan §F's boundary overlay asserts "pinned
      chain never evicted".
    - The corpus also uses "locked" for locked debug presets (`docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "11. Presets =
      whole graphs"), not a quad property.
  - "visible-set", no definition as an overlay; readings in the corpus:
    - The live set: `docs/contracts/principia_scheduler_contract.md` § "Part 7 — The frame loop (lockstep
      presentation)" (line 176): "BARRIER over the live set only", and its closing summary (line 223): "The frame loop
      presents only the barrier-synced live set"; `docs/design/principia_temporal_architecture_note.md` § "The frame
      loop — the one genuinely new object (and what makes lockstep clean)" (lines 134–137): "LIVE SET     quads already
      at the playhead. March together, barrier-synced, presented coherently every frame", beside "CATCHING-UP"
      quads; `docs/contracts/principia_canonical_spec.md` § "5. Temporal & rendering model *(authoritative:
      `temporal_architecture_note`, `render_contract`, `scheduler_contract`, `checkerboard_contract`)*" (line 67): "every visible
      sample's `SimState` marches with it under a **barrier over the live set**".
    - `docs/contracts/principia_export_animation_contract.md` § "Part 2 — The two playback types and their costs"
      (line 30): "one full integration of the visible set".
    - `RenderQuad` is written "one per visible quad" (§3.7a), and the caching contract speaks of "the visible region".
  - Search at origin/main 55513b7b: the hyphenated "visible-set" occurs only at render_contract:316 and
    render_gui_spec:894, and "locked/stale" only there (the unhyphenated "visible set" and "live set" are above); no
    requirement in `plan/requirements.yaml` and no task file in `plan/tasks/` names either (outside `docs/archive/`,
    `docs/reference/` and `workbench/`, record only per R-159).
- **Silence (and a placement question):** the row lists two overlays that no doc defines (what a visible-set overlay
  marks, what "locked/stale" marks for a quad), no requirement names, and no task builds; the corpus offers competing
  readings for each word (above) and does not choose. render_gui_spec §12.1 places them in Tier 3, which TASK-M5-29
  builds at M5 with its record defined at M8 (REQ-PERF-079), but neither task names them; the other two overlays that
  sentence placed in Tier 3, the fallback tint and the pending hatch, were built at M1 as post occupants under RQ-239.
  The row's other overlays (quadtree boundaries, active leaf outlines, fallback tint, pending hatch) exist.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Define both now (R-72, a definition requirement) and build them in M1, as TASK-M1-19's deliverable or a new
    task's, reading `ctx.quad` as the fallback tint and pending hatch do.
  - (b) Move both to TASK-M5-29's Tier 3 path, under a named requirement that defines them (R-72); REQ-TOOL-011's
    checklist then cites that requirement for the two cells, as it cites REQ-RENDER-027 for the DECODE row.
  - (c) Name a reading from the corpus for each word, the overlay then defined (R-72) and placed under (a) or (b), or
    ticked against an existing view: "stale" as `quad_state` 4 (ticked against `s_state`) or as caching Part 5's stale
    layer (frozen quads off the live set, with Part 5's locked-tilt grading); "locked" as the chart (tilt) lock or as
    Part 4's pinned class; "visible-set" as the live set (scheduler Part 7; temporal note), or another reading; or
    either overlay dropped (a ruling).
  - (d) Drop both from Part 6's row (a ruling; render_contract Part 6 and render_gui_spec §12.1 then conformed).
    Dropping is the human's call only.
  - The options may be taken per overlay.
- **Applied meanwhile:** TASK-M1-19 is built with every other row of Part 6's table; its PR's checklist marks the
  visible-set and locked/stale cells of the structural overlays row as waiting on RQ-276. The row's other four
  overlays are ticked as usual. This settles nothing here.
- **Waits:** REQ-TOOL-011 carries `rq: RQ-276`, for those two overlays only, so TASK-M1-19's PR does not merge until
  this entry is ruled (`plan/OPERATIONS.md` § "Autonomy (R-369)"), with the M1 exit and the chain behind TASK-M1-19, as
  under RQ-275. TASK-M5-29 does not wait: it builds the Tier 3 path under every option, and (b) needs it to run; it is
  named, not marked. TASK-M1-13 (REQ-TOOL-026), merged, is not reopened.
- **Ruling:** R-425 (13 Oct 2026): option (b). Both overlays move to TASK-M5-29 under a new defining requirement
  (REQ-TOOL-166), which reuses existing corpus meanings where they exist (for example, `quad_state` 4 for "stale")
  rather than defining new ones.

## RQ-274: R-423's M1-gate check cannot confirm `q_mass` and `mean_y` on R-400's cool-warm ramp: `q_mass`'s formula and `mean_y`'s per-sample `y` are undefined, each ledger range is the unbounded default, and the corpus's own words read each as non-negative *(silence, R-400, R-423, R-72, REQ-RENDER-084, REQ-RENDER-085, REQ-PAY-088, REQ-PAY-073; context: the M1 gate, TASK-M1-17, TASK-M1-20, TASK-M2-03, TASK-M3-13)*

*Found by the physics review of PR #197 (review 5484757609, § "The M1-gate five-field check (R-423)"), which ran
R-423's gate check of the five fields early. Filed per `plan/MILESTONES.md` § "M1"'s gate item: "A field that fails
goes to `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` for the human". Nothing is chosen.*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-423 — At the M1 gate physics checks the six fields R-400 moved to cool-warm; `theta`, an angle,
    takes the cyclic ramp, not the diverging one *(amends R-400)*", the human's words: "For the M1 gate: physics to
    check the six fields moving to cool-warm." `plan/MILESTONES.md` § "M1 — The synthetic payload and the eyes", the
    gate item: "a read-only physics check of `q_mass`, `ftle`, `mean_y`, `S` and `diffusion`: that each is a signed
    quantity whose zero is a meaningful centre for a diverging ramp centred at zero. A field that fails goes to
    `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` for the human".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-400 — A numeric field view whose declared ledger range spans zero is on a diverging ramp
    centred at zero; fields that can't be negative keep viridis *(closes RQ-260)*": "Where §3 gives a field no range
    the ledger declares (−∞, ∞) (`crates/ledger/src/payload.rs`:7), which spans zero; a field the corpus gives a
    non-negative domain must not read as signed by that default. So TASK-M1-17 declares the lower bound of each field
    whose domain the corpus or the field's own definition makes non-negative". REQ-RENDER-084 (TASK-M1-17, merged in
    PR #195) is the requirement that draws a field whose range spans zero on `ramp_coolwarm`.
  - `q_mass`: `crates/ledger/src/payload.rs`:261, `ic("q_mass", 3, Scale::Lin),` with no `.range(…)`, so the
    unbounded default, beside `m0`–`m2` (`.range(fraction)`) and `rho_mag`, `lambda_mag` (`.range(from_zero)`).
    `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.6 `ICDescriptor` (12 × f32)" (:171) lists "`q_mass`" with no
    scale or range; `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` § "3.6 ICDescriptor derived quantities (decode-time,
    pre-integration)" gives no formula. `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PAY-088 (`kind: definition`, `milestone: M2`,
    TASK-M2-03): "The formulas of the ICDescriptor fields `q_mass`, `rho_mag`, `lambda_mag`, `rho_ratio`, `rho_angle`
    and `r_min_pair_0` … must be defined in dd_decoder §3.6 by the task that needs it".
    `docs/design/principia_colour_composition.md` § "1.2 Family B — field-ramp  →  `vec3` or `f32`" (:95) lists among
    the decoded-IC quantities "mass ratios".
  - `mean_y`: `crates/ledger/src/payload.rs`:217, `f32_at("mean_y", 26, Scale::Lin),`, the unbounded default.
    `docs/design/principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` § "4. Welford diffusion (streaming regression)": "Slope of spread `y`
    on time `t` (the diffusion coefficient)"; `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.4 `SimState` scalars —
    with presentation metadata" (:113), the mini-spec: "Regression of spread `y` on time `t`, slope = diffusion rate",
    and (:123) "`mean_y += dy / n`", so `mean_y` is the running mean of `y`.
    `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-PAY-073 (`kind: definition`, `milestone: M3`, TASK-M3-13): "The per-sample value `y`
    that the Welford diffusion regression fits against time must be defined in `principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` §4 by
    the task that needs it".
- **Physics' result** (review 5484757609): `S`, `ftle` and `diffusion` pass; each is signed at finite time, with zero
  a meaningful centre, and none is an angle. `q_mass` and `mean_y` are **not confirmed**:
  - `q_mass`: "Its formula is unwritten (REQ-PAY-088, TASK-M2-03, M2; dd_decoder §3.6 lists only the name). Its ledger
    range … is the unbounded default, not derived. A mass ratio would be ≥ 0, which is viridis under R-400; a
    log-ratio or asymmetry would be signed. The corpus doesn't settle it."
  - `mean_y`: "It is the running mean of the per-sample y, which is undefined (REQ-PAY-073, M3, R-72). The corpus calls
    y a 'spread' (payload §4; generation_root §3.4 mini-spec), which would make mean_y ≥ 0 and viridis. Its ledger
    range (payload.rs:217) is again the unbounded default."
- **Silence:** the corpus does not say whether `q_mass` or `mean_y` can be negative. Each is on `ramp_coolwarm` only
  because no range was declared, which is R-400's default reading, not a finding that the field is signed; and the
  corpus's own words ("mass ratios"; "spread `y`") lean the other way. Their definitions are later milestones' (M2 and
  M3), so the M1 gate cannot settle the check from the corpus.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Keep both on `ramp_coolwarm` provisionally, until REQ-PAY-088 defines `q_mass` and REQ-PAY-073 defines `y`;
    nothing changes now, and the M1 gate records them as provisional.
  - (b) Switch both to viridis now, reading `q_mass` as a non-negative ratio and `mean_y` as the mean of a

    writes REQ-PAY-073 (M3), each task declaring the field's range from its definition and the physics reviewer

- **Applied meanwhile:** the gate check's result is recorded in `plan/MILESTONES.md` § "M1"'s gate item and in R-423;

- **Waits:** the M1 gate's R-423 cool-warm item, for `q_mass` and `mean_y` only: the gate report cannot give either
