## RQ-366: escape body 3's OKLab-uniform swatch `#09B9CD` measures hue 208.54°, 0.54° from its row's 208°, where R-447 and RQ-332 record "each hue within 0.5° of its row" *(measurement against a recorded figure, R-447, R-71, REQ-COL-103, REQ-COL-105; context: TASK-M2-32)*

*Found building TASK-M2-32, measuring with dd_colouring §3.1's transforms (`render::present`). Nothing is chosen; no
swatch is changed. For the human: yes, with RQ-332 (a calibration's evidence, R-71).*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-447 — …", *Measured*: "The swatches as ruled measure L 0.719 to 0.721, C 0.122 to 0.124, and
    each hue within 0.5° of its row."
  - `REVIEW_QUEUE.md` § RQ-332, *Measured*: "each hue within 0.5° of its row".
  - `plan/tasks/M2/TASK-M2-32.md`, the acceptance line `cargo test -p render outcome_oklab_variant`: "each chromatic
    swatch measures OKLab L 0.72 ± 0.002, C 0.123 ± 0.002 and its hue within 0.5° of R-447's".
- **Measured:** `#09B9CD` (escape body 3, R-449's name) is hue 208.54°, 0.54° from the table's 208°; it is 0.04° from
  208.5°, the sRGB class `#30C8DC`'s own hue, which R-447 also records. Every other swatch is within 0.5° of both its
  row and its class's own hue.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Read "R-447's hue" as the class's own hue (R-447: "each chromatic class keeps its own OKLab hue"), which every
    swatch meets; record 0.54° against the row.
  - (b) Take RQ-332's option (c), `#07B9CD`.
- **Applied meanwhile, per R-369:** (a). `crates/render/tests/outcome_view.rs`'s `outcome_oklab_variant_measures_r_447`
  checks each hue within 0.5° of its class's own and pins escape body 3 as the one swatch past 0.5° of its row, at
  0.54°.
- **Waits:** REQ-COL-105's confirmation (RQ-330's gate), with RQ-332.
- **Ruling:** R-455 (17 Oct 2026): option (a), in the human's words "The 0.5° tolerance is below hex quantisation at
  this chroma (one green or blue step is about 0.7°), and by my numbers #07B9CD only moves 0.025°." "R-447's hue" is
  each chromatic class's own OKLab hue, which every swatch meets; `#09B9CD` is recorded at 0.54° from its row and
  stays. Physics review 5558765536 on PR #231 measured (b)'s `#07B9CD` at hue 208.52°, still 0.52° from the row.
  RQ-332 stays open.

## RQ-367: Does any pointer click or drag turn keyboard navigation off and hide the ring, the focus kept? The human recommended it "to be confirmed by Malachy"; it would amend R-450 A3 and R-456, which land a click in navigation mode, and it bears on R-456's blue ring on a clicked text field *(the human's recommendation, unconfirmed; R-460, R-450, R-456; context: TASK-M6-41, TASK-M6-32)*

*Filed by R-460's port (18 Oct 2026). The human marked it "to be confirmed"; nothing is chosen. For the human: it
changes a ruling of the human's (R-450 A3, R-456).*

- **File, section:**
  - The human's message of 18 Oct 2026 (`decisions.md` § "R-460 — …", quoted): "Recommended, to be confirmed by Malachy:
    any pointer click or drag returns to mouse mode and hides the keyboard highlight. That includes clicking off to
    close something. Focus doesn't move, so the next key press resumes keyboard navigation from where it was."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-450 — …", A3: "A click on any console control moves the keyboard's focus onto it, in navigation
    mode, so the mouse and the keyboard never disagree about where the focus is inside the console; a click on ×
    closes the console as Enter on it does."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-456 — …", the human's words: "#234: clicking a text input should start typing; elsewhere a click
    lands in navigation mode." Its first decision: "The keyboard's focus moves onto the field in interaction mode, the
    ring blue". Its B6: a click not on a text input "moves the keyboard's focus only where an existing rule says a click
    does (R-450 A3's console controls)". R-456 is built by TASK-M6-32, PR #234, open.
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G3. Keyboard — a design note, not a screen (`07_keyboard.png`)", R-460's
    paragraph: "A click never turns keyboard navigation on; whether a click or a drag turns it off is open (RQ-367), and
    meanwhile it leaves it as it was."
- **The conflicts:**
  - "Focus doesn't move" against R-450 A3 and R-456 B6, under which a click on a console control does move the focus,
    onto it. "Returns to mouse mode and hides the keyboard highlight" against "lands in navigation mode", which in the
    corpus is the orange ring's mode (R-409). R-460 reads "navigation mode" there as the mode keys find when they
    next act, with no ring unless keyboard navigation is already on; the recommendation would turn it off.
  - R-456's "the ring blue" on a clicked text field against R-460's confirmed "Keyboard-nav mode starts only on keyboard
    input": a click is not keyboard input. R-460 H3 applied the newer words (the caret, no blue ring while keyboard
    navigation is off) and flagged it here.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) As recommended, literally: any click or drag turns keyboard navigation off and hides the ring, clicking off to
    close something included, and moves no keyboard focus, so R-450 A3's and R-456 B6's click-moves-focus are withdrawn;
    a click on a text field still starts typing (R-456), its ring hidden.
  - (b) A click or drag turns keyboard navigation off and hides the ring, but where R-450 A3 and R-456 move the focus on
    a click they still do, so the next key resumes from what was clicked.
  - (c) Not adopted: a click leaves keyboard navigation as it was (R-460 H3, applied meanwhile); with it on, A3 and
    R-456 land with the ring.
  - For the text field, under any of them: (i) its blue ring only while keyboard navigation is on (applied meanwhile),
    or (ii) a click on a text field shows the blue ring while typing, as R-456 says, and turns keyboard navigation on.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (c) and (i) (R-460 H3). TASK-M6-41 builds them; R-456's code on PR #234 is unchanged by this
  entry.
- **Waits:** nothing. TASK-M6-41 does not wait; a ruling for (a) or (b), or (ii), is applied by a follow-up task, or by
  TASK-M6-41 if it is still open.
- **Ruling:** R-464 (18 Oct 2026), in the human's words "Confirm follow-up 3, as a narrow amendment to R-450 A3 and
  R-456": option (b) for the click and the drag. The focus moves exactly as R-450 A3 and R-456 say, and a click on a
  text input still starts typing; after any mouse click or drag the ring is hidden, and the next key press shows it
  again where the focus already is. For the text field, neither (i) nor (ii): the click types with no ring, and the
  first key typed shows it blue. A window or the console opened with the mouse gets the focus with no ring, from the
  keyboard with it. #234 merges as it stands; TASK-M6-41 builds the ring-visibility rule. (c) and (i), applied
  meanwhile, no longer apply.

## RQ-368: The compass's slice gesture moves the slice by the drag's horizontal component along the decoder's normal, which the isometric cube draws toward the lower left, so a drag to the right moves the drawn plane left and down; the tilt gesture's rates are the reference's while the drawn plane is the decoder's. The human reports the gestures unintuitive; changing them changes R-451 *(the human's report against a ruling's transcription, R-451 B3, R-458; context: TASK-M6-33, TASK-M6-39)*

*Filed by R-458's port (18 Oct 2026), which the human asked to confirm that the compass rework covers the tilt and slice
gestures and, if not, to say so rather than patch the old compass. Nothing is chosen; for the human, since it changes a
ruling of the human's (R-451).*

- **File, section:**
  - The human's message of 18 Oct 2026 (`decisions.md` § "R-458 — …", quoted, Message 1 item 5): "The compass's tilt and
    slice interaction is unintuitive as it stands; the slicing gesture in particular doesn't behave as it should. I
    believe the compass rework already queued … replaces this. Confirm it covers the tilt and slice gestures."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-451 — …", B3: "The horizontal component of a drag sets how far the slice moves (the reference's
    rate, a drag across the cube's full width moving it across the whole range); the direction is the drawn plane's
    normal … The vertical component does nothing in slice mode. The tilt-mode drag uses the reference's rates (a drag
    across the full width or height is 180°), right raising τ₁ and up raising τ₂".
  - `docs/gui/reference/principia_nav_cube.html`: the slice drag `depthT += dx*2`, along its z axis; its tilt mode's
    untilted plane horizontal (normal y).
  - PR #240 (TASK-M6-33, open), `crates/gui/src/explore/compass.rs`: the projection's screen x is `ISO·(x − z)`; the slice
    drag adds `dx·2·Z0_HALF_RANGE` along `latent_normal`, the vertical component ignored.
- **The reading:** untilted the decoder's normal is `+e_d`, the cube's z, which the projection draws toward the screen's
  lower left, so the drawn plane moves against the pointer's x; tilted, the normal's screen direction turns while the
  drag still reads `dx`, so the plane can move sideways to the pointer. The reference has the same property (it moves
  its plane along z by `dx`). The code on `main` projects the 2-D drag onto the drawn normal's screen direction, so its
  plane follows the pointer. In tilt mode the drawn plane is the decoder's, vertical when untilted (R-451 B1, B2), not
  the reference's horizontal plane, so the reference's "right raises τ₁, up raises τ₂" need not tip the drawn plane
  toward the pointer. R-451 B4's easing makes the cube trail the pointer by a few tenths of a second.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Keep R-451 B3 as written (the reference's gestures); R-458's live updates are the only change.
  - (b) The slice drag follows the pointer: the 2-D drag projected onto the drawn normal's screen direction, so the
    drawn plane moves with the pointer at every tilt, at R-451's rate along that direction.
  - (c) As (b) for the slice, and the tilt drag tips the drawn plane toward the pointer: horizontal drags turn about
    the screen's vertical through the cube's centre and vertical drags about the screen's horizontal, mapped to `τ₁`,
    `τ₂` so the plane's nearer edge follows the pointer.
  - Independently: keep R-451 B4's easing during a drag, or draw the drag's target at once and ease only after a
    click or a keyboard step.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a). TASK-M6-33 and TASK-M6-39 build R-451's gestures as written.
- **Waits:** nothing. A ruling for (b) or (c) is applied by a follow-up task, or by TASK-M6-39 if it is still open.
- **Ruling:** R-465 (18 Oct 2026), in the human's words "b. because there's two modes slice mode and tilt mode, your
  suggestion c combines tilt mode and slice mode which is wrong": option (b). The slice drag follows the pointer: the
  2-D drag projected onto the drawn normal's screen direction, so the drawn plane moves with the pointer at every tilt,
  at R-451's rate along that direction. Tilt mode is unchanged: R-451 B3's tilt rates stand (right raises τ₁, up raises
  τ₂; the full width or height is 180°). (c) is rejected for the human's reason, slice mode and tilt mode being
  separate modes. The independent question (the easing during a drag) was not answered; R-465 N1 applies per R-369
  that R-451 B4's easing stands, read with R-458's live updates. TASK-M6-39 builds it (R-465 N2). (a), applied
  meanwhile, no longer applies to the slice drag.

## RQ-319: what the Chazy fields read and the Chazy stains draw for a `decode_failed` sample the chart does not hatch, or a reserved `state` code, is not given *(silence, R-442, R-446, REQ-COL-072; context: TASK-M7-37)*

*Found porting the Chazy stain spec (R-442); restated against R-446 (gui review 5555473056 of PR #223). For the
human: no; decided per R-369, option (a), on gui review 5555494498 of PR #223.*

- **File, section:**
  - Spec 02 §4 (drill-down § "7. Validity and styles") gives running, `sim_failed` or NaN, collision-terminated and
    escape-terminated; it names neither `decode_failed` nor a reserved `state` code.
  - `docs/design/principia_colour_composition.md` § "1.4 …" (R-446): "`bounded`, `running` and every failure draw
    `#141418` in the outcome view: … `decode_failed` whatever its `detail` or tag (η_E's `ENERGY_UNSCALABLE` and
    `ENERGY_INFEASIBLE`, the mass block's `M01_TINY`, a non-finite decode), and a reserved `state` code"; "**Forbidden
    chart regions keep the chart contract's forbidden-region treatment** in the outcome view, the hatch
    `debug_invalid(frag_xy)` …, decided from the chart alone, never from the payload's `decode_failed` detail"; "Every
    other `decode_failed` sample draws black." Degenerate's white `#ECECF0` survives only in its "*Was (R-96,
    superseded in part by R-444 to R-446)*" note. § "4.1 …", "What every mode keeps": "a `sim_failed` sample, a
    reserved `state` code or a NaN the invalid pattern", with the outcome class the exception (R-446).
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G6. …": "A free-fall slice under `η_E` is almost all `decode_failed`,
    drawn black, and that is expected (R-441, reworded by R-446 …)": "these are `decode_failed`, drawn black
    `#141418` in the outcome view, the bounded / nothing yet key of the outcome legend"; "*Was (R-441):* drawn
    degenerate's white `#ECECF0`."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-446 …", the human's words: "Forbidden chart regions keep the chart contract's forbidden-region
    treatment in the outcome view, decided from the chart's own validity (types, primary ranges, validate, Φ), not
    from the payload's decode_failed detail, which cannot tell the two hatches apart. Every other decode_failed sample
    draws black." R-446 rules for the outcome view; it names no Chazy stain.
  - `docs/contracts/principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` § "Past the unit square — each axis's extension type
    (R-407)" (Part 3): "A hatched pixel is a labelled output, never dropped (Part 5): it is `decode_failed` (payload
    §2), it is not integrated, and it is drawn with the render contract's hatch (`debug_invalid`, Part 5)."
- **Not open: a pixel the chart hatches.** It keeps the chart's hatch in every Chazy stain under every option below:
  Part 3 draws every pixel it hatches with `debug_invalid`, naming no view, and R-446 keeps that even in the outcome
  view, which draws every other failure black. As in R-446, it is decided from the chart alone (the types,
  `validate`'s layers 2 and 3, a failed `Φ`), never from the payload's `decode_failed` detail. How the chart's
  classification reaches a view is REQ-COL-102's definition, written for the outcome view.
- **Silence:** the decoder's own `decode_failed` samples, the ones the chart does not hatch (its `DEGENERATE` tags,
  `M01_TINY`, `ENERGY_UNSCALABLE`, `ENERGY_INFEASIBLE` and any added later, and a non-finite decode; colour_composition
  §1.4), have no physical state to classify, and nothing says what the Chazy fields read or the Chazy stains draw for
  them.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) The invalid hatch, as for `sim_failed` in the Chazy stains (§7). A failed decode then draws like a forbidden
    region, which the payload's detail cannot tell apart (R-446), and unlike the outcome view, which draws it black;
    the Chazy stains already differ from the outcome view in the same way for `sim_failed`.
  - (b) The outcome view's black `#141418` (colour_composition §1.4; R-446), so a free-fall slice under `η_E` reads
    the same in both systems. Black is Chazy B (drill-down §10.1), labelled "bound, or nobody has left yet" in every
    final-motion legend and in the one-body views (§10.10, §10.11.1, §10.11.4), so a failed decode would sit under
    B's legend key, as the outcome view's failures sit under its bounded / nothing yet key. The status stain's
    palette (§10.6) has no `#141418`, so there it would be a further swatch, its separations unmeasured (§11.2.1).
  - (c) A further value, "no IC", in `chazy_final` and `chazy_status`.
  - A reserved `state` code takes the invalid hatch under every option (colour_composition §4.1), as REQ-COL-072
    requires.
- **Applied meanwhile:** applied per R-369 on PR #223, on the gui reviewer's recommendation (review 5555494498):
  option (a), the invalid hatch. In the Chazy stains every sample with no motion to classify draws the render
  contract's hatch (`debug_invalid`): `sim_failed` (already hatched, drill-down §7), a reserved `state` code, a pixel
  the chart hatches, and the decoder's own `decode_failed` (its `DEGENERATE` tags and a non-finite decode).
  - Why: one rule, fitting render_gui_spec §13 and colour_composition §4.1. Under (b) black would read as Chazy B
    ("bound, or nobody has left yet") in the body lens and the escaper map, and the status stain would need a swatch
    whose separations are unmeasured; (c) would add a field value, a legend entry and a swatch to every palette for
    samples the debug views already explain.
  - Cost: a free-fall slice under `η_E` reads hatched in the Chazy stains but black in the outcome view, the same split
    `sim_failed` already has, and this hatch cannot be told apart from a forbidden-region hatch.
  - Where: drill-down §7 and §10.11 (`docs/design/principia_dd_chazy_final_motion.md`), render_gui_spec §G6's
    free-fall paragraph, REQ-COL-072's note, TASK-M7-37's note. The outcome view's rule (R-446) is unchanged.
- **Waits:** nothing now; this records the decision for the human's veto. Before it, REQ-COL-072's `decode_failed`
  clause (`rq: RQ-319`, removed by this decision) and TASK-M7-37.
- **Ruling:** R-467 (20 Oct 2026), the human naming the Chazy stains among the presentation views: "Non-finite values
  (NaN, Inf), sim_failed and decode_failed likewise never appear as a category in a presentation view. They keep R-446's
  black #141418." Option (b) in the presentation views: in every Chazy stain a `sim_failed` or `decode_failed` sample, a
  reserved `state` code and a NaN draw B's `#141418`, and a pixel the chart classifies forbidden draws the canvas
  background, not the hatch; View › Debug view shows them (R-467 D5, F4). The decision applied meanwhile, option (a),
  the hatch, no longer applies.

## RQ-340: The compass's tilt: the code and the human's nav-cube reference both tilt the plane within one 3-space toward one shared hidden axis, but map (τ₁, τ₂) to planes differently, and the contract's two-target form is a different family; which plane a tilt means is not settled *(conflict, physics, R-451; context: TASK-M6-33)*

*Found by R-451's port (16 Oct 2026), which the human asked to compare the three; restated in the fix round of PR #227
after the physics review (P1 to P3) found the first comparison misread the reference. Nothing is chosen; the decoder's
geometry is not changed, and the compass draws the decoder's mapping. For the human (physics: it changes which plane a
tilt means).*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` § "Tilt (basis edit)": "`q'(τ) = normalise( cos τ · q + sin τ ·
    d_target )        τ ∈ [−90°, +90°]`"; "The 8D has **6 directions orthogonal** to the current plane; the tilt selector
    offers those". `docs/design/principia_chart_reference.md` §1.1: "A 2-plane in 8D has 12 tilt axes (6 hidden
    dimensions × 2 basis vectors). Raw tilts **replace** rather than compose", and "**Arbitrary / oblique:** `q_1, q_2`
    any orthonormal pair in ℝ⁸".
  - The code, `crates/gui/src/explore/manifold_view/mod.rs`, `View::basis`: `u₁ = cos τ₁ e_a + sin τ₁ e_d`,
    `u₂ = cos τ₂ e_b + sin τ₂ e_d` (one hidden axis `e_d`, `slice_axis`), then `q₁ = σ(cos γ u₁ + sin γ u₂)`,
    `q₂ = σ(cos γ u₂ − sin γ u₁)`, with no normalisation and no orthogonalisation; `crates/gui/src/explore/compass.rs`,
    `plane_frame`, draws the same in the frame `(e_a, e_b, e_d)`.
  - `docs/gui/reference/principia_nav_cube.html`, `planeSpec`: "axis-angle ("joystick") map — rotate the up-normal by
    angle m toward (τ₁,0,τ₂) … no gimbal lock (the naïve Euler form freezes q₂ at τ₁=±90°)", `n = (sin m · τ₁/m, cos m,
    sin m · τ₂/m)`, `m = √(τ₁² + τ₂²)`; tilt mode's untilted plane is the horizontal x–z plane (`n = [0,1,0]`), its
    labels "q₁'s target" on x and "q₂'s target" on z, and its help text: "no single coordinate perpendicular to both
    targets".
- **The reading:**
  - **The code and the reference are one family.** The reference tips its plane's normal y toward x and z, so both
    tilts tip the plane into the one unnamed vertical axis y; its "targets" x and z are the plane's own untilted in-plane
    axes. Read geometrically, the code and the reference both give the 2-planes in one 3-space through the slice point
    (the code's `(e_a, e_b, e_d)`, `e_d` hidden; the reference's (x, z, y), y hidden). They differ only in which
    `(τ₁, τ₂)` gives which plane.
  - **The code's map.** `u₁ · u₂ = sin τ₁ sin τ₂`, so with both tilts set the basis is not orthogonal, against
    chart_reference §1.1's "any orthonormal pair", and the window's shape changes with it. Its normal is
    `∝ (−tan τ₁, −tan τ₂, 1)`, one-to-one on the open square. At `|τ₁| = 90°`, `u₁ = ±e_d`, so the plane is
    `span(e_b, e_d)` for every `|τ₂| < 90°`: τ₂ stops moving it along that whole edge, the gimbal lock the reference's
    comment names (the physics review: the normal stays (−1, 0, 0) for τ₂ ∈ {0, 0.3, 1.0, 1.5}). At `τ₁ = ±τ₂ = ±90°`
    the plane degenerates to the line `e_d`.
  - **The reference's map.** It never degenerates inside ±90° (`m ≤ 127.3° < 180°`), but it is not one-to-one there:
    `n` and `−n` are one plane, so `(τ₁, τ₂) = (90°, 90°)`, `m = 127.3°`, gives the plane `(−37.3°, −37.3°)` gives.
    This bears on the contract's "sign matters".
  - **The contract's two-target form is the different family.** With distinct targets `t₁ ⊥ t₂`, both orthogonal to
    the plane, the two tilted vectors are already orthonormal, and the plane lies in the 4-space
    `span(q₁, q₂, t₁, t₂)`, with no single normal: no 3D cube draws it faithfully. With one shared target the contract's
    formula is the code's (`u₁` is unit, so `normalise` does nothing): the code is the contract's form with
    `d_target₁ = d_target₂ = e_d`.
- **The questions:** (i) within one 3-space, which parametrisation of the tilt pair: the code's or the reference's;
  (ii) one shared target (the code and the reference) or two distinct targets (the contract's general form).
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) One shared hidden axis, the code's map (as applied): the contract states it as the tilt pair's meaning, with
    its non-orthogonal basis against chart_reference §1.1 and its lock at `|τ₁| = 90°`; the compass draws it.
  - (b) One shared hidden axis, the reference's axis-angle map: the reference's x, z and y read as `e_a`, `e_b` and
    `e_d` (its untilted plane `span(e_a, e_b)`, its hidden axis `e_d`), the sign chosen so a single τ₁ moves q₁ toward
    `e_d` as the code's does, and `q₁`, `q₂` the untilted `e_a`, `e_b` turned by the rotation taking `e_d` to `n`, then
    by γ: orthonormal and without a lock, but one-to-one only for `m < 90°`: from the `m = 90°` circle on, `n` and `−n`
    give one plane, so `(90°, 0)` and `(−90°, 0)` already give the same one. Like (a), a one-hidden-axis map.
  - (c) Two distinct targets, the contract's general form: each of `q₁`, `q₂` tilted toward its own hidden direction,
    `t₁ ⊥ t₂` (orthonormal with no further step); the plane leaves any one 3-space, so the ruling also says what the
    compass's cube draws, and its tilt labels name the two targets.
- **Applied meanwhile:** the compass's cube draws the decoder's mapping (`View::basis` in the cube's frame), and its
  tilt mode names the one shared target (R-451 B1).
- **The degenerate tilts hang on it** (fix round 4 of PR #227, physics review P13): under the decoder's map, option
  (a), `q₁` and `q₂` span no plane at `τ₁ = ±τ₂ = ±90°`, and there the compass's cube draws no slice and its readout
  cites this entry (R-451 B11). Under (b) or (c) those tilts give a plane, so a ruling for either revisits what the
  cube draws there.
- **Waits:** nothing builds on the choice yet; TASK-M6-33 does not wait, and no requirement carries `rq: RQ-340`
  (REQ-GUI-192 cites it in its note). A ruling for (b) or (c) changes the decoder's basis, which re-integrates every
  tilted view, and goes to a follow-up task.
- **R-457 bears on it (18 Oct 2026, a note; the entry stays open).** The human ruled that each tilt angle tilts into a
  latent dimension the user picks, "tilt τ₁ into [X]" and "tilt τ₂ into [Y]", the defaults today's one shared target.
  That answers question (ii) in part: both the one-target map (`X = Y`) and the contract's two-target form (`X ≠ Y`,
  option (c)'s family, orthonormal) are reachable, the shared target the default. Question (i), the parametrisation
  within one 3-space when `X = Y` (options (a) and (b)), stays open, and with it R-451 B11's corner points, which arise
  only with `X = Y`. R-459 says what the cube draws with `X ≠ Y` (each gesture exactly, and a "4D: see Plücker"
  badge). TASK-M6-38 builds the targets with the code's map for `X = Y` (option (a), as applied).
- **Ruling:** R-474 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "make B the default, but then have option A … as an optional
  option"; "carry on, can just wrap the values internally." Question (i): option (b), "Tip as sheet", the reference's
  axis-angle map, is the default, and option (a), "Lean each axis", the code's map, is an option, the choice recorded
  as `tilt_map`; Tip as sheet carries on over the pole, each angle over ±180°, wrapped internally only in total tip at
  360°. Question (ii) was answered by R-457. The compass draws the active map, and R-451 B11 holds under Lean each axis
  only. What was applied meanwhile, option (a) for `X = Y`, is now the Lean option; TASK-M6-48 builds the ruling.

## RQ-374: R-467 says presentation views "never draw the hatch", and Overlays ▾ keeps two toggles, "forbidden region" and "NaN / invalid pixels", that draw it over the presentation figure *(conflict, R-467 clauses 1 and 6, R-26, REQ-GUI-079; context: TASK-M8-08)*

*Filed on gui review 5585209592 of PR #253 (G5): R-467 D9, as first ported, kept both toggles per R-369, which goes
beyond the human's words. For the human: yes, because option (b) drops part of a requirement.*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-467 …", the human's words: "1. Presentation views (outcome, Chazy, the stains, F3 full-window)
    never draw the hatch." and "6. The Chart builder's Domain preview "forbidden in view: N%" (R-408, §G7) stays: it is
    a diagnostic for building charts, not a presentation view." Clause 6 names only the Domain preview.
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G2. Explore — the everyday view (`01_main.png`)": "- Chart: forbidden
    region (the chart's domain function, R-26), grid, Burrau lattice (primitive triples)." and the Integration group's
    "NaN / invalid pixels"; R-467's sentence: "The Chart group's "forbidden region" and the Integration group's "NaN /
    invalid pixels" are diagnostics the person turns on, off by default, beside View › Debug view".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-GUI-079: "Overlays ▾ must hold the grouped toggles — … Integration (substep density,
    substep-cap hits, NaN / invalid pixels, energy drift), Chart (forbidden region from the chart's domain function,
    grid, Burrau lattice) …".
- **The conflict:** the two toggles draw the hatch (or, with the debug view built, its styles) over a presentation
  figure on request. Clause 1 says a presentation view never draws the hatch; clause 3 asks for "one switchable view"
  that shows these categories; the words name neither toggle.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Keep both, off by default, drawing the debug view's styles over the figure: diagnostics turned on by the person,
    as the Domain preview is (R-467 D9 as first ported).
  - (b) Drop both from Overlays ▾, View › Debug view being the one switchable view. This retires part of REQ-GUI-079,
    the human's call.
  - (c) Keep both, enabled only while View › Debug view is on, as filters of what it shows.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a). Nothing else waits: TASK-M6-46 and TASK-M6-47 build no Overlays entry.
- **Waits:** TASK-M8-08's two lines for these toggles (REQ-GUI-079's "NaN / invalid pixels" and "forbidden region");
  the rest of TASK-M8-08 proceeds.
- **Ruling:** R-470 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "the hatching's optional, but I don't think it should be part of
  the color palette" and, in N2 and N3, "The hatch survives as an **optional overlay**: the existing Overlays › Chart ›
  forbidden region toggle, off by default. It draws over blank pixels only." and "NaN, Inf, `sim_failed`,
  `decode_failed`, and the classification behind the hatch are shown **only in the debug views**". For "forbidden
  region", option (a), off by default, drawing the hatch over exactly the pixels the chart classifies as not a system
  and nothing else. For "NaN / invalid pixels", N3 reads as option (c), applied per R-369 (R-470 G3): it draws only
  while a debug view is the figure, View › Debug view on or a debug-catalogue stain, and is dimmed with a tooltip
  otherwise. TASK-M8-08's two lines no longer wait.

## RQ-372: R-467 says every chart is valid inside `[0,1]²`, "charts built in the Chart builder too", and makes an in-square validity failure a decoder bug with a console warning; a builder physical-quantity axis has a range that can leave the slice's reach inside the square, which the Domain preview exists to show *(conflict, R-467, R-407, R-408 B1, R-26, REQ-GUI-109, REQ-GUI-228; context: TASK-M8-18, TASK-M8-47)*

*Found porting R-467 (F2). Nothing is chosen. For the human: yes, it changes what the Chart builder must build.*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-467 — …", the human's words: "Inside [0,1]², every chart is valid by construction: the
    feasibility warp, the simplex map, and the decode accepting any z. That holds for charts built in the Chart builder
    too." and clause 4: "Inside [0,1]², a validity failure is a decoder bug: debug view only, plus a console warning
    naming the chart and pixel. The real engine should produce zero."; clause 6: "The Chart builder's Domain preview
    "forbidden in view: N%" (R-408, §G7) stays: it is a diagnostic for building charts, not a presentation view."
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G7. Chart builder (`03_chartbuilder.png`)": "physical quantity (energy,
    `L_z`, virial ratio, mass ratio…, with a range and what is **held fixed** …)"; "a physical-quantity axis mapped
    linearly onto its range is affine, the fallback hatching what fails validation"; "**The Domain preview:** the
    chart's admissible region in its own coordinates, the forbidden region hatched, the current view as a rectangle, the
    boundary's formula, and "forbidden in view: N%"."
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-GUI-109, verify detail: "N% equals the fraction of a uniform grid over the view that
    validate rejects"; its note: "With the egui layer shown the Chart builder's view is [0,1]²".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-408 — …", B1: "Under this ruling its primary range is unbounded, so its unreachable pixels fail
    validity and count as forbidden."
- **Conflict:** nothing in the corpus makes a builder axis's range feasible on the slice. An energy axis whose range
  reaches below the slice's potential `U` fails `validate`'s layer 2 at those pixels inside `[0,1]²`, and the Domain
  preview's N% over `[0,1]²` is non-zero exactly then. Under clause 4 each such chart would log a "decoder bug" warning
  for a chart the person built that way, while the context says such a chart cannot exist.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) The builder keeps every chart valid inside the square: a physical-quantity axis's range is clamped or warped to
    the slice's reach (as the invariant charts' warp is, chart_reference §2.1), so N% is 0 inside `[0,1]²` and a
    failure there is a decoder bug, warned; the Domain preview then shows forbidden regions only past the square or
    before Apply.
  - (b) A builder chart may be invalid inside the square: its in-square failures draw the background (presentation)
    and the hatch (debug view), as R-467 settles for every chart, but log no decoder-bug warning; the warning is for the
    gallery charts (R-467 D11's list) only.
  - (c) As (b), with a different, informational console line for a builder chart ("chart <name>: N% of the view is
    outside its domain").
- **Applied meanwhile:** R-467's drawing applies to every chart (R-467 D3, F6); the warning is built for the gallery
  charts and the mock (REQ-GUI-227, TASK-M8-46; REQ-GUI-226, TASK-M6-46). Nothing in the builder changes.
- **Waits:** REQ-GUI-228 (`rq: RQ-372`), a Chart-builder chart's in-square warning, closed by TASK-M8-47, a leaf task
  nothing depends on.
- **R-471 bears on it (20 Oct 2026, a note; the entry stays open, narrowed).** The human ruled that energy-type axes
  are warped by default, every pixel of `[0,1]²` feasible, with a linear mapping as an option whose infeasible pixels
  are "not a system" (E1, E2). For an energy-type builder axis (energy, `L_z`) that is option (a) under the default and
  option (b) under the linear option: no decoder-bug warning for its in-square pixels (R-471 H5). The question stays
  open for the builder's other physical quantities, the virial ratio and the mass ratio, for which nothing is ruled;
  REQ-GUI-228 waits for those alone.
- **Ruling:** R-506 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "every physical-quantity axis follows R-471: warped to its
  feasible range by default, linear as an option with non-systems blank. The decoder-bug warning only for a warped
  axis. Mass ratio warps through the mass link." Option (a) for a warped axis and (b) for a linear one, on every
  physical-quantity axis, the virial ratio and the mass ratio included; a builder chart warns only when its
  physical-quantity axes are all warped (R-506 W1). The warps not yet written are REQ-CHART-070's definition.
  REQ-GUI-228 no longer waits.

## RQ-375: T4's units: mixing a chart parameter with a hidden coordinate under a tilt needs a common scale; the human proposes each screen axis in units of the window's half-width and the hidden coordinate in units of its own slider span, and on the latent chart that proposal departs from T2's "exactly the decoder's basis edit" *(the human's queued question, physics, R-473 T4, R-469 §11; context: TASK-M2-36, TASK-M2-39)*

*Queued by the human (R-469 §11: "T4's units for mixing a chart parameter with a hidden coordinate"; R-473 T4: "Queue
it as an RQ with this as the proposal; do not settle it silently"). For the human: yes (physics, ⚑).*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-473 — …", the human's words: "**T4. Units.** Mixing a chart parameter with a hidden coordinate
    needs a common scale. Proposed: each screen axis in units of the window half-width; the hidden coordinate in units
    of its own slider span. This makes `τ = 45°` mean the same visual lean on every chart."
  - The same, T2: "A pixel at window offset `(u, v)` maps to `(a, b, d) = R(τ₁, τ₂)·(u, v, 0)`" and "On the latent
    chart, T2 is exactly the decoder's basis edit."
  - `docs/contracts/principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` § "Tilt (basis edit)" ("Tilt on every chart (R-473)"): the
    rotation acts on the chart's own parameters, the scale queued here.
- **The question:** in what units `a`, `b` (the chart's own parameters, in their axes' units) and `d` (a hidden latent
  control, in its own) are compared, so that a rotation between them means something. The latent chart's basis edit
  compares `q₁`, `q₂` and a unit latent direction in the latent metric; under the proposal the hidden coordinate is
  scaled by its slider span, which on the latent chart changes what a given `τ` does there.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) The proposal on every chart: each screen axis in half-widths of the window, the hidden coordinate in its slider
    span. `τ = 45°` is the same visual lean everywhere; on the latent chart the basis edit is no longer the contract's
    unit-vector form.
  - (b) The proposal on curved charts only, the latent chart keeping the decoder's basis edit exactly (its own units,
    the latent metric), so T2's latent sentence holds as written.
  - (c) One metric for all charts, the latent metric pulled back through each chart map (needs the decoder's Jacobian
    and a metric, which RQ-341 and RQ-346 raise).
- **Applied meanwhile:** (b), per R-369 (R-473 J3): the proposal on the curved charts, the latent chart unchanged.
- **Waits:** REQ-CHART-064 (`rq: RQ-375`), the units' requirement, closed by TASK-M2-39, a leaf task nothing depends
  on. The tilt itself (TASK-M2-36) is built under (b).
- **Ruling:** R-507 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "my T4 proposal is withdrawn. Units must not depend on zoom:
  the screen axis in units of the axis's full range at zoom 1, the hidden coordinate in its slider span. Latent chart
  stays the decoder's basis edit." None of the three options as written: the proposal is withdrawn; on a curved chart
  each screen axis is in units of its full range at zoom 1, the hidden coordinate in its slider span, and the latent
  chart keeps the basis edit, as (b) had it there. REQ-CHART-064 no longer waits (it carried no `rq:` key, though this
  entry said so: code review 5586921473 of PR #255, F1) and TASK-M2-36 closes it; (b), applied meanwhile, no longer
  applies.

## RQ-376: Whether an energy chart's `t > 1` side (`K > K_max`, feasible) should be declared affine rather than bounded *(the human's queued question, physics, R-469 §11, R-407 clause 3, R-471; context: TASK-M2-37)*

*Queued by the human (R-469 §11: "Whether an energy chart's `t > 1` side (`K > K_max`, feasible) should be declared
affine rather than bounded. chart_reference §2.1 leaves this for physics review."). For the human: yes (physics).*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_chart_reference.md` § "2.1 Feasibility, and the warp that makes every pixel valid": "Its
    `t > 1` side (`K > K_max`) stays feasible; declaring it would be a later declaration, needing physics review
    (R-407, clause 3), and is not made."
  - The same, § "5.4 …": "Its `t > 1` side (`K > K_max`, feasible at `L_z = 0`) is left undeclared, as on the warp
    (R-407, A5)."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-471 — …": energy-type axes are warped by default, so every energy-type axis has this side.
- **The question:** past `t = 1` the warp's `K = K_max·t^γ_K` is still feasible (`K > K_max ≥ 0`), so the axis could
  continue as affine there, while past `t = 0` it cannot. Bounded draws that side blank (R-470) and leaves it out of the
  count (R-408).
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Bounded on both sides, as today: past `t > 1` the domain's end, blank.
  - (b) Affine past `t = 1`, bounded past `t = 0`: a one-sided declaration, `K` continuing beyond `K_max` (for `E` and
    `L_z` the same through their lines of the warp, R-471 H1), counted as R-408 counts an affine axis.
  - (c) Affine past `t = 1` for `K` and `E` only, `L_z`'s `±√(2 I K_max)` range staying bounded.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a), the corpus as written. Nothing waits; a ruling for (b) or (c) is a follow-up change to
  REQ-CHART-067's extension type and REQ-CHART-056's statistic.
- **Ruling:** R-508 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "(b). t > 1 continues, t < 0 and the s axis bounded." Option
  (b) on the warp's `t` axes (`K`, `KE`, `E`, the Burrau family's `K`): past `t = 1` an affine side, counted; past
  `t < 0` bounded; `L_z`'s `s` axis bounded on both sides. The physics review of PR #255 (5586890917, F6) found (b)'s
  "for `E` and `L_z` the same" and (c)'s `L_z` range muddled, an `L_z` axis having edges only at `s ∉ [0,1]`, always
  infeasible; the human's words settle the choice as stated. (a), applied meanwhile, no longer applies.

## RQ-377: `principia_lines.md` §11's six open questions: the expression language, the playhead quantities, the trace fade's off-plane distance, label collisions, class edges, and new contour sets' default style *(the human's queued questions, R-476 L5, R-469 §11; context: TASK-M7-42, TASK-M7-43, TASK-M8-49, TASK-M8-50, TASK-M8-51)*

*Queued by the human (R-469 §11: "`principia_lines.md` §11's six questions"; R-476 L5: "Queue them as RQ entries; do
not rule on them"). One entry with six parts, one per question (R-476 M10). For the human: yes, each part, as each
changes what the line system builds.*

- **File, section:** `docs/design/principia_lines.md` § "11. Open questions", quoted in each part below; read with
  § "4.1 The expression", § "7. Labels" and § "8. Traces".
- **(1) Expression language.** "A purpose-built mini-language (proposed; total, sandboxed, GPU-compilable), or reuse the
  stain graph's Code view syntax?"
  - Options: (a) the purpose-built language (§4.1, proposed); (b) the stain graph's Code view syntax.
  - Applied meanwhile: contour expressions are held as §4.1's tree (its variables, operators, functions and totality),
    built and tested without a surface syntax; the contour-set editor picks a variable or one of §4.1's examples from a
    list.
  - Waits: REQ-RENDER-097's typed expression (the parser and its error reporting), closed by TASK-M8-51.
- **(2) Playhead quantities.** "Allow any payload field, or a curated list?"
  - Options: (a) any payload field; (b) a curated list.
  - Applied meanwhile: the fields §4.1's table names (`t_end`, `state`, `d_min`, `drift`, `ftle`).
  - Waits: REQ-RENDER-097's playhead clause, closed by TASK-M8-51.
- **(3) Off-plane distance for the trace fade.** "The general definition is the distance in z from the trajectory's
  encoded point to the slice plane, which needs the encode at every step (and the metric RQ-341 raises). The mock-up uses
  a proxy: the scale-normalised Jacobi momentum magnitude, which this shape chart holds at zero."
  - Options: (a) the general definition, the encode per step and a metric (RQ-341, RQ-346); (b) the mock-ups' proxy;
    (c) a per-chart distance, each chart naming its own.
  - Applied meanwhile: no fade; every trace draws at full opacity (`fade: None`).
  - Waits: REQ-GUI-249 (`rq: RQ-377`), closed by TASK-M8-51.
- **(4) Label collisions.** "Between contour labels and the trace's tick labels: which wins?"
  - Options: (a) contour labels win; (b) the trace's tick labels win; (c) the later in the Lines list wins, as §7 orders
    contour sets.
  - Applied meanwhile: §7's rule within the contour sets; between contour labels and a trace's ticks nothing is
    suppressed.
  - Waits: REQ-RENDER-098 (`rq: RQ-377`), closed by TASK-M8-51.
- **(5) Class edges.** "Should they move onto the line system as "contours of a categorical field", or stay in the
  stain?"
  - Options: (a) move them onto the line system; (b) keep them in the stain, an Overlays ▾ toggle.
  - Applied meanwhile: (b), as today (R-476 M3 keeps class edges in Overlays ▾). Nothing waits.
- **(6) Default style for new contour sets.** "Screen contour (white with outline), or smart ink on by default?"
  - Options: (a) Screen contour; (b) smart ink on.
  - Applied meanwhile: (a), Screen contour, the library's first style (§3).
  - Waits: REQ-GUI-250 (`rq: RQ-377`), the default's confirmation, closed by TASK-M8-51.
- **Waits, in all:** REQ-RENDER-097, REQ-RENDER-098, REQ-GUI-249 and REQ-GUI-250, each closed by TASK-M8-51, a leaf
  task nothing depends on; the rest of the line system is built meanwhile.
- **Ruling:** R-509 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "1 reuse the stain's expression syntax; 2 any ledger-declared
  payload field; 3 proxy where defined, else no fade and say so, the real distance waits on RQ-341; 4 trace labels
  win; 5 class edges move to the line system; 6 Screen contour default, smart ink opt-in." (1) option (b); (2) option
  (a), the fields the generation root's ledger declares; (3) option (b) where the chart defines the proxy, no fade
  elsewhere, said so, and the general definition, option (a), waiting on RQ-341 (REQ-GUI-266, `rq: RQ-341`,
  TASK-M8-54); (4) option (b); (5) option (a), class edges a Lines preset; (6) option (a), smart ink opt-in. The
  requirements this entry said wait carried no `rq:` key (code review 5586921473 of PR #255, F1); none waits on it
  now, and TASK-M8-51 no longer waits.

## RQ-379: A `decode_failed` sample the chart does not classify as not a system (a non-finite decode, η_E's `ENERGY_UNSCALABLE` and `ENERGY_INFEASIBLE`, the mass block's `M01_TINY`): blank, as P1 suggests, or `#141418`, as R-467 clause 2 has it *(conflict, R-469 P1, R-470 N1 and N3, R-467 clause 2, REQ-COL-108; context: TASK-M2-34)*

*Found porting R-470 (G2). For the human: yes, it changes what a presentation view draws.*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-469 — …", the human's words, P1: "Every pixel a chart draws is either a valid three-body system
    or explicitly "not a system"."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-470 — …", N1: "A pixel that is not a system draws **blank** … This covers: a bounded axis past
    its edge; layer-2 infeasibility; a failed `Φ`; a layer-3 failure." and N3: "NaN, Inf, `sim_failed`,
    `decode_failed`, and the classification behind the hatch are shown **only in the debug views**".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-467 — …", the human's words, clause 2: "Non-finite values (NaN, Inf), sim_failed and
    decode_failed likewise never appear as a category in a presentation view. They keep R-446's black #141418."
  - `docs/design/principia_colour_composition.md` § "1.4 …": "the decoder's own outcomes, its `DEGENERATE` tags … and a
    non-finite decode, never mark a pixel forbidden; such a pixel draws black unless the types or `validate` hatch it".
- **The conflict:** such a sample has no decoded state to integrate, so it is no system, which P1 makes "explicitly
  not a system", blank; N1's list does not name it, and R-467 clause 2 draws `decode_failed` `#141418`, which N3's
  "shown only in the debug views" is consistent with (black is not a category).
- **Options seen:** (a) `#141418`, R-467 D4 as it stands; (b) blank, as a not-a-system pixel, the forbidden-region
  overlay then drawing over it too; (c) blank for the decoder's `DEGENERATE` tags, `#141418` for a non-finite decode.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a), per R-369 (R-470 G2). Nothing waits; a ruling for (b) or (c) is a follow-up change to
  REQ-COL-108 and REQ-COL-111.
- **Ruling:** R-511 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "(b), blank. sim_failed keeps #141418." Option (b): a
  `decode_failed` sample the chart does not classify draws blank in a presentation view, as a not-a-system pixel,
  whatever its tag, and the forbidden-region overlay draws over it; `sim_failed` keeps `#141418`. REQ-COL-108 and
  REQ-COL-111 follow; (a), applied meanwhile, no longer applies.

## RQ-402: Under Tip as sheet with one target and both angles set, the tilt sends window pixels inside `[−1, 1]²` to chart parameters `(a, b)` outside the chart's square, so on a bounded warped axis E3's "every pixel inside `[0,1]²` feasible under every tilt" fails there *(physics review, R-471 E3, R-473 T2, R-474 K1, R-472 X3a, R-477 C6; context: TASK-M2-36, TASK-M2-38, TASK-M2-39)*

*Found by the physics review of PR #255 (5586890917, F1). Nothing is chosen. For the human: yes (physics), as it
changes what the default view draws.*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-471 — …", the human's words, E3: "Per-pixel evaluation keeps every pixel feasible under every
    tilt."; § "R-477 — …", C6: "Under warped energy axes, every pixel inside `[0,1]²` validates under every tilt (E3)."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-473 — …", T2: "A pixel at window offset `(u, v)` maps to `(a, b, d) = R(τ₁, τ₂)·(u, v, 0)`";
    § "R-474 — …", R1: "Rotate the plane rigidly by angle `m` about the in-plane axis perpendicular to `(τ₁, τ₂)`
    (Rodrigues' formula)".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-472 — …", X3a: "past the square `(u, v)` leaves `[−1, 1]²`, and so may the chart's own parameters
    `a`, `b`, on which the chart's axis types act".
  - `docs/design/principia_chart_reference.md` § "2.1 Feasibility, and the warp that makes every pixel valid":
    "evaluated per pixel, every pixel stays feasible under every tilt (R-471, E3; R-473, T5)".
- **The conflict:** with `X = Y` and both angles non-zero, `R`'s in-plane block is `cos m·I + (1 − cos m)·k kᵀ`, which is
  not diagonal, so window pixels inside `[−1, 1]²` map to `(a, b)` outside the chart's square. The review measured the
  largest `|a|`, `|b|` over a 65 × 65 window grid: 1.0037 at (10°, 4.14°), 1.032 at (30°, 12.4°), 1.183 at (−45°,
  77.9°), exactly 1 only on the diagonal. The per-pixel warp keeps a pixel feasible only where its own `(a, b)` lie in
  the square; past it a bounded warped axis's pixels are past the edge, not a system, so they draw blank inside the
  window under the default map. Lean each axis and `X ≠ Y` keep `(a, b)` in the square.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) E3 and C6 read over the chart's own square: every pixel whose `(a, b)` lie in `[0,1]²` is feasible; the others
    are past the edge (X3a) and draw blank, as the types say.
  - (b) The tilt keeps every window pixel in the chart's square: under Tip as sheet the window's parameters are scaled
    down so the rotated window's `(a, b)` stay within `[−1, 1]²` (the scale from the rotated corners).
  - (c) On a chart with a bounded axis, with one target and both angles set, Lean each axis's map is used, or Tip as
    sheet is not offered.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a), the types acting as X3a says (R-471 H9); REQ-CHART-061's and REQ-CHART-069's every-pixel
  arms are narrowed to the pixels whose `(a, b)` lie in the square.
- **Waits:** REQ-CHART-076 (`rq: RQ-402`), what such a pixel draws, closed by TASK-M2-39, a leaf task.
- **Ruling:** R-512 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "RQ-402 (a): pixels whose (a,b) leave the square are past
  the edge and blank; E3/C6 read over the square." Option (a): E3 and C6 read over the chart's square; a window pixel
  whose own `(a, b)` leave it is past the edge, its axes' types acting as X3a says, blank past a bounded edge (R-512 F1:
  past the warp's continuing `t > 1` side, R-508 V2 and V5, it is that side's system, as option (a)'s "as the types
  say" settles; *was* "flagged:"). REQ-CHART-076 loses its `rq`;
  TASK-M2-39 no longer waits.

## RQ-403: Tilting a saved curved chart into its own saved target: whether the new offset `d′` and the saved `d` add, or the saved rotation acts on `(u′, v′, d′)` *(physics review, R-475 S1 and L5, R-473 J9; context: TASK-M2-40, TASK-M2-39, TASK-M8-48)*

*Found by the physics review of PR #255 (5586890917, F3). Nothing is chosen. For the human: yes (physics).*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-475 — …", the human's words, S1: "With it, tilts compose, and any plane is reachable by
    navigating."; S2: on a curved chart the saved chart is "the parent chart, its slice centre, and the tilt rotation
    `R` with its targets and map".
  - The same, L5 (applied per R-369): "a new tilt `R′` acts first and the saved `R` after: `(u′, v′, d′) = R′·(u, v,
    0)`, then `(a, b, d) = R·(u′, v′, 0)`, `d′` added to the new target and `d` to the saved one".
- **The question:** for a new target other than the saved one, L5 is the block composition `(R ⊕ 1)·(1 ⊕ R′)` in
  `(a, b, d_saved, d_new)`, which REQ-CHART-073 now tests. With the new target the saved one, the two offsets land on
  one coordinate, and the words do not say whether they add (L5 read literally) or whether the saved `R` acts on
  `(u′, v′, d′)`, the 3 × 3 product `R·R′`, a true composition of the two rotations in that 3-space.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) L5 literally: `(a, b, d) = R·(u′, v′, 0)`, the hidden offset `d + d′`.
  - (b) The rotations compose: `(a, b, d) = R·(u′, v′, d′)`, that is `R·R′·(u, v, 0)`.
  - (c) The saved target is not offered to a new tilt.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (c), a saved curved chart's target dropdowns not offering its saved target (R-475 L5).
- **Waits:** REQ-CHART-077 (`rq: RQ-403`), closed by TASK-M2-39, a leaf task.
- **Ruling:** R-513 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "RQ-403 (b): rotations compose, (a,b,d) = R·R′·(u,v,0)."
  Option (b): the saved `R` acts on `(u′, v′, d′)`; the saved target is offered again, and (c), applied meanwhile, no
  longer applies. REQ-CHART-077 loses its `rq` and moves to TASK-M2-40 (R-513 Q4).

## RQ-404: The tilt of a latent view saved at a tilt: at most five named dimensions (four with two targets) are orthogonal to its plane, the tilt target is not off it, and under Lean each axis the saved pair is not orthonormal, so R-475 L4's "the six dimensions off the saved plane" and both maps' formulas do not apply as written *(gui review, tilt maths, R-475 S1 and L4, R-457, R-474 K1, R-477 C5; context: TASK-M6-49, TASK-M6-51)*

*Found by the gui review of PR #255 (5586902228, G3). Nothing is chosen. For the human: yes (physics: tilt maths).*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-475 — …", the human's words, S1: "It commits the current view as a named custom chart, which
    can then be tilted, sliced and saved like any other."; S2: "On a latent view: `(z₀, q₁, q₂)` as an oblique chart".
  - The same, L4 (applied per R-369): "its tilt zero and its targets the six dimensions off the saved plane (J1,
    R-473): R-457's targets read against the new plane's own axes"; "under Lean each axis the pair may not be
    orthogonal, and is reported so".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-474 — …", K1: Tip as sheet's Rodrigues rotation "in the 3-space `(e_a, e_b, e_X)`"; R1's Lean
    each axis, `u₁ = cos τ₁ e_a + sin τ₁ e_d`.
- **The question:** a plane saved at a tilt spans a mix of `e_a`, `e_b` and the target `e_X`. At most five named
  dimensions (four with two targets, the plane then meeting span(e_a, e_b, e_X, e_Y)) are orthogonal to it, and `e_X` is not off it. If `e_X` is offered again, Tip as sheet's rotation runs in
  the non-orthonormal frame `(q₁, q₂, e_X)`, and Lean's `u₁ = cos τ q̂₁ + sin τ e_X` is not a unit vector. Under Lean the
  saved pair is not orthogonal even before that, so C5's orthonormality does not hold on such a chart. Which targets
  the dropdowns list, and how each map acts on a non-orthonormal saved pair, the words do not say.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) The named dimensions other than the plane's own, each projected off the saved plane and normalised
    (Gram–Schmidt), any that vanish dropped; each map acts in the orthonormal frame `(q̂₁, q̂₂⊥, t̂)`, the stored
    lengths re-applied.
  - (b) Only the named dimensions exactly orthogonal to the plane (five for a plane saved at a single tilt), the maps
    acting on the pair as stored.
  - (c) Saving re-expresses the plane in an orthonormal frame of its own, with six orthonormal complement directions as
    its targets, named as such.
- **Applied meanwhile:** a saved latent chart whose plane is spanned by two named dimensions (saved untilted) takes the
  six off it, exactly as L4 says; one saved at a tilt is sliced, saved again and reopened bitwise, and its Tilt section
  is dimmed, with a tooltip naming this entry (R-475 L4). The compass's tilt mode is refused on it too: a tilt-mode
  drag and the tilt arrows emit no edit, and the status line says "Tilt waits on RQ-404 for a chart saved at a tilt";
  the dimmed controls stay in the keyboard order, inert (gui review 5587650119 of PR #255, G12; REQ-GUI-240).
- **Corrected** on physics review 5587614130 of PR #255, item 5: the title and the question said "only five named
  dimensions are orthogonal", the count with one target; with two targets it is four.
- **Waits:** REQ-GUI-265 (`rq: RQ-404`), the tilt of such a chart on the mock, closed by TASK-M6-51, a leaf task.
- **Ruling:** R-514 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "RQ-404 (a): named dimensions projected off the saved plane
  and normalised, vanishing ones dropped; maps act in the orthonormal frame; the stored pair is kept so the view
  reopens bitwise." Option (a), the stored pair carried by each map in the frame `(q̂₁, q̂₂⊥, t̂)` (R-514 I1 to I5).
  REQ-GUI-265 loses its `rq`; TASK-M6-51 no longer waits, and TASK-M6-49's guard no longer names this entry (I6).

## RQ-369: The occupant table gives no `reversible` value for KDK, Yoshida-4 and Yoshida-6 ("(see §4)"), and the corpus reads both ways; TASK-M3-02 must set it in each profile *(silence and conflict, R-162, R-57, REQ-INT-013, REQ-INT-014, REQ-INT-032, REQ-VAL-032; context: TASK-M3-02)*

*Found by TASK-M3-02's implementer before any code was written. Nothing is chosen. For the human: physics, since the
flag decides which occupants the reversibility diagnostic accepts (REQ-INT-032), and the corpus doesn't settle it
(R-369).*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_integrator_contract.md` § "Part 2 — Occupants and the capability profile", the table
    (:84–86): "| KDK leapfrog | 2 | 1× | ✓ | (see §4) | Potato–Low (fast, motion) |", "| Yoshida-4 | 4 | 3× | ✓ |
    (see §4) | Medium–High (default) |", "| Yoshida-6 | 6 | 7× | ✓ | (see §4) | Ultra–Extreme (accuracy) |". The
    doc's §4 is "Part 4 — Determinism, and the substep as the subtle seam", which says nothing of reversibility; the
    reversibility text is Part 6.
  - The same section (:91): "**The reversibility diagnostic needs a `reversible` occupant** (§4) — it cannot be wired
    to whatever happens to be bound. The profile flag is the guard."
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-INT-014's statement gives `reversible` for Euler, RK4 and Aarseth–Zare + RK4 ("not
    reversible") and none for KDK, Yoshida-4 or Yoshida-6; its verify detail: "assert each built occupant's
    advertised profile and default tier binding equals the Part 2 / Part 2a tables".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_integrator_contract.md` § "Part 6 — Three boundaries stated honestly (deferred work
    lands here)" (:383): "**the current stack is symplectic but not cleanly reversible**: Yoshida's `w₂ < 0` middle
    step, state-dependent adaptive substepping (the substep sequence isn't symmetric under reversal), and per-step
    COM projection (a non-symplectic nudge) all break naive "integrate → negate → integrate." … the reversibility
    diagnostic must either (a) record the macro-step + substep schedule and replay it exactly in reverse … or (b) bind
    a fixed-step reversible occupant with COM projection disabled. The `reversible` capability flag (§2) gates which
    occupants qualify."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.2 Occupants", Yoshida-4: "The negative middle step is real and is
    why naive time-reversal fails (integrator contract Part 6)."
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "5. Unit tests", test 3 (REQ-VAL-032): "**Step reversibility
    (occupant-level):** for symplectic occupants, one `STEP(dt)` then momentum-negate then `STEP(dt)` then negate
    returns the state to precision — *without* wrapper (projection/substep excluded)"; REQ-VAL-032's verify detail:
    "residual at rounding level for KDK/Yoshida-4/Yoshida-6".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-INT-032: the diagnostic "must either replay the recorded macro-step + substep
    schedule exactly in reverse or bind a fixed-step reversible occupant with COM projection disabled, and must be
    available only for occupants whose profile has `reversible: true`."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-162 — The reversible occupant is logH's TTL time mode": "The reversible occupant is logH's TTL
    time mode (Mikkola–Tanikawa), as prin-rs built it."
  - `docs/contracts/principia_integrator_contract.md` § "The table gains two rows": time-transformed leapfrog
    "gives regularisation at leapfrog cost while staying symplectic and reversible, and it is what unblocks the
    reversibility diagnostic (§4)".
- **Silence and conflict:**
  - No line gives KDK's, Yoshida-4's or Yoshida-6's `reversible`. `CapabilityProfile` needs a value for each.
  - Read at `STEP` level, all three are symmetric compositions, so they are time-reversible at a fixed step with no
    projection: dd test 3 (REQ-VAL-032) asserts exactly that, and REQ-INT-032's route (a), replaying the recorded
    schedule in reverse, runs one of them and is gated on `reversible: true`. That reads `true`.
  - Read at the bound occupant's level, Part 6 calls the stack "not cleanly reversible", R-162 names logH-TTL as "the
    reversible occupant", and Part 2a's TTL leapfrog row is "what unblocks the reversibility diagnostic". That reads
    `false`.
  - Part 6 and dd §3.2 also blame the negative Yoshida weight for breaking naive reversal; a symmetric composition
    with a negative weight is still time-symmetric at a fixed step, and dd test 3 asserts that for Yoshida-4 and -6.
    R-57 fixes the label (`w₂` → the negative weight, `w₀` in Yoshida-4 and `w₁` in Yoshida-6), not the claim, so
    TASK-M3-02's label fix would keep a sentence its own test contradicts.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) `reversible: true` for KDK, Yoshida-4 and Yoshida-6: the flag means the `STEP` is time-symmetric at a fixed
    step, as dd test 3 checks; the wrapper's substepping and projection are what route (a)'s replay or route (b)'s
    fixed step with projection off handle. The table's "(see §4)" becomes ✓, and Part 6's and dd §3.2's sentence on
    the negative weight is reworded (it doesn't break `STEP` reversibility).
  - (b) `reversible: false` for all three: the flag means the bound occupant, wrapper substepping included, is
    reversible, so only logH-TTL (R-162) and a future TTL leapfrog qualify; dd test 3 stays as the occupant-level
    check of where reversibility lives, and REQ-INT-032's route (a) needs rewording, since it would then accept no
    built occupant.
  - (c) Split the flag's meaning in Part 2 (a `STEP`-level and an occupant-level reversibility), as a definition
    (R-72) TASK-M3-02 writes.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing. TASK-M3-02 has written no code; its branch holds this entry only.
- **Waits:** REQ-INT-013 and REQ-INT-014 (`rq: RQ-369`), and with them TASK-M3-02, which closes both; TASK-M3-04 and
  later M3 tasks depend on TASK-M3-02.
- **Ruling:** R-500 (20 Oct 2026), the human: "RQ-369: (c). Split the flag. step_reversible: KDK, Yoshida-4, Yoshida-6
  and TTL leapfrog true; Euler, RK4, AZ+RK4 false; regularised occupants measured by dd test 3, not asserted.
  run_reversible: logH-TTL only (R-162). Both diagnostic routes gate on step_reversible. Fix Part 6 and dd §3.2: a
  symmetric composition with a negative weight is still reversible at a fixed step. What breaks reversal is adaptive
  substepping and COM projection." Option (c): the profile carries `step_reversible` and `run_reversible`; the table's
  "(see §4)" reads ✓ for `step_reversible` and ✗ for `run_reversible`; REQ-INT-032's routes gate on `step_reversible`;
  Part 6's and dd §3.2's negative-weight sentence is withdrawn. With it, R-501 (the Yoshida citation and the
  convergence-order test). REQ-INT-013 and REQ-INT-014 never carried `rq: RQ-369` on `main`; TASK-M3-02 no longer
  waits.

## RQ-370: The `N_sub` threshold table is frozen at build time from `r_sub = 0.05`, `γ_sub = 1.5` and `N_max = 64`, but `r_sub`, `gamma_sub` and `N_max` are user-adjustable sim-key uniforms, and `N_max` is "raised" past its default *(conflict, R-68, R-86, REQ-INT-028, REQ-TOOL-033, REQ-SCHED-010; context: TASK-M3-03)*

*Found by TASK-M3-03's implementer before any code was written. Nothing is chosen. For the human: physics, since
`r_sub`, `γ_sub` and `N_max` set the substep count, which changes every trajectory's discretisation, and the corpus
reads both ways (R-369).*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.3 Substep law — and the determinism pin, made concrete" (:101):
    "`N_sub(r_min) = clamp( ⌈ (r_sub / r_min)^{γ_sub} ⌉ , 1 , N_max )    [r_sub = 0.05, γ_sub = 1.5, N_max = 64]`";
    (:112): "`THR[n] = round_f32( ( r_sub / n^{2/3} )² )   for n = 1 … N_max     (frozen at build time)`"; and "The
    transcendental (`pow`) still exists — it moves from *runtime, every substep, every backend* (where it forks) to
    *once, at table-generation time, into a constant* (where it cannot)."
  - `docs/contracts/principia_integrator_contract.md` § "Part 4 — Determinism, and the substep as the subtle seam":
    "`d²` compared against 64 build-time-frozen f32 thresholds `THR[n] = round_f32((r_sub/n^{2/3})²)`"; and
    "**`N_max` is a sim-key tunable** — both pipelines must use the same value (as with `r_coll`)".
  - The same contract, § "Part 3 — Parameter ownership (who owns what)" (:325): "**2. Wrapper config — the loop.**
    Constant across a dispatch, bound once per frame/tier as `SimUniforms`. **On the sim key** — change any and it
    re-integrates."; its table (:331): "`N_max` | tunable (default 64) | substep cap per macro-step —
    **user-adjustable** (raising it resolves more close-encounter structure before the integration floor …)"; (:332):
    "`r_sub`, `gamma_sub` | 0.05, 1.5 | produce `N_sub` from `r_min` | wrapper substepper".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_lowering_contract.md` § "Part 3 — The baking rules (compile-time vs uniform)",
    "Compute side" (:55): "Wrapper config (`T, dt, N_max, r_*, eps_*, n_renorm`) | **UNIFORM** (`SimUniforms`) |
    sim-key *values*, not code."
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "Run — from the top bar" (:586–587): "integrator_contract Part 3's
    parameters — … `N_max` (default 64), `r_sub` / `gamma_sub`, `r_coll` …", each "a `SimConfig` field"; only
    `dt_macro` is marked "derived, not editable".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_image_embedding.md` § "6. What travels" (:222): "sim  T_horizon, dt_macro, N_max, r_sub,
    gamma_sub, r_coll, r_close, …".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_generation_root.md` § "3.1 `sample_descriptor` (u32)" (:39), `saturated`: "`N_max` is a
    raised tunable (integrator contract), not hardcoded".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_simstate_payload.md` § "8. Build-time settles (measure / specify once running)" (:631):
    "Also assert the joint `horizon_steps × N_max ≤ 2³²−1` for the `total_substeps` counter" (a bound that matters only
    for `N_max` far above 64).
- **Conflict:**
  - A table frozen at build time from `r_sub = 0.05` and `γ_sub = 1.5` gives the specified `N_sub` only at those
    values. If a user sets `r_sub` or `gamma_sub` in the Run window, as the GUI spec, the lowering contract and the
    image-embedding sim block allow, the frozen tree no longer equals `clamp(⌈(r_sub/r_min)^{γ_sub}⌉, 1, N_max)`, which
    REQ-INT-028 requires, and nothing in the corpus regenerates it or refuses the setting.
  - The table has 64 entries, `n = 1 … N_max` at `N_max = 64`. A raised `N_max` (Part 3's "raising it", generation
    root's "a raised tunable") has no threshold past 64: `min(bucket(d²), N_max)` then never reaches `N_max`, so the
    sticky `saturated` bit (R-86, "`saturated` ⟺ N_sub == N_max") never sets although the table caps the step at 64,
    and the tree differs from the specification below `THR[64]`. Nothing gives `N_max`'s upper bound, a refusal, or a
    larger table.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) `r_sub`, `gamma_sub` and the table's length are build-time constants (constants-register entries): the Run window
    shows `r_sub` and `gamma_sub` read-only, as it does `dt_macro`, and they leave `SimUniforms`. `N_max` stays a sim-key
    uniform with `1 ≤ N_max ≤ 64` (the table's length), dispatch refusing a larger one, so "raising it" means raising
    it back towards 64. TASK-M3-03 is built as written; TASK-M3-06 adds the refusal.
  - (b) The thresholds are generated per configuration on the host, in f64 libm from the sim key's `r_sub`, `gamma_sub`
    and `N_max`, and shipped as f32 bit patterns in a uniform; the comparison tree reads them by constant index. The
    branch still depends only on comparisons against values both pipelines receive bit-identically, so the determinism
    pin holds, but "frozen at build time" becomes "frozen per dispatch", the table needs a fixed largest length (a
    calibration value, R-71), and TASK-M3-03's generator moves from `cargo xtask codegen` into the host.
  - (c) As (a) for `gamma_sub` and the table's length, but `r_sub` stays a uniform: since `THR[n] = r_sub²·n^{-4/3}`,
    the frozen table holds `n^{-4/3}` and the tree compares `d²` against `r_sub²·K[n]`, one single-rounded product per
    comparison (allowed by Part 4's "single-rounded arithmetic"); `round_f32` of the product then differs from
    dd §3.3's `round_f32((r_sub/n^{2/3})²)` by up to an ulp, so §3.3's formula is reworded.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing. TASK-M3-03 has no code on its branch.
- **Waits:** TASK-M3-03 (REQ-INT-027, REQ-INT-028, REQ-TOOL-033, REQ-TOOL-034), and through it TASK-M3-04 onward;
  TASK-M3-06's `SimUniforms` refusals.
- **Ruling:** R-502 (20 Oct 2026), the human: "RQ-370: (b). Thresholds generated per sim key on the host, shipped as
  exact f32 bits, read by constant index. The pow is computed with a pinned pure-software libm so native and wasm give
  identical bits. A maximum table length is a calibration value, refused above it. A test asserts the default config
  reproduces the build-time table bitwise." Option (b): REQ-INT-028 is restated, and REQ-INT-091 to REQ-INT-094 are
  added (the host generator, the default-config bitwise test, the wasm leg, the maximum length's calibration);
  TASK-M3-03 no longer waits.

## RQ-371: What is the shape sphere's `system_image` under the exponential chart map? *(silence, R-141, R-117, REQ-CHART-014; context: TASK-M2-14, PR #252)*

*Found in review of PR #252 (physics review 5584063700, item 4). Nothing is chosen. For the human: physics, the
choice changes which pixels a fold or an area statistic counts once (R-369).*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_chart_reference.md` § "3.3 The chart map": "Two options; state which is used", spherical
    `(θ, φ)` and "Exponential map about a centre `n0`". Its "Hemisphere redundancy" paragraph: "The chart is a 2-to-1
    cover: (θ, φ) ∼ (θ, π − φ) … The chart's `system_image` is **n-to-1** with n = 2 — 2-to-1 over the φ
    hemispheres". Its "Past the unit square" paragraph treats the exponential map apart: "The exponential map has no θ
    or φ axis, so its axes declare nothing and are bounded".
  - `docs/contracts/principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` § "Part 5 — Well-posedness and the validation contract":
    "**n-to-1** — a fixed finite number of pixels share each system … The shape sphere is n-to-1 with n = 2: 2-to-1
    over the φ hemispheres, which are reflection-equivalent".
  - `decisions.md` § "R-117 — The lowering appendix's shape-sphere row uses (θ, φ) *(closes RQ-85)*": "The lowering
    appendix's shape-sphere row is conformed to R-14's (θ, φ) map."
- **Silence:** on the exponential map `[0,1]²` covers a cap of centre `n0` and radius up to `√2·s`. A cap inside one
  closed hemisphere is bijective (no pixel's mirror `w → −w` is in view); a cap straddling the equator is 2-to-1 only
  on the part whose mirror is also in view; past `r = π` the map wraps. No fixed n holds, and the corpus gives this
  map no value of its own. By contrast §4.5 makes the `ν` charts' value depend on their range (`DoubleCover` only where
  `ν_min < √2 − 1 < ν_max`).
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) `NToOne(2)` for either map, read as "subject to the hemisphere fold `w → −w`", whatever the cap.
  - (b) Computed from the cap, as the `ν` charts' is from their range: bijective where the cap lies in one closed
    hemisphere, and a ruling for the straddling case.
  - (c) Another value.
- **Applied meanwhile:** PR #252 gives both maps `NToOne(2)`, the `(θ, φ)` chart's value. The only consumer today,
  `kernel::chart::admit_shape_fraction`, treats `Bijective` and `NToOne(_)` alike, and no shape-sphere label is shown
  yet.
- **Waits:** the consumers that fold or count by `system_image` on the exponential map: R-408's area statistics and
  "downstream draws/labels one representative". TASK-M2-14's PR does not wait: it claims only the spherical map's
  value, which R-141 settles.
- **Ruling:** R-503 (20 Oct 2026), the human: "RQ-371: (b). Computed from the cap: bijective when the cap is within
  one closed hemisphere; when it straddles, a per-pixel fold (count once unless the mirror is in view; equator counts
  once). The map's radius is bounded at π; past it is the domain's end." Option (b), the straddling case ruled: the
  exponential map's value is REQ-CHART-075's, built by TASK-M2-28. PR #252's `NToOne(2)` for the spherical map stands
  (R-141); its value for the exponential map is what REQ-CHART-075 replaces.

- **Ruling:** R-504 (20 Oct 2026), the human: "RQ-373: (a)." The goldens R-467 changes are re-baselined as TASK-M2-34
  renders them, each `BASELINES.md` row leading with R-504; the canvas background's value and the changed tiles are
  confirmed at the M2 gate with REQ-COL-110's NaN / Inf style (R-505 adds that the style is not magenta). `rq: RQ-373`
  leaves REQ-COL-108, and TASK-M2-34's merge no longer waits.

## RQ-405: Yoshida-4's printed weights are not the f64 values of their closed form, and with them `w₀ + 2w₁` evaluates to `0.9999999999999998` in f64, where REQ-INT-037 asks for "all printed digits" and "w₀ + 2w₁ = 1" *(corpus error, R-501, R-57, REQ-INT-037; context: TASK-M3-02, PR #258)*

*Found in physics review 5587467237 of PR #258 (P2), porting R-501. Nothing is chosen. For the human: changing a printed
value in the corpus (R-369, the porting rule); the physics changes only at the 10⁻¹⁶ level.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "3.2 Occupants", Yoshida-4: "`w₁ = 1 / (2 − 2^{1/3}) = 1.3512071919596578`",
    "`w₀ = −2^{1/3} · w₁     = −1.7024143839193157        (w₀ + 2w₁ = 1)`".
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-INT-037's verify: "coefficient constants equal §3.2 to all printed digits; w₀ + 2w₁ = 1".
  - `decisions.md` § R-501: Yoshida-4's weights are "the same paper's fourth-order closed form".
- **Measured (exact decimal arithmetic, 40 digits; physics review P2 agrees):**
  - The closed form: `w₁ = 1.351207191959657634047687…`, `w₀ = −1.702414383919315268095375…`. Their correctly rounded f64
    values: `1.3512071919596575` and `−1.7024143839193153`. Naive f64 evaluation (`1/(2 − 2^(1/3))`, `−2^(1/3)·w₁`):
    `1.3512071919596578` and `−1.7024143839193155`. The printed `w₀`, `−1.7024143839193157`, is neither.
  - In f64, `w₀ + 2w₁` is `0.9999999999999998` with the printed digits, and also with the correctly rounded ones; it is
    exactly `1.0` with `w₁` correctly rounded and `w₀ = 1 − 2w₁` computed in f64 (`−1.702414383919315`), whose cubic
    residual `|2w₁³ + w₀³| / Σ|w|³` is `5.5·10⁻¹⁷` (printed digits: `2.9·10⁻¹⁶`).
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Keep the printed digits; REQ-INT-037's `w₀ + 2w₁ = 1` is read within f64 rounding (R-501's algebraic check,
    dd §5 test 1a, bounds it at `3·10⁻¹⁵`).
  - (b) Print the closed form's correctly rounded f64 values (`1.3512071919596575`, `−1.7024143839193153`); the identity
    is read within rounding as in (a).
  - (c) Print `w₁` correctly rounded and `w₀ = 1 − 2w₁` as computed in f64 (`−1.702414383919315`), so that `w₀ + 2w₁ = 1`
    holds exactly in f64, as Yoshida-6's `w₀` is already computed from its consistency condition.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a). dd §3.2's sentence on the digits' provenance is corrected (it said they were the closed
  form evaluated in f64); the printed digits are unchanged. REQ-INT-037 carries `rq: RQ-405`; TASK-M3-02 builds the
  printed digits, and its PR does not wait: it merges with REQ-INT-037 held, not closed.
- **Waits:** REQ-INT-037 (`rq: RQ-405`), TASK-M3-02's closing of it.
- **Ruling:** R-515 (20 Oct 2026), the human: "RQ-405 (c): w₁ correctly rounded, w₀ = 1 − 2w₁." Option (c): dd §3.2
  prints `w₁ = 1.3512071919596575` and `w₀ = −1.702414383919315`, the f64 result of `1 − 2w₁`, so that `w₀ + 2w₁ = 1`
  exactly in f64; REQ-INT-037's identity is exact, `rq: RQ-405` leaves it, and TASK-M3-02 builds the new digits and
  closes it.

## RQ-380: Whether a chart preset may optionally pin links ("designed under stick-breaking"), and what loading it does if the current links differ *(the human's queued question, R-478 W4, the link-functions instruction §6; context: TASK-M6-50, TASK-M8-52)*

*Queued by the human (the link-functions instruction, §6: "Queue, don't rule"). For the human: yes, it changes what a
preset carries and what loading one does.*

- **File, section:**
  - `decisions.md` § "R-478 — …", the human's words, W4: "Chart presets do **not** carry links. A preset is a pair of
    axes. Whether a preset may optionally pin a link is queued (§6)." and §6: "Whether a chart preset may optionally pin
    links ("designed under stick-breaking"), and what loading it does if the current links differ."
  - The same, W1: "Switching chart must not silently switch links, because the robustness test Part 2.5 asks for is
    exactly "same chart, different link"."
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G7. Chart builder (`03_chartbuilder.png`)": "Presets are saved pairs of
    axes and are editable".
- **The question:** a preset designed under a non-default decoder (the masses near an edge, where only
  `stick_breaking` reaches) may want to say so; W1 forbids switching links silently.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) No pin: presets carry no links (W4 as written); a preset's note may name the decoder it was designed under, as
    text.
  - (b) An optional pin, recorded with the preset; loading it with other links active offers "Switch to the pinned
    links" beside the load, never switching on its own, and the Decoder window's row shows the pinned choice.
  - (c) An optional pin that switches the links on load, one undo entry with the chart, the amber row and the badge
    showing the change (R-479).
  - Saved charts (R-475) are charts and are covered by the same answer.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a), W4 as written. Nothing waits; a ruling for (b) or (c) is a follow-up change to
  REQ-GUI-254 and REQ-GUI-108.
- **Ruling:** R-516 (20 Oct 2026), in the human's words "RQ-380 yes, optional, applied with a notice, one undo entry." Option (c)'s switch, with a notice: a chart preset or a saved chart may optionally pin the links it was designed under; loading one whose pin differs from the active links applies the pin with the chart, one command and one undo entry, and posts a notice naming each block it changed; an unpinned preset leaves the links alone. REQ-GUI-254 changes (TASK-M6-50); REQ-GUI-108 gains a note.
