## RQ-535: R-570's "cost per frame is set by the screen, not by the view's extent" at any zoom-out, where R-537 EX7(a)'s root lattice has no quad coarser than a level-0 root, so the baseline cover of a far zoomed-out viewport grows with its extent *(conflict: the human's ruling against an earlier ruling's applied text; R-570, R-537 EX4, EX7(a); REQ-SCHED-104, REQ-SCHED-056, REQ-SCHED-101; context: TASK-M8-56)*

*Found by R-570's port. Nothing is ruled.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/incoming/2026-10-21_latent_unbounded/INSTRUCTION_latent_unbounded_2026-10-21.md` § "The behaviour", item 2:
    "The only limits are the existing ones: the frame budget and the baseline cover, which already span the viewport
    whatever its size. Cost per frame is set by the screen, not by the view's extent."; § "Tests",
    zoom_out_cost_bounded: "a frame at 10⁶ × the home view computes no more quads than the baseline cover of the
    viewport. **Control:** at home zoom the same count, so the cost does not grow with the view's extent."
  - `decisions.md` § "R-537 — …", EX7(a): "The level-0 cells of the root lattice tile the whole parameter plane. Every
    cell the view touches has a root, so every visible point has an ancestor and EX4 can hold."; EX4: "The baseline
    cover (caching contract Part 4) is the coarsest cover of the whole viewport, extension included."
  - `docs/contracts/principia_caching_contract.md` § "Part 4 — Baseline-first: an absolute tier, not a priority weight":
    "every cell the view touches has a root of the root lattice (EX7), so every visible point has an ancestor".
  - `docs/design/principia_deep_zoom.md` § "The precision split (the CPU/GPU seam, decode side)": the quad address is
    taken "in the slice plane's own frame, relative to the plane anchor …: in-plane pan and zoom change which addresses
    are requested, never the addresses themselves (R-97)".
- **The conflict:** a level-0 root is fixed in the slice plane's own frame, and an in-plane zoom does not re-address
  it (R-97). If the root is about the home view's size, the coarsest cover of a viewport at 10⁶ × the home view
  is therefore about 10⁶ × 10⁶ level-0 roots, each far below a pixel, on an affine chart each a different set of
  systems; the corpus has no coarser quad. "The baseline cover … already span[s] the viewport" holds, but its count,
  and so the cost per frame, grows with the square of the view's extent, so zoom_out_cost_bounded's control (the same
  count at 10⁶ × and at home zoom) cannot pass on the corpus as written. On periodic charts the union of images
  (EX7(c)) already computes each canonical root once.
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) The root lattice gains coarser levels on an affine axis, `L < 0`, each cell of level `L − 1` the 2 × 2 block
    of level-`L` cells it holds, with no lower bound, so the baseline cover is a screen-sized number of quads at any
    extent; the canonical maps, the keys and the addresses at `L ≥ 0` are unchanged (R-97).
  - (b) A zoom-out past a set extent re-bases the plane's frame, so the depth-0 root again spans about the window: a
    re-addressing event, its cached quads re-keyed or dropped, against R-97's "never the addresses themselves".
  - (c) Past a set extent the baseline cover samples one footprint per screen cell without a quad per root, a cover
    outside the quadtree.
- **For the human:** no as a ruling on navigation, which R-570 settles; it is the scheduler's addressing, which R-537
  marks ⚑ (physics) and the perf reviewer reviews, so the orchestrator and the reviewers may decide it under R-369 with
  physics review. Queued because R-570's item 2 says the existing cover already does this.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing; no option is applied.
- **Waits:** REQ-SCHED-104 (zoom_out_cost_bounded, TASK-M8-56), `rq: RQ-535`. The rest of TASK-M8-56 does not wait,
  and neither do REQ-GUI-357 or REQ-CHART-087: zooming out is never refused while this is open (R-570).
- **Decided (22 Oct 2026, per R-369, by the orchestrator on PR #279; not a human ruling):** (a). Coarser levels
  `L < 0` of the same quadtree: a level-`L` quad with `L < 0` spans `2^(−L) × 2^(−L)` level-0 roots, its address in
  R-97's frame; the baseline cover of a viewport is taken at the level whose quads are the smallest still at least the
  cover's on-screen size, so the number of quads in it depends on the screen, not on the view's extent; coarser quads
  are scheduled, cached and refined like any other, refining down through `L = 0` as the user zooms in. Reason: it
  keeps one addressing scheme and R-97's frame; (b) conflicts with R-97, and (c) would duplicate the scheduler.
  Applied in `principia_scheduler_contract.md` Part 3 ("Infinite zoom-out: levels coarser than the root"),
  `principia_deep_zoom.md` § "The precision split (the CPU/GPU seam, decode side)" and
  `principia_chart_decoder_contract.md` Part 4; REQ-SCHED-104 no longer waits and verifies the coarse-level cover.
  ⚑ Physics review of the addressing is required, as for R-537 EX7; perf reviews the cost (TASK-M8-56).

## RQ-553: In a latent chart's saturated far field the decode collapses in f64 as in f32, so `AT_F32_FLOOR` fires at home zoom and the precision warning tells the user that f32 has run out where the cause is saturation, against R-570 item 5 and W13 *(conflict: the human's ruling and an invariant against the warning's trigger; R-570, R-54, R-90, W13; REQ-CHART-087; context: TASK-M8-57)*

*Found by physics review 5624655553 of PR #279, P3. Nothing is ruled.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/incoming/2026-10-21_latent_unbounded/INSTRUCTION_latent_unbounded_2026-10-21.md` § "The behaviour", item 5:
    "**Precision is not a navigation limit.** At large chart coordinates the f32 deep-zoom floor arrives sooner, because
    the floor depends on where you zoom. That is handled by the existing precision ladder and the collapsed-decode
    guard, not by stopping the pan or zoom."
  - `docs/design/principia_deep_zoom.md` § "4. Sliding depth bound": "(iv) the **decode floor proper**
    (`AT_F32_FLOOR`) — only if the *linearised* decoder's samples collapse (~depth 50+), genuinely terminal, a
    deep-zoom backstop".
  - `docs/gui/principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G2. Explore — the everyday view (`01_main.png`)": "The warning is
    **raised by events** (R-54): `DECODE_SWITCHOVER` firing on visible quads, and `AT_F32_FLOOR`."
  - `docs/contracts/principia_canonical_spec.md` § "9. The load-bearing invariants (the walls — the primary comparison
    checklist)", 13: "**Never blank, never lies, never freezes**".
- **The conflict:** in the saturated far field the f64 decode is flat as well: `σ` rounds to 1 in f64 past about 37 and
  `tanh` to ±1 past about 19, so `J_D`'s central differences (deep_zoom §2 a) are 0, the linearised samples collapse too and `AT_F32_FLOOR` fires, at home
  zoom. Stopping refinement there does no harm, the field being genuinely flat; but the warning it raises says that f32
  has run out, where the cause is saturation, not precision, against item 5 and W13's "never lies".
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) A collapse at which f64 `J_D`'s columns along the view's plane are 0, the quad's f64 sample points being
    distinct, is a saturation collapse: refinement stops as at the floor, and no precision warning is raised (the
    readout may say the field is saturated).
  - (b) The warning is raised, its text naming a collapsed decode rather than f32 running out.
  - (c) The warning stands as R-54 defines it.
- **For the human:** the warning's trigger is design, which R-369 lets the orchestrator and the reviewers decide with
  physics review; queued because R-570's "What to check" says to queue a conflict rather than resolve it.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing; deep_zoom §4 records the conflict and settles nothing, and the ladder and the warning
  stand as written.
- **Waits:** nothing; no requirement tests the warning in the far field. TASK-M8-57 builds the ladder as written,
  and a decision here changes only the warning's trigger.
- **Corrected (physics review 5624721489 of PR #279, P4; applied per R-369):** option (a)'s test, identical f64
  decodes, cannot tell saturation from the genuine floor, where f64 also barely resolves the offsets; the derivative
  does: at the floor `J_D ≠ 0`. And `J_D` is taken by central differences in f64 (deep_zoom §2 a), no `sech²` being
  evaluated. *Was (the conflict):* "`σ` rounds to 1 in f64 past about 37 and `sech²` underflows, so `J_D` has zero
  columns". *Was (option (a)):* "A collapse whose f64 decodes are also identical is a saturation collapse: refinement
  stops as at the floor, and no precision warning is raised (the readout may say the field is saturated)."
- **Decided (23 Oct 2026, per R-369, by the orchestrator on PR #279; not a human ruling):** (a), as corrected. The
  precision warning is not raised for a collapse where f64 `J_D`'s columns along the view's plane are 0 while the
  quad's f64 sample points are distinct (a saturation collapse, a true flat image); it is raised as R-54 defines it
  everywhere else, so the genuine floor still warns. Option (c) is out by W13 and R-570 item 5; between (a) and (b) the
  physics reviewer judged the choice a design one, no computed result changing. Recorded against R-54 and R-570 item 5;
  applied in `principia_deep_zoom.md` § "4. Sliding depth bound", `principia_render_gui_spec.md` § "G2. Explore — the
  everyday view (`01_main.png`)" and REQ-GUI-012 (TASK-M6-21), whose verify gains the far-field and genuine-floor arms.
- **Refined (23 Oct 2026, per R-369, by the orchestrator on PR #279, fix round 3; gui review 5624788928, G6; physics
  to confirm):** the test is per direction, so a view saturated along one in-plane axis only, the bands along the axes
  (`principia_chart_reference.md` §5.4), is covered: a collapse between adjacent samples along in-plane direction `k`
  is a saturation collapse when f64 `J_D`'s column along `k` is 0 while those two f64 sample points are distinct; the
  warning is suppressed only for a quad every collapsed pair of which is a saturation collapse, and is raised as R-54
  defines it otherwise. The Decided bullet above stands as the decision of fix round 2, its "f64 `J_D`'s columns along
  the view's plane are 0 while the quad's f64 sample points are distinct" superseded by this test. REQ-GUI-012 gains
  the band arm and two genuine-floor cases (physics review 5624781454, P5).
- **Refined (23 Oct 2026, per R-369, by the orchestrator on PR #279, fix round 4; gui review 5624846336, G8):** the
  suppression needs at least one collapsed adjacent pair, every one of which is a saturation collapse; a quad that
  switches at `ℓ_switch` with no collapsed pair (R-90) warns as R-54 defines it. The Refined bullet above's "suppressed
  only for a quad every collapsed pair of which is a saturation collapse", vacuously true for a quad with none, is
  superseded. REQ-GUI-012 gains arm (e).

## RQ-523: REQ-CHART-082's run arms leave the M2 gate for M3, since TASK-M2-43 cannot reach a stepper, the wrapper or the outcome classifier *(deferral: a requirement's arms leave a gate, which only a ruling does; R-544, R-369; REQ-CHART-082, REQ-CHART-085; context: TASK-M2-43, TASK-M3-38)*

*Found by TASK-M2-43's pre-flight; raised by code review 5605370280 of PR #276, F1, which the human has been asked.
Nothing is ruled.*

- **File, section:**
  - `plan/requirements.yaml`, REQ-CHART-082's verify as merged by PR #273 (main 9c8cb15e, :3185): "the run from Π·x,
    Π applied to a decoded state, is the run from x mirrored, bitwise; … on a named fixture (the masses, the pairs and a
    horizon stated in the test, which the test shows non-chaotic there by the pair's state separation staying below a
    stated bound over the horizon) the outcome and t_end are equal and L_z and the frame's winding opposite across each
    pixel pair; … controls: R-141's gauge left in (L_z and the winding equal, not opposite), a momentum rule that turns
    p_x below the equator (outcome, t_end or winding broken)"; its milestone `M2`.
  - `plan/tasks/M2/TASK-M2-43.md` § "Acceptance tests" as merged (main :73-:85): "`cargo test -p kernel
    shape_sphere_mirror_test` — REQ-CHART-082's verify: … the run from `Π·x`, `Π` applied to a decoded state, is the
    run from `x` mirrored, bitwise; … on a named fixture (masses, pairs and horizon stated in the test, shown
    non-chaotic there by the pair's state separation staying below a stated bound) outcome and `t_end` are equal and
    `L_z` and the frame's winding opposite across each pixel pair"; its Depends on: TASK-M2-08, TASK-M2-14,
    TASK-M2-17.
  - `decisions.md` § "R-544 — …", item 5: "The mirror becomes a test, not an identification. Where every coordinate
    the chart holds fixed is mirror-equivariant (released from rest is), mirror-invariant fields are exactly symmetric
    across the equator and signed fields exactly antisymmetric. Where one is not (a fixed `L_z ≠ 0`, say), the two
    hemispheres are different systems and the test does not apply; the test says which case it is in."
  - `plan/WORKFLOW.md` § "No deferral": "A requirement leaves a gate only by a ruling".
- **The problem:** the run arms need real integrator runs: a stepper (TASK-M3-02), the wrapper (TASK-M3-04) and the
  outcome classifier (TASK-M3-12), none of them on main and none reachable from TASK-M2-43's dependencies. As written,
  TASK-M2-43 cannot pass REQ-CHART-082 at M2. Moving the arms to M3 takes them out of the M2 gate, a deferral that
  R-369 does not cover (precedent: R-113, R-125 and R-273 were the human's rulings).
- **Options seen:**
  - (a) Move the run arms to M3: REQ-CHART-085, closed by TASK-M3-38 (after TASK-M3-04, TASK-M3-12 and TASK-M2-43),
    with REQ-CHART-086 defining the frame's winding (R-72); REQ-CHART-082 keeps at M2 the classification, the "does not
    apply" case, the shape and state pair checks and the bitwise `Π·x` check. PR #276 drafts this.
  - (b) Move the whole of TASK-M2-43, and REQ-CHART-081, REQ-CHART-082 and REQ-ENC-034 with it, to M3, after
    TASK-M3-04 and TASK-M3-12; TASK-M2-28, which depends on TASK-M2-43, would move or wait with it.
  - (c) Keep the arms at M2: TASK-M2-43 builds a minimal stepper of its own (a fixed-step mirror-exact integrator and
    a collision and horizon outcome, as `checks/r544_check.py`'s `run()` models), used only by the test.
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a), as a draft that waits on this ruling: REQ-CHART-082 and REQ-CHART-085 each carry
  `rq: RQ-523`, so neither gate passes silently either way. REQ-CHART-086 (the winding's definition) does not wait.
- **Waits:** REQ-CHART-082 (TASK-M2-43) and REQ-CHART-085 (TASK-M3-38).
- **Ruling:** R-579 (23 Oct 2026), in the human's words "RQ-523 (TASK-M2-43 scope): (a). Move REQ-CHART-082's run checks to M3 as REQ-CHART-085, closed by TASK-M3-38 after TASK-M3-04 and TASK-M3-12. REQ-CHART-082 keeps at M2: the classification, the "does not apply" case, the shape and state pair checks, and the bitwise Π·x check." Option (a); not (c), "a toy stepper built only for the test proves the toy is mirror-exact, not the integrator that ships"; not (b), "it would hold up TASK-M2-28 (#271) for no gain". REQ-CHART-082 and REQ-CHART-085 no longer carry `rq: RQ-523`.

## RQ-537: dd_integrator §5 test 8 asserts "`retrograde` matches the L_z sign", where the mirror leaves `θ̃`, and so `retrograde`, unchanged while it negates `L_z` *(conflict: the design doc's test against the mirror symmetry R-544 item 5 tests; R-544, R-113, R-389; REQ-INT-082, REQ-CHART-086; context: TASK-M3-11, TASK-M3-38)*

*Found by code review 5605370280 of PR #276 (a pointer for physics), read against REQ-CHART-086. Nothing is ruled.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_integrator.md` § "5. Unit tests", test 8 (:349): "on a circulating bounded orbit, `θ̃` is
    continuous (no 2π jumps), `orbit_count` matches a hand-counted winding, `retrograde` matches the L_z sign".
    REQ-INT-082's verify (TASK-M3-11's `theta_unwrap_real_orbit`): "retrograde matches the L_z sign".
  - The same doc § "3.7 The shape readout and winding (live, per macro-step — lockstep, ratified)" (:272): "`θ̃`: the
    equatorial longitude `atan2(n_v-axis, n_u-axis)` accumulated continuously"; `retrograde = sign(θ̃) < 0`.
  - `decisions.md` § "R-14 — One shape-sphere convention, the IC Inspector's *(closes RQ-12, corrects R-12's
    premise)*": `u = ‖ρ̃‖² − ‖λ̃‖²`, `v = 2 ρ̃·λ̃`, `w = 2(ρ̃ ∧ λ̃)`.
  - `decisions.md` § "R-544 — …", item 5, and the full-state mirror (inverse_encode_contract Part 1): "Under the
    correct full-state mirror, `L_z → −L_z`".
- **The conflict:** the mirror `Π` (`y → −y` on every position and momentum) leaves `u` and `v` unchanged and negates
  `w`, so the run from `Π·x` has the same `θ̃(t)` as the run from `x`, and the same `retrograde`, while its `L_z` is
  opposite. Of a mirror pair of circulating bounded orbits, at most one can have `retrograde` matching the `L_z` sign,
  so test 8 holds for a chosen orbit, never as a property. R-544 makes both twins chart pixels.
- **Options seen:** none is chosen here. Possibly: test 8's `L_z` clause dropped or restated as a fixture-specific
  check; `retrograde` read from a winding the mirror negates (such as REQ-CHART-086's frame winding); or a stated
  hemisphere convention for the comparison.
- **Applied meanwhile:** nothing; REQ-INT-082 carries `rq: RQ-537`. REQ-CHART-086's "θ̃ … which the mirror leaves
  unchanged" stands as the reason the frame's winding is not `θ̃`.
- **Waits:** REQ-INT-082's `L_z` clause (TASK-M3-11).
- **Ruling:** R-580 (23 Oct 2026), in the human's words "drop that clause. … Test 8 keeps: θ̃ continuous, with no 2π jumps, and orbit_count matching a hand count. Add in its place: the run from Π·x has bitwise-identical θ̃(t) and retrograde, and opposite L_z, to the run from x. REQ-INT-082's verify changes to match." Applied in dd_integrator §5 test 8 and REQ-INT-082, which no longer carries `rq: RQ-537`.

## RQ-565: Lookup's layer-1 verdict at the `α = 0` pole, where `β` is undefined: the clamp's move as its worst case over `β`, or the clamp's image of `β` defined along `p_λ` *(physics; found by physics review 5638054366 of PR #284, P1; REQ-ENC-036, REQ-ENC-038; context: TASK-M2-19)*

*Found by the physics review of PR #284, P1. For the human: yes, the choice changes which entered states lookup
accepts, and the docs don't settle it.*

- **File, section:**
  - `docs/contracts/principia_inverse_encode_contract.md` § "Chart-aware validation", "The class a clamp moves": the
    bound for `β` is "`R/b + R/a + |β|` for `β`", with no rule where `b = 0`, and "`β`: … `ΔL_z = −2b·sin(Δβ/2)·(p_λ·ê)`"
    assumes a defined `β`.
  - `plan/requirements.yaml` REQ-ENC-036: "so that one physical state gets one verdict under every gauge".
  - `docs/design/principia_dd_decoder.md` §3.2: "`α→0` puts body 2 at the inner-pair CoM (`‖λ̃‖→0`, azimuth `β`
    undefined *at the point*)", and R-437's defined `β = 0` at `λ̃ = 0`.
- **The question:** at `α = 0` (body 2 at the inner pair's centre of mass) `α` clamps and moves body 2 to
  `b' = sin(πε_z/2)/√μ_λ` in the direction `β`, which the state does not define, creating `|ΔL_z|` up to `b'·‖p_λ‖`.
  Physics found one `L_z = 0` state accepted in some gauges and rejected in others (37 / 27 over 64 images for the
  equal-mass Euler line with a perpendicular kick): where `λ̃` lands on 0 exactly, `β` is R-437's 0 and the move is
  rejected; where it lands on rounding, `R/b` inflates the bound and the move is accepted.
- **Options seen** (physics's):
  (a) read the move as its worst case over `β`, `|ΔL_z| ≤ b'·‖p_λ‖` (and likewise `ΔU`'s `β`-dependent part), so the
  clamp is rejected in every gauge unless `p_λ` is 0 within its own bound. It matches the canonical gauge's verdict,
  keeps encode's `β` convention (R-437, R-438) untouched, and never accepts a state whose `L_z` class the clamp really
  changed.
  (b) define the clamp's image of `β` at the pole gauge-invariantly, e.g. along `p_λ`, so the move is 0 and the clamp
  is accepted in every gauge.
  Physics recommends (a).
- **Applied meanwhile:** (a), as the contract's interim pole rule ("At the `α = 0` pole"), applied per R-369 in fix
  round 5 of PR #284, with `lookup_validation_ladder_pole` giving each of the three states one verdict over 64 images.
  Nothing waits.
- **Ruling:** R-591 (24 Oct 2026), in the human's words "RQ-565 (verdict at the α = 0 pole): (a). Read the clamp's move as its worst case over β: |ΔL_z| ≤ b'·‖p_λ‖, and likewise ΔU's β-dependent part. The clamp is rejected in every gauge unless p_λ is zero within its own bound." Option (a); not (b), which "picks the one direction, along p_λ, that makes r × p vanish. It hides the L_z change by choosing the most favourable answer for a quantity the state does not have." "R-437 and R-438 are untouched. The interim stands as the ruling." Applied in inverse_encode_contract § "Chart-aware validation", "At the `α = 0` pole", and REQ-ENC-038.
